# @statewalker/vcs-commands

## 0.6.0

### Minor Changes

- cd5cd60: **`git add -i` as a library API.** `add().setInteractive(true)` runs add-interactive.c's session: the status listing (staged/unstaged columns, `+N/-M`, `unchanged`, `nothing`, `binary`), the "*** Commands ***" menu with git's `[x]` unique-prefix brackets, and its actions status, update, revert (an unborn HEAD included, "note: <path> is untracked now."), add untracked, patch (the `add -p` engine over the chosen files) and diff (`git diff -p --cached` with the diff configuration), help and quit. Every read git makes from standard input goes to the `setPatchSelector` callback: menus and file selections as the new `choose` request (numbers, ranges `1-3`/`3-`, prefixes, `*`, `-` to deselect, `?`, an empty line or the end of input, with git's quirks), hunk prompts as before. The result's `interactive` carries git's standard output and standard error; `setCompatibility("2.43")` drops the 2.5x `-U`/`--inter-hunk-context`/`--no-auto-advance` options. `DiffCommand.callDiffstat()` gives a comparison's diffstat files (unmerged paths marked). Update and add untracked stage each chosen path as git's `add_file_to_index`/`remove_file_from_index` under the index lock (no pathspec or sparse-checkout checks; a path that cannot be staged stops the action with git's errors and leaves the index unwritten), an index lock another process holds is reported as git's lockfile error, revert prints "reverted" only when no unmerged entry remains, and lists are numbered in strcmp order.
- a8277da: **`git add` checks its pathspec, stages gitlinks and removals as git does; faster index writes.**
  
  - `git add` reads its patterns as git pathspecs and checks them as git's `cmd_add` does. A pattern that names nothing fails with "fatal: pathspec 'x' did not match any files" before anything changes (this used to pass in silence). Ignored paths named without `setForce` are reported with git's "The following paths are ignored by one of your .gitignore files" and its hint: the other paths are added and the index written, then `call` rejects with an `AddFailedError` that carries the result. `add -u` with a pattern that matches nothing tracked fails with git 2.5x's "error: pathspec 'x' did not match any file(s) known to git". New options: `setDryRun` (`-n`, which lists git's `add '<path>'` / `remove '<path>'` lines in `result.output`), `setIgnoreMissing` (`--ignore-missing`, only with a dry run), `setIgnoreErrors` (`--ignore-errors`, `add.ignoreErrors`) and `setWarnEmbeddedRepo`. `AddResult` gains `ignored` and `output`.
  - A tracked file that became a directory is removed by `add -u`, `commit -a`, and by `add .` / `-A`, which add the directory's files as new. This also resolves a conflicted path replaced by a directory, keeping its stages as resolve-undo. A directory holding a repository with a commit takes the file's place as a gitlink instead.
  - A repository inside the worktree is staged as a gitlink to its HEAD, with git's "warning: adding embedded git repository: x" and its advice (`advice.addEmbeddedRepo`). One without a commit fails with "'x/' does not have a commit checked out" and "fatal: adding files failed", and the index is not written. A submodule's gitlink follows the commit checked out there.
  - `add -N` writes the empty blob, so `git fsck` and `git write-tree` find it. Its entries carry no stat data, as git's do. On a changed tracked file it keeps the entry and invalidates the file's cache-tree path, as git does.
  - `add --renormalize` invalidates the cache-tree path of every file it adds again, changed or not, so the `TREE` extension matches git's.
  - `commit -a` brings the tracked files up to date without a pathspec, as git's `add_files_to_cache` does.
  - `stash push` records ORIG_HEAD, as the `reset --hard` git runs does.
  - The index encoder (versions 2 and 3) writes every entry into one buffer. Sizes are summed first. ASCII paths and object ids are written in place, and each path and id is checked in the same pass. An entry encoded before is copied from where its bytes were last written. The first write of a 50,000-entry index goes from about 145 ms to about 40 ms, and `commit -a` on such a repository from about 870 ms to about 660 ms.
- 09aa062: **Attributes and end-of-line conversion as git does them, and git's configuration chain.**
  
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `.gitattributes` are read as git's `attr.c` reads them (per-directory files, `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes`, `core.attributesFile` or `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/attributes`, the system file; macros, the built-in `binary`, set / unset / `!attr` / values, no negative patterns), through `AttributesResolver`. `GitContentFilter` is git's `convert.c`: `text`, `text=auto`, `eol`, `crlf`, `-text`, `core.autocrlf`, `core.eol`, `core.safecrlf` (git's warnings and errors), the binary heuristics, the CRLF-in-index rule, renormalisation, `ident`, `working-tree-encoding` (UTF-16 / UTF-32 with git's BOM rules; other encodings fail as git's failed conversion does) and filter drivers registered by the host (`FilterDriver`; a missing required driver fails with git's message). A `Worktree` now has an optional content filter (`contentFilter`, `setContentFilter`): `computeHash` hashes a file as it is cleaned and `writeContent` smudges it (`raw: true` writes as is); `readContent` still gives the file as it is. `MemoryWorkingCopy` installs git's filter on its worktree (options `contentFilter`, `filterDrivers`, `environment`); `storeWorktreeFile` cleans (with `onWarning`, `renormalize`); `readCleanContent` reads a file as the repository would store it (`readConvertedContent` only when a conversion applies). `GitHostEnvironment` describes the user's global files for hosts that opt in.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig` reads git's chain: the system and global files (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/config`, `~/.gitconfig`) from an optional host `environment`, `include.path` and `includeIf` (`gitdir:`, `gitdir/i:`, `onbranch:`), and `config.worktree` with `extensions.worktreeConfig`; writes stay in the repository's file (`getLocal` reads only it). `FileWorktree` supports the content filter, `readGitDirFile` and a global excludes file read through the chain. `GitWorkingCopy` and the factory install git's filter.
  - **vcs-commands:** `add`, `commit -a`, `status`, `stash`, `checkout`, `reset --hard`, `clone` and `stash apply` clean and smudge content as git does. `AddCommand.setRenormalize` is `git add --renormalize`, `AddResult.warnings` holds git's line-ending warnings, and a tracked file whose stat still matches is no longer read again (as git's `add_to_index`).
  - **vcs-transport-lfs:** `createLfsFilterDriver` is git-lfs's clean / smudge as a filter driver (`filterDrivers: { lfs }`).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` gives the user's environment (`HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`) for hosts on Node.
  - **Which attributes a checkout reads:** a checkout of a tree (`checkout <branch>`, `reset --hard`, `stash`/`stash apply`, `clone`, `Worktree.checkoutTree`) smudges every file with the attributes of the index it leaves behind, as git's unpack-trees reads `o->result`: `.gitattributes` itself and files sorting before it get the target's rules. Writers pass it as `WorktreeWriteOptions.attributes` (`ContentAttributesContext`, built with `attributesIndexOf` / `attributesIndexOfTree`); a plain `writeContent` and `checkout -- <path>` read the worktree's `.gitattributes` first, as git does. `checkout -- <path>` now writes the file (and records the index). The filter no longer remembers what was written (`noteWritten` / `noteRemoved` are replaced by `noteChanged`, which only drops cached files).
  - **Speed:** the content filter reads its configuration and attributes files once per worktree batch (`WorktreeContentFilter.batch`, joined by `FileWorktree.batch` / `MemoryWorktree.batch`; `add` runs as one batch), keeps parsed files by blob id or stat, and skips a path at once when no attribute and no setting converts it.
  - **Fixes:** status rename detection reads a file cleaned once (its stat size when nothing converts it); `add --renormalize` fails on a deleted tracked file with git's message and renormalises without write flags (no `core.safecrlf`, a bad `working-tree-encoding` keeps the content); re-opening a working copy replaces git's filter installed by an earlier one. Linked worktrees read the repository's config plus their `config.worktree`; the factory passes the real git directory for `includeIf.gitdir:` (`rootPath`, default the FilesApi's `rootDirectory`); `GitWorkingCopyConfig.refresh()` re-evaluates `includeIf.onbranch:` after HEAD changes (the filter calls it per batch). `GitHostEnvironment.globalConfig` is `$GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`; `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` (option `env`) and defaults the system files to `/etc/gitconfig` and `/etc/gitattributes`.
- a86d47a: **`git backfill`, `git sparse-checkout clean`, pulls into an unborn branch and gc prune in
  partial clones.**
  
  ```typescript
  // git backfill --min-batch-size=1000: a partial clone's missing blobs, batched by path
  const { batches } = await git.backfill().setMinBatchSize(1000).call();
  // git sparse-checkout clean -f: the directories outside the cone, removed whole
  await git.sparseCheckout().setArguments(["clean", "-f"]).call();
  ```
  
  `BackfillCommand` (git 2.49+) walks the commits from HEAD or the revisions given, then their
  trees path by path in git's path-walk order, and fetches the absent blobs through the promisor
  remotes in batches of `--min-batch-size` ids (default 50000), with `--sparse`/`--no-sparse` and
  `--[no-]include-edges`; under 2.43 compatibility it fails as 2.43, which has no such command.
  Revisions are read as git reads them: annotated tags are peeled (`backfill v1`, `v1..main`,
  `main ^v1`), `HEAD:dir` walks that tree from `dir/`, `HEAD:file` and tagged blobs are fetched,
  and a revision git refuses fails with git's message. `setAll(true)` (`--all`), `--not`,
  `addPath()` (`-- <path>`, history simplified to the commits changing the paths) and
  `setArguments()` (the command line) are supported; other rev-list options and pathspec magic
  are refused with exit status 128 instead of being ignored.
  
  `sparse-checkout clean` (git 2.5x; unknown to 2.43) removes every directory the index collapses
  to a sparse directory (never one at or below a directory holding an intent-to-add entry, as
  git's invalid cache-tree nodes keep them; `sparse-checkout reapply` follows the same rule), with
  git's refusals (`must be in a sparse-checkout`, cone mode,
  `clean.requireForce`), `--dry-run`, `--force`, `--verbose` and its `Removing`/`Would remove`
  lines.
  
  `pull --rebase` on an unborn branch with an index refuses before fetching, as git does
  (`Updating an unborn branch with changes added to the index.`); other pulls into an unborn
  branch run git's `pull_into_void` (`MergeCommand.callIntoVoid`), keeping staged changes the
  fetched commit does not overwrite.
  
  `GcOrchestrator` with `FileGcStrategy` now prunes partial clones: promisor objects are kept
  without being walked (`GcRoots.keep`), the local objects promisor objects link to are roots,
  and unreachable loose objects go as `git gc --prune=now` drops them.
- 8681320: **`git bisect` as git 2.5x runs it.** New commands on the `Git` facade: `bisect()` (git's arguments as given), `bisectStart()`, `bisectState(term)`, `bisectSkip()`, `bisectNext()`, `bisectReset()`, `bisectTerms()`, `bisectLog()`, `bisectReplay()` (over a log's text) and `bisectRun()` (the command run through a host hook: 0 good, 125 skip, 1-127 bad, 128 or more aborts; 126/127 verified on a good commit). They port `builtin/bisect.c` and `bisect.c`: `find_bisection` (weights, the halfway shortcut, `--first-parent`), skipped commits with git's pseudo-random `skip_away`, the merge-base checks, path-limited bisection, custom terms, `--no-checkout` (BISECT_HEAD), `--reset-when-found`, the state files and `refs/bisect/*` byte for byte, the candidate checked out with git's reflog line, stdout ("Bisecting: N revisions left to test after this (roughly M steps)", the first bad commit with `git show --stat --summary`), git's errors and exit codes. Each result carries `stdout`, `stderr`, `exitCode`, the candidate and the first bad commit. The checkouts print what `git checkout` prints, shallow clones bisect as git bisects them, a path-limited bisection compares trees pruned (subtrees that are equal or outside the pathspec are not read), and stdout keeps the raw bytes of subjects (`stdoutBytes`).
  
  **`git log --summary`.** `LogCommand.callBytes`/`callText` take `summary` (`create mode`, `delete mode`, `rename ... (n%)`, `copy`, `mode change` lines, as diff.c's `diff_summary`), and `DiffCommand` prints the `summary` format; a shallow commit is shown without parents.
- 8681320: **`diff.renames=copies` finds copies.** `git diff` and `git log` (`--stat`, `--name-status`, `--summary`, `-p`) detect copies as git's `diffcore_rename` does under DIFF_DETECT_COPY: modified paths are sources too, a source can be taken by several destinations (the last use of a deleted source is the rename, the others copies), no basename matches. `detectRenames` takes `copies` and sources marked `kept`, and its pairs say `copy`.
  
  **`git diff` of a conflict prints the combined diff first.** With several formats including the patch, the `diff --cc` of a conflicted path comes before the stat and summary of the other paths, as `run_diff_files` prints it.
  
  **`git bisect` checkouts.** The tracking report follows `status.compareBranches` (git 2.56: each of `@{upstream}` and `@{push}` compared once, a blank line between reports, `git pull <remote> <branch>` advice for a push ref behind), and `GIT_PRINT_SHA1_ELLIPSIS=yes` puts `...` after the ids of "HEAD is now at" and "Previous HEAD position was". `GitHostEnvironment` gains `printSha1Ellipsis`, which `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads from `GIT_PRINT_SHA1_ELLIPSIS`.
- f1cab9d: Pathspecs for `git checkout [<tree-ish>] -- <pathspec>`, `git restore` and `git reset [<tree-ish>] -- <pathspec>`.
  
  `CheckoutCommand`'s path mode now runs git's `checkout_paths`: wildcards, magic (`:(glob)`, `:(icase)`, `:(literal)`, `:(top)`, `:/`), `:(attr:...)`, `:(exclude)`, directories and `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS`; "pathspec 'x' did not match any file(s) known to git" (status `CONFLICTS`, nothing changed); `setOverlay(false)` (`--no-overlay`), `setStage` (`--ours`/`--theirs`, which now leaves the index unmerged as git does), `setMerge`/`setConflictStyle` (`-m`, `--conflict`, re-creating conflicts from resolve-undo too), `setForced` (unmerged paths passed over with a warning), `setIgnoreSkipWorktreeBits` (skip-worktree entries are passed over otherwise), `setQuiet`, `setCountCheckoutPaths` ("Updated N paths from ...") and `setPathspecFromFile`. A new `RestoreCommand` (`git.restore()`) covers `--source`, `--staged`, `--worktree`, `--overlay`, `--ours`/`--theirs`/`--merge`/`--conflict`, `--ignore-unmerged` and `--ignore-skip-worktree-bits`. `ResetCommand` with paths follows `read_from_tree`: pathspecs, unmerged paths (recorded for resolve-undo), skip-worktree bits, `setIntentToAdd` (`-N`, also without paths), `setPathspecFromFile`, an unborn HEAD, and git's refusals ("Cannot do soft reset with paths.").
  
  `InitCommand.setDirectory` now roots the worktree at that directory (it rooted it at the FilesApi's root), and a clone into a fresh repository skips the repository's own git directory when checking that the destination is empty, so `setGitDir("meta").setWorktree(true)` accepts a clone. The staging areas gain `getResolveUndo` and `dropResolveUndo`, and `FileWorktree` exposes `getGitDir`.
- 0135cdd: **Command-line config (`git -c`) on every config read; discovery's ceiling and file system boundary.**
  
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig` reads `GitHostEnvironment.commandLineConfig` last, after `config.worktree`, in scope `command` (git's `do_git_config_sequence`): the working copy's config, submodules' and repositories opened for serving all see it, so `-c` overrides the repository's own settings for every command. Its `include.path` / `includeIf.*.path` are followed (scope `command`, `file:<path>`); a relative include is git's fatal `relative config includes must come from files`; a `gitdir:./` condition prints git's `error: relative config include conditionals must come from files` (`loadErrors`, `GitConfigOptions.onError`, once per read) and holds in 2.5x but not under `GitConfigOptions.compatibility: "2.43"`; a directory named by a command-line include is git's `warning: unable to access '<dir>': Is a directory` plus the fatal line, and an include loop throws `ConfigIncludeDepthError` (git's `fatal: exceeded maximum include depth` text, exit 128). `includeIf.hasconfig:remote.*.url:` now holds in a repository's chain too, matched against the URLs of the whole sequence (system, global, the repository's file, `config.worktree`, the command line with what its includes bring in), as git's `populate_remote_urls`; the protected config sees command-line includes' URLs. `commandLineConfigError` (and that include error) make `load()` throw `CommandLineConfigError` (exit 128) once the files are read, as git dies at its first config read. `GitConfigOptions.commandLine: false` leaves it out. `listWithOrigin()` gives command-line values without a file; `showOrigin(entry)` prints `--show-origin`'s `file:<file>` / `command line:`. `readProtectedConfig` takes the command line from the chain, so its includes count for `safe.directory` too.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `discoverRepository` honours `$GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` (setup.c's `canonicalize_ceiling_entry` and `longest_ancestor_length`: relative entries ignored, `realPath` until an empty entry) and stops at a file system boundary where the FilesApi's `lstat` reports another `dev` unless `$GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM` (`FilesystemBoundaryError`, git's two lines, exit 128).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `GitHostEnvironment.ceilingDirectories` / `discoveryAcrossFilesystem`; `withoutCommandLineConfig(environment)` (git's `local_repo_env`); `OpenRepositoryOptions.commandLineConfig` (default true, a server's own `-c`; false for the remote side of a local transport).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` fills `ceilingDirectories` and `discoveryAcrossFilesystem` from `$GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` and `$GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM`.
  - **vcs-commands:** local fetch, push, ls-remote and clone open the remote with `commandLineConfig: false`: its upload-pack / receive-pack never see the client's `-c`, as connect.c clears `local_repo_env`; clone's ownership check of the source's objects (in the cloning process) still does.
  - **vcs-store-files:** with an `environment`, `createGitFilesBackend` reads its own settings through git's whole sequence, the command line last: `core.logAllRefUpdates` / `core.bare` / the lock timeouts (ref store), `core.multiPackIndex` / `core.deltaBaseCacheLimit` (pack set), the alternates' config; so do the linked worktrees' reflog settings and `multiPackIndexWrite`/`Verify`'s `midx.version` and bitmap settings (`MultiPackIndexOptions.environment`, passed on by `repack`). The repository format stays the file alone. `discoverRepository` climbs the physical directory (`realPath` of `cwd`, mapped back into the FilesApi), so a symlinked `cwd` no longer bypasses `$GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`.
  - **vcs-transport:** `createServedFetchHandler` answers 500 when the resolver throws `CommandLineConfigError` or `ConfigIncludeDepthError`, as http-backend dies.
- 1777896: **Commit-graphs: read, written and verified as git does, and used by history walks.**
  
  On a 5000-commit repository with a graph, a walk of every commit takes 3.5 ms instead of 49 ms, a merge base of distant commits 3 to 5 ms instead of 41 to 48 ms, and an ancestor check about 2 ms instead of 40 ms, level with `git rev-list` and `git merge-base` over the same graph.
  
  - **vcs-core (`history/commit-graph`):** `readCommitGraph` loads `objects/info/commit-graph` or the split chain in `info/commit-graphs/` as git's `commit-graph.c` does (header, chunk table, OIDF, OIDL, CDAT, EDGE, GDA2/GDO2, BIDX/BDAT, BASE; git's checks and messages on load; a broken chain kept up to its last good layer). `CommitGraph` gives each commit's parents, tree, date, topological level and generation (corrected commit date with GDA2) without reading the commit; `bloomMayContainPath` queries its changed-path filters. `writeCommitGraphFile` writes files byte-identical to `git commit-graph write` (`--append`, `--split[=no-merge|replace]`, `--size-multiple`, `--max-commits`, `--expire-time`, `--[no-]changed-paths` with murmur3 hash versions 1 and 2, `--max-new-filters`; GDA2 unless `commitGraph.generationVersion=1`; the filters of an existing graph kept). `verifyCommitGraph` is `git commit-graph verify`. `loadRepositoryCommitGraph` and `commitGraphCompatible` follow git's `prepare_commit_graph`: no graph with `core.commitGraph=false`, replace refs, grafts or a shallow repository.
  - **vcs-core (commits):** `createCommits(objects, { commitGraph })`; `Commits.getHeader` (tree, parents, commit time, generation) and `getParents`, `getTree` and `walkAncestry` read the graph for the commits it has and parse the others (a stale graph is fine). `findMergeBase` is now git 2.56's merge-base (`paint_down_to_common` over a queue ordered by generation then date, with its side-exhaustion exit in the graph; redundant bases removed), returning every merge base, best first, as `git merge-base --all` (it returned the first common ancestor of a depth-first walk). `isAncestor` and the redundancy check skip commits whose generation shows they cannot reach the commit sought. `getMergeBases` and `isAncestorOf` (`commit-reach.ts`) are exported. `History.commitGraph` is the provider; `listObjects` takes a `commitHeader` lookup.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the files backend reads the repository's graph on first use. `writeCommitGraph` is `git commit-graph write --reachable` (or from given commits) in a repository; `readCommitGraphSettings` reads the config. `gc` writes the graph at the end when `gc.writeCommitGraph` is true (the default), as `git gc`, and its reachability walk reads commits from the graph.
  - **vcs-commands:** `fsck` verifies the commit-graph as git's fsck runs `git commit-graph verify` (exit bit 16, `FSCK_ERROR_COMMIT_GRAPH`). merge, rebase, pull, octopus and merge-ort's virtual bases use the store's walks and the graph's generations (the merge-base queue ordered by generation when the graph has corrected dates, as git orders it).
  - **vcs-transport-adapters:** upload-pack and receive-pack walks read commits from the graph.
- e306a5e: **Commit, tag and stash identities as native git resolves them.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** one resolver for every commit and tag the porcelain makes: `resolveAuthorIdent(environment, config)` and `resolveCommitterIdent(environment, config)` follow git's `ident.c`. The name and email come from `GIT_AUTHOR_*` / `GIT_COMMITTER_*` (the host environment), then `author.name` / `author.email` or `committer.name` / `committer.email`, then `user.name` / `user.email`, then `$EMAIL` for the email, with git's crud trimmed. The date is `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE` / `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`, else the clock in the local timezone offset. With `user.useConfigOnly`, a commit or annotated tag that the configuration names nobody for is refused with git's message (`IdentityUnknownError`). When nothing names anyone, the command's other, explicitly given identity is used, else `webrun-vcs <vcs@localhost>` (the ref stores' reflog identity). No commit is made by `Unknown` any more. `parseGitDate` reads every common form git's `parse_date` reads: raw `<seconds> <+-HHMM>`, `@<seconds>`, seconds alone (in the local offset of that time), RFC 2822, ISO 8601 (with an offset, `Z`, a zone name, or none, which means local time), git's own format, `YYYY.MM.DD`, `MM/DD/YYYY`. ISO and RFC dates keep their offset. An unreadable date is refused with `fatal: invalid date format: <date>` (`InvalidDateError`).
    - The per-command semantics follow git. **commit:** the author comes from `setAuthor` / `setAuthorIdent` or the resolver. `--amend`, and the commit that concludes a cherry-pick, keep the earlier author unless you call the new `setResetAuthor(true)`. `setAuthor(name, email)` replaces only the name and email (its date is the kept author's, else `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE` or now). The committer always comes from the resolver; before, it was the author. **merge (and the merge commit of `pull`):** the author and the committer each come from the resolver. **cherry-pick:** the picked commit's author is kept. **revert:** the resolver gives the author. **rebase picks:** the original author is kept, and the resolver gives the committer. `RebaseCommand.setCommitterDateIsAuthorDate` and `setIgnoreDate` give git's `--committer-date-is-author-date` and `--ignore-date`; both are kept in `rebase-merge/` across a stop and imply a forced rebase. **stash:** the author and the committer each come from the resolver, unless `setAuthor` is given. **tag -a:** the tagger comes from the committer resolver.
    - `GitCommand` gains `resolveAuthor`, `resolveCommitter`, `datedIdent` and `clock` for subclasses. `committerIdent` (reflog lines) now also reads `committer.*`.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `GitHostEnvironment.author` carries git's author variables, and `defaultEmail` carries `$EMAIL`.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`, `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE` and `EMAIL`.
- d489ee7: **`commit -i`, `COMMIT_EDITMSG` for every commit, `:(attr:...)` pathspecs and git's exit codes on errors.**
  
  - `CommitCommand.setInclude(...paths)` is git's `commit -i` / `--include`: the tracked paths named are staged into the held index lock as `add_files_to_cache` stages them, then the whole index is committed. It is refused with `--only` paths, with `-a` and without paths, in git's words; an element that matches no index entry fails as git 2.5x fails ("did not match any file(s) known to git"). `setAll` with paths now fails with git's "paths '<path> ...' with -a does not make sense".
  - Every commit writes `COMMIT_EDITMSG` as git's `prepare_to_commit` writes it: a `-m` message (or `--amend --no-edit`'s, or a prepared `MERGE_MSG` under `setEdit(false)`) with its whitespace cleaned up unless verbatim, before a commit with nothing to commit is refused. The refusals come in git's order (nothing to commit, then an empty message, then an untouched template), `-v` cuts a `-m` message at the scissors line, and `setEdit(false)` keeps a prepared message's comments (whitespace cleanup, as `--no-edit`). A prepared `MERGE_MSG` without an editor is taken as `git commit --no-status` takes it once the editor accepts it. `cherry-pick` / `revert --continue` write `COMMIT_EDITMSG` as their `git commit --no-edit --cleanup=strip` does, and `merge --continue` concludes the merge through `git commit` (its template, the status included).
  - `:(attr:<spec>)` pathspec magic (`name`, `-name`, `!name`, `name=value`, several per element) matches paths by their attributes from `.gitattributes`, `info/attributes`, `core.attributesFile` and the index, in status (tracked paths and the untracked scan, which matches an untracked directory by its own name), add, rm, commit `--only` / `-i`, diff, clean, stash push and combined `log` diffs. Status no longer refuses it; a command that does not take it refuses with git's "pathspec magic not supported by this command: 'attr'". `ContentAttributesContext.isDirectory` reads a directory's attributes as git's `git_check_attr` of a name ending in `/`; an untracked-scan pathspec may answer with a promise.
  - A `diff.submodule` without a value fails status, commit, log and merge as git 2.5x's `git_diff_ui_config` fails them.
  - `GitApiError.exitCode` is the exit status native git ends with: given, else from the message's lines (`usage:` 129, `fatal:` 128, a leading `error:` 1), else the class's default (1 for `NoMessageError`, `EmptyCommitError`, `AddFailedError`, `RmFailedError`, `MvFailedError`, `SwitchRefusedError`, `PathNotInIndexError`, `PathNotFoundInTreeError`, `MergeConflictError`, `InvalidMergeHeadsError`, `NotMergedError`, `CannotDeleteCurrentBranchError`, `StashApplyFailedError`, `PushRejectedException`; 129 for `MissingArgumentError`; 2 for `RemoteNotFoundError`, 3 for `RemoteAlreadyExistsError`; 128 otherwise); `withExitCode` sets it where the condition decides.
- 457680a: **Diff and blame output byte for byte as git's, on the xdiff port.**
  
  `@statewalker/vcs-utils`: the xdiff port takes git's whitespace flags (`ignoreWhitespace` `-w`, `ignoreWhitespaceChange` `-b`, `ignoreWhitespaceAtEol`, `ignoreCrAtEol`), and gains xdiff's emitter: `unifiedDiff`/`unifiedDiffHunks` print the hunks of `git diff` (context, inter-hunk context, `-W`, `--ignore-blank-lines`, function lines in hunk headers cut at 80 bytes and at broken UTF-8 as git does, `\ No newline at end of file`), `diffLineCounts` counts as the diffstat does, and `xdiffHunks` gives the changes `git blame` uses (common tail trimmed). `userdiffFindFunction` and `BUILTIN_FUNCNAME_PATTERNS` port git's diff drivers (builtin `cpp`, `python`, `java`, ... patterns of git 2.56, and `diff.<driver>.xfuncname`/`funcname`), matched as glibc matches them: `compilePosixRegex` (new, `@statewalker/vcs-utils`) is a POSIX regular expression engine over bytes, extended and basic syntax with the GNU extensions, leftmost-longest matches and subexpressions placed as glibc places them, back-references, `REG_ICASE` folding ASCII only, the patterns glibc refuses refused (git then dies with "Invalid regexp to look for hunk header"), and time linear in the line for patterns without back-references (JavaScript's backtracking took seconds on the `csharp` pattern). `BUILTIN_FUNCNAME_PATTERNS_2_43` holds the drivers of git 2.43 and 2.44 (`userdiffFindFunction`'s third argument). `legacyMinimal` reproduces git before 2.5x, whose `minimal` still set aside many-matched lines. Breaking: the JGit-style `HistogramDiff`, `HistogramDiffIndex`, `MergeAlgorithm`/`merge3Way`, `getAlgorithm`, `DiffAlgorithm` and `DEFAULT_ALGORITHM` are removed (nothing used them; diffs and merges run on `xdiff` and `mergeLines`); `SupportedAlgorithm`, `MyersDiff`, `RawText` and `RawTextComparator` stay.
  
  `@statewalker/vcs-commands`: `DiffCommand.callText(format)`/`callBytes(format)` print `git diff` as git does: the patch (`diff --git`, `old mode`/`new mode`, `new file mode`, `deleted file mode`, `similarity index`/`rename from`/`rename to`, `index` lines with abbreviated ids, `Binary files ... differ`, quoted paths, a file turned into a symbolic link as a deletion and an addition), `stat` (git's widths; `setOptions({ stat: { width } })` for `COLUMNS`), `numstat`, `shortstat`, `name-only` and `name-status`, with `setOptions` for `-U`, `--inter-hunk-context`, `-W`, `--diff-algorithm`, `--[no-]indent-heuristic`, the whitespace options and `--ignore-blank-lines`, and the configuration (`diff.algorithm`, `diff.indentHeuristic` default on, `diff.context`, `diff.interHunkContext`, `diff.renames` default on, `diff.renameLimit`, `core.quotePath`, `diff.statNameWidth`, `diff.statGraphWidth`, `diff=<driver>` attributes with `diff.<driver>.binary`/`.algorithm`/`.xfuncname`). `git diff` without trees now compares the index with the worktree (it compared HEAD with itself), `setOldTree(a)` alone compares `a` with the worktree (it compared `a` with HEAD), and paths sort in git's byte order; `setRenames(true)` detects renames in `call()`. `formatNoIndexDiff` prints `git diff --no-index` for two files. `DiffFormatter` now prints exactly git's text: `toString` ends lines with LF, `formatAll` concatenates, `formatBytes` keeps non-UTF-8 content, hunks start at 0 for an empty side (`@@ -0,0 +1,3 @@`), hunk lines include the `\ No newline at end of file` marker, and the default algorithm is myers as git's. `BlameCommand` is a port of git's blame (`blame.c`): commits by committer date, whole-file renames followed by default (git's default; `setFollowRenames(false)` is `--no-follow`), the xdiff diff with `diff.algorithm` and the indent heuristic, root commits as boundaries (`setShowRoot`, `blame.showRoot`), `setIgnoreWhitespace` (`-w`), `setLineRanges` (`-L`); lines are split at LF only (a CR-only file is one line, as in git). `BlameEntry` gains `previous` and `boundary`, `BlameResult` gains `content`, and `formatBlamePorcelain` (`--porcelain`, `--line-porcelain`) and `formatBlame` (default output, `-n`, `-f`, `-e`, `-s`, `-l`, `-t`, `-b`) print them as git does. Options for older git: `histogramRediff`, `legacyMinimal`, `renameCountLastSpan`, `namesFromContents` (diff) and `setCompatibility` (blame), `alwaysWidenAbbrev` (blame output).
  
  A conflicted path shows as git shows it: in `git diff`, the combined diff of stages 2 and 3 against the worktree file (`diff --cc`, `index a,b..0000000`, `@@@` hunks, dense as git's `combine-diff.c`; `writeCombinedDiff`), printed before the other files, and in the other formats as an unmerged entry followed by stage 2 against the worktree; in `git diff A`, the worktree file against `A`. A submodule with changes of its own shows as `Subproject commit <id>-dirty` in the worktree modes (untracked files in it count only with `diff.ignoreSubmodules=none`, as git's default ignores them; `diff.ignoreSubmodules` is read). `setOldTree`/`setNewTree` with a revision that names nothing throws (`RefNotFoundError`, "bad revision"), as git exits 128, instead of diffing against an empty tree. Abbreviated ids follow git: `core.abbrev` (auto, a number, `no`), 7 digits up to 16383 packed objects and more beyond (`findAbbrevLengths` in `@statewalker/vcs-core`, from the new optional `GitObjects.approximateCount`, which the files backend answers from its packs only, as git counts), and longer where another object shares the prefix; in `index` lines (`DiffOutputOptions.abbrevLengths`) and in blame's default output (`BlameResult.autoAbbrev()`, `formatBlame`'s `autoAbbrev`, then the boundary marks of git 2.5x or one more digit for older git).
  
  Remaining differences from git's diff: paths that are not valid UTF-8 (core and the stores keep paths as strings); `diff.noprefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix`, `diff.orderFile`, textconv drivers (`diff.<driver>.textconv`) and `submodule.<name>.ignore` are not read; glibc itself mishandles a few contrived patterns with word anchors after a repetition (it finds `b*\B` in "Ab" at 2), which the matcher does not copy.
- 820d99e: **`git diff` path prefixes, order file and textconv, as git prints them.**
  
  - **Prefixes:** `DiffCommand` reads `diff.noprefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix` (`i/` and `w/` for the index against the worktree, `c/` and `w/` for a commit against it, `c/` and `i/` for `--cached`, `a/` and `b/` between two trees, the combined diff of a conflicted path included) and `diff.srcPrefix` / `diff.dstPrefix` (git 2.45+). New options `noPrefix` (`--no-prefix`) and `defaultPrefix` (`--default-prefix`, which gives `a/` and `b/` whatever `diff.srcPrefix` / `diff.dstPrefix` say) join `srcPrefix` / `dstPrefix`. A prefix that needs quoting is quoted with its path, as git's `quote_two_c_style` (`"\303\244/a.c"`), in the `diff --git`, `---` / `+++` lines and the combined diff.
  - **Order file:** `diff.orderFile` and the option `orderFile` (`-O<orderfile>`) reorder the files of every output format as `diffcore-order.c` does (wildmatch on the path and its leading directories, first matching pattern, unmatched last, stable). A relative path is read from the worktree's root (one outside it, `../x`, through the host environment from the worktree's host path, `FileWorktree.hostPath`; without a worktree, from the git directory's, `GitWorkingCopyConfig.hostGitDir`); a missing file fails with git's message, and `diff.orderFile=~/x` without a home directory with git's "failed to expand user dir". `parseOrderFile`, `orderRank` and `orderByPatterns` are exported.
  - **Textconv:** `diff.<driver>.textconv` (with `.cachetextconv`; no cache is kept, which changes nothing in the output) converts both sides of a patch and of a combined diff, reading them as git's `prepare_temp_file` writes them (a blob smudged as checkout writes it, `eol` and filter drivers included; a worktree file as it is, not cleaned; `DiffSideSpec.readTextconvInput`), a converted side never being binary (`diff.<driver>.binary` and `-diff` still apply to the other cases); the option `textconv` is `--textconv` / `--no-textconv`. Running the command is the host's job: `installTextconvRunner(workingCopy, runner)` registers a `TextconvRunner`; without one no textconv applies (git's `--no-textconv`), where git would fail.
  - **Fixes:** `--stat` and `--numstat` count binary files in bytes even with `--text`, as git does; the `diff` attribute's driver applies to regular files only (an absent side, a symbolic link and a submodule take the default driver, as `diff_filespec_load_driver`).
- de6af72: **Fetch and clone check what they received, as git does.**
  
  - **vcs-transport:** `fetchFromConnection` runs git's `check_connected` before it writes any ref: every new ref tip must reach only objects that are here, down to the existing refs and the alternates' refs (`connectivityErrors`, receive-pack's walk, so a fetch on top of a branch reads only what it brought). A deepening fetch is checked against its new shallow commits before the shallow file changes; a clone checks the refs it writes, except those in a pack index-pack found self-contained. A failure throws `FetchCheckError` with rev-list's lines (`fatal: bad object`, `error: Could not read`, `fatal: bad tree object`, `fatal: missing blob object`). `FetchClientOptions.fsckObjects` runs index-pack's `--strict` over each received pack (imported into a quarantine, so a refused pack leaves nothing behind): fsck of every object and every named object received or present (`fatal: did not receive expected object`), as a clone's `--check-self-contained-and-connected` also does. `RepositoryFacade.loadObject` reads an object's content, `PackImportOptions.onObject` reports each received object, and commits and tags the typed stores refuse are walked as git parses them (`lenientCommitHeader`, `lenientTagTarget`).
  - **vcs-core:** pack imports report every received object with its place in the pack (`onObject` of `importPack`, `installPack` and `SerializationApi.importPack`: whole entries first, then deltas).
  - **vcs-commands:** `git fetch` fails with git's `error: <url> did not send all necessary objects` (exit 1) and `git clone` with `fatal: remote did not send all necessary objects` when a server leaves history out, writing no ref. `fetch.fsckObjects` / `transfer.fsckObjects` (or `FetchCommand.setCheckFetchedObjects`) check received objects with `fetch.fsck.<msg-id>` severities and `fetch.fsck.skipList` (`fetchFsckOf`), in git's words (`error: object <id>: <msg-id>: <message>`, `fatal: fsck error in packed object`, `fatal: index-pack failed`). `FetchCommand.setCompatibility` picks 2.43's or 2.5x's output.
  - **Received links read in memory:** pack imports report the content of every received commit, tree and tag while they hold it (`onContent` of `importPack`, `installPack`, `unpackObjects`, `SerializationApi.importPack` and `PackImportOptions`), and the fetch client checks index-pack's links from it (`ReceivedLinks`) instead of reading every object back: a clone's self-contained check of 219k objects takes 30 ms instead of 4 s. An import into a quarantine opens none for an empty stream, and a fetch discards its quarantine on any failure before the commit (a response error after the pack included). fsck reads only the blobs it checks (`ReceivedObjectsFsck.wantsBlob`). Lenient commit/tag reads ask the object's type first (`storedObjectType`) and are used by `VcsRepositoryFacade.getCommitInfo` with a commit-graph too; `VcsRepositoryFacade.getObjectType` reads the object header. `fetch.fsck.*` is read in config order where the config lists its variables (`RemoteConfigStore.entriesWithPrefix`): unknown ids give git's `warning: Skipping unknown msg id '<id>'`, the first demoted fatal id dies, and a `fetch.fsckObjects`/`transfer.fsckObjects` that is no boolean dies with `fatal: bad boolean config value`.
- a8df903: **`git.fsck()` and `git.verifyPack()` read each pack once, and SHA-1 is two to three times as fast.**
  
  On a repacked 5000-commit repository (39.7k objects in one pack), over the built packages, a full `fsck` takes 1.0 s instead of 2.3 s, `--connectivity-only` 0.6 s instead of 0.9 s, and `verify-pack -s` 0.7 s instead of 1.7 s. Output, exit status and verdicts are unchanged; the native comparison tests pass on git 2.43 and 2.56.
  
  - **vcs-commands (fsck, verify-pack):** a pack is checked in one sequential read: each entry's bytes feed the pack's SHA-1 and the entry's CRC-32 and are inflated there, once; a delta is applied to its base from a 16 MiB cache of the objects unpacked just before it (in pack order a base precedes its deltas), and each object is hashed in one call. Whatever that path does not handle as it stands (REF_DELTA, a base not cached, an object over 1 MiB, an entry reached through a corrupt part of the idx, any failure) goes through the pack's own reader, so error messages and verdicts are those of before. The pack checksum errors are still printed before the entries' output. The ids are read straight from the idx and the object table is probed with their bytes, the commit parser hands back where its ids are instead of decoding them, and the object hash keeps each slot's key next to its object number. A tree checked in full is remembered as the object numbers of its entries, so the connectivity walk does not unpack it again (not with `--name-objects`, nor for an object with a copy that failed a check); the walk reads through the store's synchronous path (`getNow`) and runs without awaiting when nothing needs I/O. `--connectivity-only` inflates no blob: a dangling object's type comes from its pack entry's header, following delta bases, as git's `packed_object_info`. `verify-pack` takes each entry's header from the same read instead of reading it again.
  - **vcs-commands (`fsck-object-checks`):** new `scanTree` (a tree's entries as a table, without an object per entry), `parseCommitLinks` (`parseCommitBuffer` with the ids left in place), `hexIdAt` and `rawIdAt`; `fsckTree` takes a tree's `scanTree` table when the caller has one.
  - **vcs-utils:** SHA-1 is this package's own instead of `@noble/hashes`': a fully unrolled JavaScript block function, and a WebAssembly one assembled at load time (no binary shipped) wherever the runtime compiles WebAssembly from bytes; elsewhere (workerd, a CSP without `wasm-unsafe-eval`) the JavaScript one runs. `createSha1()` uses it, so every caller gets the speed. New exports: `Sha1` (the incremental hasher without the `Hasher` wrapper), `sha1Of(head, body)` (a header and a body hashed as one message in a single WebAssembly run, padding included) and `sha1Wasm()`. CRC-32 is computed slicing by 8 (eight bytes per step, about twice as fast).
- 0a39cdc: **New `git.fsck()` and `git.verifyPack()` porcelain, with git's checks, messages, output order and exit status.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (`FsckCommand`):** `git fsck` after git's `builtin/fsck.c` and `fsck.c`. Every stored object is read and checked with git's message ids, severities and texts (`error in tree <id>: duplicateEntries: contains duplicate file entries`): trees (modes, zero-padded modes, order, duplicates, `.`, `..`, `.git` under its HFS+/NTFS spellings, full and empty names, null ids, symlinked `.gitmodules`/`.gitattributes`/`.gitignore`/`.mailmap`), commits (header order, tree and parent ids, author and committer lines, dates, time zones, NUL bytes), tags (object, type, tag name, tagger, gpgsig, extra headers) and `.gitmodules`/`.gitattributes` blobs. `fsck.<msg-id>` config, `setMessageType` and `setStrict` (`--strict`) set severities. Then everything reachable from the refs, the reflogs, the index (entries, cache-tree, resolve-undo), linked worktrees' HEADs and indexes, and the stash is walked; `missing`, `dangling` (default) and `unreachable` (`setUnreachable`) objects and `broken link`s are printed in git's order (the layout of git's object hash table). Options: `setConnectivityOnly`, `setDangling`, `setReflogs`, `setNameObjects`, `setRoot`, `setTags`, `setLostFound`, and `setCompatibility("2.43" | "2.56")` for the output differences between git versions: the default, `2.56`, is `git fsck --no-references` of git 2.45+ (HEAD's own checks moved to `git refs verify`, not implemented); `2.43` checks HEAD and linked worktrees' HEADs, following symbolic ref chains as git's `resolve_ref_unsafe` does (5 reads at most, HEAD's own included; past them `invalid HEAD`, and `bad ref for <reflog>`). The result has the structured findings, git's stdout and stderr text, and git's exit bits (`FSCK_ERROR_OBJECT`, `FSCK_ERROR_REACHABLE`, `FSCK_ERROR_PACK`, `FSCK_ERROR_REFS`). Symbolic refs that resolve to nothing, or need more than 5 reads, are `invalid sha1 pointer 0000…`. An invalid `core.bigFileThreshold` rejects the call with `FsckConfigError` and git's `bad numeric config value` message, as git dies on it. Over a files repository (`setRepositoryFiles`) loose objects are read from their files (zlib errors, headers, `hash-path mismatch`, stray files; blobs above `core.bigFileThreshold` streamed and hashed as git's `check_stream_oid` does; a buffer that grows to at most the declared size or what the file can inflate to; a stream that fit whole in git's 32-byte header read is cut to its declared size, as git's `unpack_loose_rest` cuts it) and packs are verified as git's `verify_pack` does (idx and pack checksums, per-entry CRC-32 and hash); over the memory, KV and SQL stores the objects of `history.objects` are checked and walked. Contents are read one object at a time.
  - **vcs-commands (`VerifyPackCommand`):** `git verify-pack` with `-v` (`<id> <type> <size> <size-in-pack> <offset> [<depth> <base>]`, the `non delta:` and `chain length = N:` histogram, `<pack>: ok`) and `-s`; a bad pack is reported with its checks' own messages and `<pack>: bad`.
  - **vcs-core:** `isDotGitComponent` (git's `is_hfs_dotgit` / `is_ntfs_dotgit`) and `isNtfsGitWorktreeFile` (git's `is_ntfs_dot*`) are exported; `openPack` takes the idx bytes (`idxBytes`) when the caller has read them.
  - **vcs-utils:** `inflateWhole` with `partial` now fills the output: the part of a stored block or a match that fits is written before it stops, as zlib does, so a header probe reads the start of an incompressible (stored) stream instead of nothing; input that runs out once the output is full stops it too, as zlib returns the full buffer then.
- 57846c3: **Faster fsck and commit-graph writing; the attribute source (`GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, `git --attr-source`, `attr.tree`).**
  
  - **vcs-core:** `scanPackObjects` reads a pack once in pack order and unpacks only the objects of the types asked for (a delta takes its base's type, so blobs and their deltas are skipped unread; a REF_DELTA whose base is an earlier entry of the same pack is unpacked as an OFS_DELTA is; what it does not handle as it stands is left to the store, and a pack whose deltas mostly lack their base is not read further); `visit` returning false stops the read. With `packEntryOrder` (offsets sorted as numbers, no comparator), `parsePackEntryHead` and `packIdxLookups`. `writeCommitGraphFile` computing many changed-path filters (1024 or more) unpacks the trees of the newest packs that way first, newest first as the store orders packs, while the packs' objects fit in 32 per filter computed (a large pack a few new filters do not justify is skipped) and up to 256 MiB of trees (the read stops there), keyed by their id bytes (option `prefetch` tunes the three); the tree diff is synchronous while its trees are in memory (no generators), and commits are read without a promise when the store holds them. The files written are unchanged; `reads.treesScanned` counts the trees unpacked beforehand. `VerifyCommitSource` may answer without a promise.
  - **vcs-commands:** `fsck` keeps git's object hash with each id in its slot (one place in memory per probe) and every object's slot, so the lookups git makes again move an object without probing; a full run checks each pack with typed arrays in pack order and a FIFO cache of bases carved from slabs; a `--connectivity-only` run unpacks each pack's commits and trees in one read in pack order (`statistics.objectsScanned`, REF_DELTA packs included) and replays git's lookups on them in git's walk order, a commit-graph's parents and trees are looked up from its bytes, and the commit-graph verification takes the commits from those reads (the copy git's verification reads: the first pack of its most-recently-used pack list that has the commit, unchecked). What a run keeps from those reads is bounded (`setMemoryLimits`, `statistics.linksKept` and `graphCommitsKept`): past the bounds the objects are read from the store again. `fsck` now reports what native git reports where it missed it: `parse_object` checks the hash of what it reads (`hash mismatch <id>`) for the refs, the reflog entries a connectivity-only walk meets untyped, the cache-tree (trees read again, as git frees their buffers) and resolve-undo, reading the packed copy first as git does; the walk reads typed objects as git's `parse_tree`, `repo_parse_commit` and `parse_tag` (`Object <id> not a tree`); a grafts file prints git's `graftFileDeprecated` hint (as 2.43 or 2.56 words it). Every attribute consumer with a worktree (checkout and check-in conversion, diff and merge drivers) and `log -- :(attr)`, which also works in a bare repository, read the in-tree attributes of the attribute source when one is set: `$GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` over `attr.tree`, resolved with git's revision syntax; a bad `$GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` fails with git's `bad --attr-source or GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, a bad `attr.tree` is ignored, an object that is no tree gives no in-tree attributes. `DiffCommand` still needs a worktree.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `GitHostEnvironment.attrSource` (`$GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`); `GitContentFilter` reads in-tree attributes from the source's tree (options `trees`, `resolveAttributesSource`, method `setAttributesSourceResolver`); `attributesSourceSetting`, `resolveAttributesSource`, `readTreeAttributesFile` and `AttributesSourceError`.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`.
- 5863bf1: **git hooks run through a portable host interface, and `git reset` refreshes the index as git does.**
  
  - `GitHostEnvironment.hookRunner` (optional) executes hooks; the commands find them as git's `find_hook` does (`core.hooksPath`, relative to the worktree top, else `$GIT_DIR/hooks` of the common git directory) through the repository's FilesApi (`Worktree.getFiles`), and a hook that is not executable gets the `advice.ignoredHook` hint (worded as the runner's `compatibility` says, 2.43 or 2.5x). Without a runner no hook runs, as before. On Node, `createNodeHookRunner` (vcs-utils-node) spawns the script, its stdout joined to its stderr where git joins them; pass it as `createNodeGitHostEnvironment({ hookRunner })`.
  - Hooks wired: commit (`pre-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `commit-msg`, `post-commit`, `post-rewrite` for `--amend`; `setNoVerify`), merge (`pre-merge-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `commit-msg`, `post-merge`; a refusal leaves the merge for `git commit`), checkout, switch, restore, clone and worktree add (`post-checkout`, its exit code in the result), rebase (`pre-rebase`, the post-checkout of onto, the picks' `prepare-commit-msg` and `post-commit`, `post-rewrite` "rebase"), cherry-pick and revert (the sequencer's `prepare-commit-msg` and `post-commit`), am (`applypatch-msg`, `pre-applypatch`, `post-applypatch`), push (`pre-push`, which can stop the push: `HookFailedError`), the receive side of a local push (`pre-receive`, `update`, `post-receive`, `post-update`, `push-to-checkout` for `updateInstead`, their output relayed as "remote: " lines), and the files ref store's batches (`reference-transaction`: `preparing` in 2.5x, `prepared`, `committed`, `aborted`; a refusal throws `RefTransactionAbortedError`). Commands expose what hooks wrote with `getHookOutput()` / `getHookStdout()`.
  - A commit with a hook runner updates HEAD in one transaction expecting its parent, as git's `update_head_with_reflog`.
  - vcs-transport: `PushClientOptions.prePush` (and `PushRefusedByHookError`), `relayPostReceive` for receive-pack (post-receive awaited before the side-band closes, as git), and the push client reads the side-band to its end so messages after the report reach `onProgress`.
  - `git reset` (mixed, and with paths) refreshes the index's stat data and reports "Unstaged changes after reset:" with `M`/`D`/`T`/`A`/`U` lines (`ResetCommand.getOutput()`), as `refresh_index` does; `setQuiet` and `setNoRefresh` as `-q` and `--no-refresh`, with the slow-refresh hint (`advice.resetNoRefresh`).
  - What a hook changes is taken as git takes it: `commit -a` reads the index lock back after `pre-commit` (`StagingIndexLock.reread`, `IndexFileLock.reread`); a partial commit (`setOnly`) gives its hooks git's temporary index (`$GIT_DIR/next-index-<n>.lock`: HEAD with the named paths), reads it back after `pre-commit` and removes it once the commit is made or given up. A hook that is a symbolic link counts only when its target is executable (followed through the FilesApi, else the host's `statFile`), and the Node runner reports a spawn failure (EACCES) as git's "cannot run".
  - reference-transaction: `committed` and `aborted` run once the ref locks are released; a deletion runs git's nested transaction on the packed store, and `Refs.remove(name, { deleteRefs })` runs the hook for refs under `refs/` (branch -d and tag -d pass `deleteRefs`, git's `delete_refs`); the hook context says which git version makes a call (`RefTransactionContext`). The receive side of a local push runs the remote's hook (a refusal makes receive-pack die, as git: the push fails with git's "fatal:" line and no ref moves); a command puts back the store's previous hook when it ends, and a command without a hook runner leaves a hook someone else set (`FileRefStore.setTransactionHook`) in place. A deletion that finds a ref lock or `packed-refs.lock` held tells the hook what git does (`aborted` only for the packed store's transaction, packed-refs.lock being taken before `prepared`). vcs-transport: `batchNonAtomic` (2.5x's two batches, each phase's update hooks run before its transaction, the deletions' committed before the rest's update hooks), `interleaveUpdateHook` (2.43's update hook then ref per command), `publishBeforeUpdate` (objects migrated after pre-receive, as git) and `BatchOptions.emptyTransaction`; the push client sends its commands in git's order (the remote's refs as advertised, then new ones).
  - rebase -i: `reword` runs `git commit --amend -e`'s hooks (pre-commit, prepare-commit-msg `commit HEAD`, commit-msg, post-commit, post-rewrite `amend`); `--continue` with staged changes those of `git commit -n -e` (prepare-commit-msg, post-commit, post-rewrite `amend` for an amend), with `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` as git sets it.
- 65474c5: **`git notes` as git does it, and notes carried over by `commit --amend` and `rebase`.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `git.notes(ref?)` gives the `git notes` subcommands: `list()`, `add()` (`-f`, `--allow-empty`, `-m`/`-F`/`-C`/`-c`, `-e`, `--[no-]separator`, `--[no-]stripspace`; an existing note without a message is edited, an empty message removes the note), `append()`, `edit()` (the message through a `NotesEditor` callback on git's template), `copy()` (`-f`, `--stdin`, `--for-rewrite`), `show()`, `remove()` (`--ignore-missing`, `--stdin`), `prune()` (`-n`, `-v`), `getRef()` and `merge()` (`-s manual|ours|theirs|union|cat_sort_uniq`, `notes.<ref>.mergeStrategy` / `notes.mergeStrategy`, conflicts in `.git/NOTES_MERGE_WORKTREE` with NOTES_MERGE_PARTIAL and NOTES_MERGE_REF, `--commit`, `--abort`, git's verbosity levels). Each returns what git prints (`output`, `errors`, `exitCode`); where git dies they throw `NotesError`. The notes ref is `--ref` (expanded as git's `expand_notes_ref`), `GIT_NOTES_REF`, `core.notesRef`, `refs/notes/commits`. `NotesTree` ports notes.c's in-memory 16-tree, so the notes trees written are native git's: the same 2-digit fanout directories as the number of notes grows and shrinks, entries that are no notes kept, git's commit and reflog messages (`Notes added by 'git notes add'`), the notes ref's commit as parent, the author and committer as git resolves them.
  - **Notes rewriting:** `commit --amend` (unless `setNoPostRewrite(true)`) and the sequencer's amends copy the amended commit's notes to the new one, and a finished `rebase` copies the notes along `rewritten-list` (`git notes copy --for-rewrite=rebase`), in the refs of `notes.rewriteRef` (no default: nothing is copied) or `GIT_NOTES_REWRITE_REF`, combined as `notes.rewriteMode` / `GIT_NOTES_REWRITE_MODE` say (`concatenate` by default, `overwrite`, `cat_sort_uniq`, `ignore`), unless `notes.rewrite.<amend|rebase>` is false.
  - **`git log` notes:** read through `NotesTree` (any fanout); the display refs are git's: the default notes ref, then `GIT_NOTES_DISPLAY_REF` or `notes.displayRef` (globs expanded; a plain name is taken as it is, as git takes it), and `LogOutputOptions.notesRefs` for `--notes=<ref>` (alone, only those refs; with `notes: true`, after the default ones).
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-utils-node:** `GitHostEnvironment` carries `notesRef`, `notesDisplayRef`, `notesRewriteRef` and `notesRewriteMode`, which `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads from `GIT_NOTES_REF`, `GIT_NOTES_DISPLAY_REF`, `GIT_NOTES_REWRITE_REF` and `GIT_NOTES_REWRITE_MODE`.
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-store-files:** `Checkout.lstatStateFile(name)` (optional) tells what is at a path of git's directory without following a final symbolic link, with a link's target (the files checkout through its PosixFilesApi `lstat`/`readlink`; state names may climb out of the directory with `..`), so `notes merge --commit` follows links in a linked worktree's own git directory; `LinkedWorktrees.refsOf(entry)` (optional) reads a worktree's refs (its HEAD and pseudo refs) from its administrative directory alone, for git's `find_shared_symref` across worktrees.
- 53ecad0: **`GIT_GRAFT_FILE` everywhere grafts count, git's diffstat widths, log decoration filters, `fetch -v` / `--porcelain`.**
  
  - **Grafts file:** `commitGraphCompatible` takes `readGraftFile` (the file `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` names, read in place of `info/grafts`); the files commit-graph provider and `writeCommitGraph` pass the environment's, so a graph is neither read nor written while that file has grafts. `git fsck` reads the grafts file the environment names too (its walk, its deprecation hint, its commit-graph checks). `fsck`'s `git commit-graph verify` now reads grafted commits as git does once the grafts file is read: the grafted parents from the object database, no parents from the graph (`VerifyGraphOptions.ungraphedParents`), and the grafts file's `bad graft data` errors repeated by that second process. A relative `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` is read from the worktree top (the git directory when bare), where git's setup has changed to before opening it, not from the process's current directory: `graftFileReader` takes a `GraftFileBase` (the repository's FilesApi and directory, or a host directory), and `graftFileBaseDir` gives that directory for a git directory.
  - **`diff --stat` widths:** names are measured with git 2.56's character widths (`gitStrWidth`, git's `utf8_strwidth`: byte count for invalid UTF-8) and shortened behind `...` as git 2.55+ does (`gitCharWidthAt`, diff.c's `utf8_ish_width`: whole characters, invalid bytes one column, control characters none). `blame`'s author column uses the same widths.
  - **Decorations:** `createRefDecorationFilter` is `git log`'s decoration filter (`--decorate-refs`, `--decorate-refs-exclude`, `--clear-decorations`, `log.excludeDecoration`, `log.initialDecorationSet=all`, the default set), and `loadRefDecorations` takes it as `logOptions` (with `decorate` / `log.decorate=full` for full ref names in `%d`, `%D`).
  - **Fetch:** `FetchCommand.setVerbose` (`-v`: ` = [up to date]` lines, the remote column measured over them) and `setPorcelain` (`--porcelain`: `<flag> <old> <new> <ref>` lines in `display`); `fetch.output=compact` is honoured. As in git, a refused ref update stops the fetch before tag backfill (no second round trip is made) and before the remote HEAD is followed. With `--atomic`, the stale refs of `--prune` are deleted in the fetch's one ref transaction (git's prune_refs queues them there): a refused update writes no ref, deletes no stale ref and writes no `FETCH_HEAD` line; a transaction the ref store refuses (a D/F conflict) leaves everything as it was, skips the remote HEAD, and is reported as git reports it: the lines the updates were accepted with, then git 2.5x's error (`FetchResult.errors`, `FetchClientResult.refTransactionError`; `FetchRefUpdate.accepted` and `aborted`). Without `--atomic` the prune stays a step of its own, first. The tags of objects already here are fetched with the tips (git's find_non_local_tags), not in a second round.
- bbf5ca1: **`git hook run` and `git hook list`, configured receive hooks, and the reference-transaction hook of pack-refs.**
  
  - `git.hookRun(name)` (`HookRunCommand`) is `git hook run`: the arguments after `--` (`setArgs`), `--to-stdin` (`setToStdin`, read only when a hook runs; "could not open" is fatal, exit 128), `--ignore-missing`, git's "cannot find a hook named" error (exit 1), git 2.5x's refusal of unknown hook names (`setAllowUnknownHookName`; 2.43 runs any name), the configured hooks before the hooks directory's script and their exit codes or'ed together. The result carries git's exit status and stderr; `getHookOutput()` gathers the same.
  - `git.hookList(name)` (`HookListCommand`) is git 2.5x's `git hook list` with `-z` and `--show-scope`: configured hooks by friendly name (`disabled` / `event-disabled` marked), the script as `hook from hookdir`, "no hooks found" (exit 1). git 2.43 has none: the command throws its usage error there.
  - The receive side of local and served pushes runs git 2.5x's configured hooks (`hook.<name>.*` of the receiving repository) for pre-receive, update, post-receive, post-update, push-to-checkout and reference-transaction, as receive-pack runs them through the hook API (`OpenedRepositoryConfig.listWithOrigin`).
  - `pack-refs` and `gc` run the reference-transaction hook as git's `files_optimize`: the packed store's transaction (`<zero> <id> <ref>` for each loose ref packed, also when there is none), then one transaction per pruned ref in reverse name order; a refusal throws `RefTransactionAbortedError` with nothing packed or the rest left loose. `pack-refs` without `--all` or refs packs the loose tags as git does (`Refs.packRefs` takes `prefixes`), and per-worktree refs (`refs/bisect/` ...) stay loose.
  - Config values carry their scope (`ConfigValueOrigin.scope`: system, global, local, worktree; an included file's is the including file's).
  - `Refs.packRefs` returns how many refs it packed (`PackRefsResult.refsPacked` counts those) and takes `auto` (git 2.5x's `pack-refs --auto`, `should_pack_refs`: nothing locked and no hook unless `max(16, log2(packed-refs size / 100) * 5)` loose refs wait; `PackRefsCommand.setAuto`, and 2.5x's `gc --auto`) and `onError`. A loose ref whose object is missing stays loose (`error: <ref> does not point to a valid object!`, `FilesRefBatchOptions.hasObject`), as does a loose name failing check_refname_format; a ref changed between its packing and its prune keeps its loose file (`error: cannot lock ref '<ref>': is at X but expected Y`, its transaction ending after `preparing`). The error lines go to the hook output and `errors` of `PackRefsResult` / `GCCommandResult`.
  - `git hook run --to-stdin=<path>`: a relative path climbing above the repository's files is read from the host (never the worktree's file of the clamped name); one that cannot be read is git's fatal "could not open".
  - A broken `hook.<name>.*` configuration of the receiving repository makes receive-pack die (`ReceivePackDiedError` from a receive hook): a local push fails with git's fatal line and "the remote end hung up unexpectedly", a served HTTP push's answer ends without a report; no ref moves.
  - Config include paths resolve `.` and `..` (`include.path=../inc` of a repository's config, read by `FileWorktree.openRepositoryAt` and the working copy alike); one above the repository's files is read from the host where the files have a root directory.
- e306a5e: **Identities and dates closer to native git.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** a fallback identity (a command's other, explicitly given identity) now gives only a name and an email, never a date. An amend or a concluded cherry-pick with nobody configured, `setAuthorIdent` with an old date, `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE` without a committer identity, and a merge with `setAuthor` all date the committer now (or by `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`), as git does. The fallback fills only the field git would guess: with only `$EMAIL` set, the name is the fallback's and the email is `$EMAIL`.
    - Any `user.email`, `author.email` or `committer.email` key, even empty or for the other role, stops git's `$EMAIL` lookup and gives an empty email (`<>`). Likewise for `*.name`. An empty `GIT_*_NAME` / `GIT_*_EMAIL` counts as given.
    - Commits and tags refuse an empty name (`fatal: empty ident name (for <email>) not allowed`, after git's hint when the name came from `user.name`) and a name of crud only (`fatal: name consists only of disallowed characters: <name>`), with the new `InvalidIdentError`, in git's order. Reflog lines are not strict.
    - `parseGitDate` is a port of git's `parse_date`. It refuses small bare epochs, dates outside 1970..2099 and 2-digit years from 10 to 69. It ignores invalid offsets (`+0160` without `@`) and normalises them after `@`. It skips unknown words. It reads `14/11/2023`, `1 1 2023 10:00`, `20231114T231320`, `+01:00:00` and out-of-range fields as git does. Local times resolve as glibc's `mktime`.
    - Each rebase pick, and each commit of a cherry-pick or revert range, reads the clock again (git's `reset_ident_date`), so picks and their reflog lines carry their own times.
    - `setAuthor`, `setCommitter`, `setTagger` and the `--author` of an amend strip crud from the name and email, as git does.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitStashStore` takes its author and committer from the new `identity` option, asked at each push. The default is now `webrun-vcs <vcs@localhost>` in the local offset (was `Git Stash <stash@localhost> +0000`), and the reflog line carries the committer.
- 2ce29f8: **Faster `git log -p` / `--stat` / `--name-status` and `git diff` between commits, with the same output.**
  
  On a 3,000-commit history (a few hundred files, built bundles) `log -p` takes about 3.1 s instead of 8.6 s (native git 1.0 s), `log --stat` 2.6 s instead of 6.9 s, `log --name-status` 0.8 s instead of 2.1 s; on a 20,000-commit one `log -p` takes about 32 s instead of 56 s.
  
  - **vcs-utils:** xdiff splits lines with a native `indexOf` scan, hashes them four bytes at a time, and a buffer's lines and hashes are kept (weakly) for its next diff, as `git log -p` diffs each version twice. The classification skips the lines both files share byte for byte at their start and end (their class is their twin's; same classes, same numbering, so the same diff). Line and prefix comparisons go four bytes at a time. `diffLineCounts` sums the script's changes instead of emitting hunks (unless `--ignore-blank-lines`), `writeHunks` writes each hunk as one buffer, `RawText.isBinary` looks for the NUL with `indexOf`, and short ASCII paths decode without a spread.
  - **vcs-core:** `Blobs.open` (optional) gives a blob's size and content from one read of the object; `probeBlob`, `load` and `size` use it (a packed delta was resolved two or three times before). `Trees.entries` (optional) hands out the cached decoded entries without a copy per read, and `Trees.raw` the stored tree bytes, which the new `TreeEntryCursor` walks decoding only what is asked (git's `tree_desc`). `GitObjects.listPrefix` and `findAbbrevLengths` take a `cache` in which a store keeps listings for one operation. `overlayMethods` binds each method once.
  - **vcs-store-files:** with that cache, the loose objects of each fan-out directory are listed once (git's `odb_loose_cache`) instead of once per abbreviated id.
  - **vcs-commands:** `log` and `diff` abbreviate through that cache (`createIdAbbreviator(workingCopy, { cacheListings })`); `log` prints inside one worktree batch, so attributes files and the configuration are read once for all its diffs, not per commit and path. Tree comparisons walk both trees' bytes in git's order and skip identical entries undecoded; a tree's full id is read at once (no revision lookup and header read); the commands sharing reads keep lately read small blobs (32 MiB), so a version is read once for its two diffs.
- 8274d13: **`git log` output as git prints it: `LogCommand.callBytes` / `callText`.**
  
  - **Formats:** `medium` (default), `short`, `full`, `fuller`, `reference`, `email`, `mboxrd`, `raw`, `oneline`, `pretty.<name>` aliases, `format:`/`tformat:` user formats (with the separator/terminator difference), `--abbrev-commit`, `--abbrev=<n>`, `--no-abbrev`, `--date=` in every mode (`relative`, `local`, `iso`, `iso-strict`, `rfc`, `short`, `raw`, `unix`, `human`, `format:`, and their `-local` forms; new `parseDateMode`/`showDate`), `--decorate[=short|full]` with `log.decorate`, `log.excludeDecoration` and `--decorate-refs`, notes (`refs/notes/commits`, `core.notesRef`, `notes.displayRef`), and the mailmap (`.mailmap`, `mailmap.blob`, `mailmap.file`; new `Mailmap`) for the built-in formats under `log.mailmap` and for `%aN`/`%aE`/`%aL`. Configuration defaults: `format.pretty`, `log.date`, `log.abbrevCommit`, `log.showRoot`, `log.follow`, `log.diffMerges`, `log.graphIndent`.
  - **`--graph`:** a port of git 2.5x's graph.c (`LogGraph`): columns, merges, octopus merges, collapsing lines, the indented "visual roots" (off with `graphIndent: false`, as git before 2.5x draws them) and `--graph-lane-limit`, with every format and the diffs prefixed by the graph; `--graph` and `topoOrder` sort as git's topological order, and a path-limited graph rewrites parents past the commits it hides.
  - **Diffs:** `-p`, `--stat` (80 columns less the graph), `--shortstat`, `--numstat`, `--name-only`, `--name-status`, root commits against the empty tree, `-m`, `--first-parent`, `-c`/`--cc` (combined diffs of a merge's trees, with the first parent's stat), `--full-diff`, `--reverse`, and `--follow` of one path across renames (each parent following its own path, as git 2.5x).
  - `DiffCommand` gains `setPathspec` (git pathspecs) and `callFormats` (several formats in `diff_flush`'s order, `--follow`); `writeCombinedDiff` takes `dense` (`-c`) and `workingTreeFile`; `formatCommitPretty` takes `formatDate` and `mailmap`; `formatRefDecorations` is exported. `git log --all` walks annotated tags from the commits they tag.
- 8274d13: **`git log` output: review fixes.**
  
  - `-m` and `--diff-merges=separate|first-parent` turn git's history simplification off for path-limited walks, as `--full-history` does (new `LogCommand.setFullHistory`): merges are compared with every parent and all are walked.
  - `--follow` finds copies of files the commit left unchanged (copies found harder, as git's `try_to_follow_renames`); `-c`/`--cc` with `--follow` limit the combined diff to the followed path, read in each parent under the name it had there. `--follow` with no or several paths, or with magic, throws git's error; `--graph` with `--reverse` too. `-n 0 --follow` shows nothing, and `--reverse -n <n> --follow` reverses the newest `n` commits walked.
  - `--diff-merges=<mode>` without `-p` diffs only the merges.
  - `log -p` / `--stat` read the trees directly (no ref lookup for a full object id in `DiffCommand`), compare only the subtrees that differ, and share the replace refs, the object count and lately read trees between the commits' diffs (new `GitCommand.shareReadsOf`): about five times faster.
  - `all()` walks the refs in git's order (by name, then HEAD) and combines with `add()`; `add()` peels annotated tags.
  - Commits with an `encoding` header are shown in UTF-8 (message and idents), as git re-encodes them; replaced commits are printed from their replacement.
  - `--full-diff` under `--graph` diffs against the parents before rewriting; `--abbrev=<n>` reaches the diffs' `index` lines and is clamped to 4..40.
  - Dates past JavaScript's range (`9999999999999 +0000` in the year 318857) and zones such as `+0060` and `-0001` print as git prints them; `format:` dates follow glibc's strftime (flags `_ - 0 ^ #`, widths, `E`/`O` modifiers) and `format-local:` names the local zone (`localTzName`).
  - `%N` stays literal without notes; a relative `mailmap.file` is read from the worktree when no host environment is given.
- 422447b: **Merges as git's merge-ort makes them: virtual merge bases, directory renames, octopus merges, merge drivers, renormalization, patch-id skips and resolve-undo, byte for byte with native git.**
  
  The merge machinery (`ortMerge`, internal) is now a port of merge-ort's own structure: every path of the three trees gets an entry with its three versions, renames are detected per side for the paths the merge needs (exact, by basename with git's directory guess, then by similarity, in the order git reads the trees, unchanged directories deferred as git defers them), and entries are resolved children first, so a file meeting a directory moves aside only when the directory stays.
  
  - **Several merge bases.** `merge` (and `pull`) merges the merge bases pairwise, oldest first, as `merge_ort_internal` does: "Temporary merge branch 1/2" markers two characters longer, binary files, links and modify/delete resolved to the base, the result named "merged common ancestors". The virtual commits and trees stay in memory; only content-merged blobs are stored, as git stores them. The merge bases come in git's order (a port of `paint_down_to_common`).
  - **Directory renames** (`merge.directoryRenames`: `conflict`, the default, `true`, `false`): what one side adds in a directory the other side moved goes along, with git's "CONFLICT (file location)" / "Path updated" messages, the split, collision and in-the-way conflicts, the "Avoiding applying" warning, and their interplay with rename/rename and rename/delete. A merge that only has directory rename trouble is unclean, as in git.
  - **Octopus merges**: `git.merge().include("a").include("b").include("c")` runs git-merge-octopus (trivial merges against each head's bases, then git-merge-one-file), with its messages, MERGE_HEAD listing every head, the refusal when an earlier head conflicts ("Should not be doing an octopus.") and the reduced head list in the message. Several heads get the setup of one: `setSquash` stages the octopus with SQUASH_MSG and leaves HEAD alone, `setStrategy(OURS)` commits HEAD's tree with every head as a parent (git-merge-ours), `RESOLVE` fails as git's two-head strategies do and `THEIRS` is refused (git has no such strategy), `setAutoStash`/`merge.autoStash` stash around it, unrelated histories are refused ("refusing to merge unrelated histories"), and `--no-ff` keeps HEAD a parent even where the heads contain it. A local change in the way stops the octopus at the head that touches it, as the script does.
  - **`MergeCommand.setAllowUnrelatedHistories`** (`--allow-unrelated-histories`): a two-head merge of unrelated histories merges against the empty tree, an octopus stops as git-merge-octopus does. Without it every merge of unrelated histories throws `NoMergeBaseError` with git's message. `setSquash` with `NO_FF` is refused as git refuses `--squash --no-ff`, and the `OURS` strategy never fast-forwards (git's `-s ours`), even with `FF_ONLY`.
  - **Merge drivers and renormalization**: the `merge` attribute (`text`, `binary`, `union`, a name), `merge.default`, `merge.<driver>.recursive`, `conflict-marker-size`; drivers of the host are callbacks (`installMergeDrivers(workingCopy, { name: createMergeDriver(fn) })`), since git's shell commands are not portable. `merge.renormalize` and `MergeCommand.setRenormalize` (`-X renormalize`) merge content as checked in afresh and drop a modify/delete whose change is only one of line endings or filters. `merge.renames`, `merge.renameLimit` are read too.
  - **Rebase drops commits upstream already has**, by git's patch-id over the diff, with the "skipped previously applied commit" warnings and hint; `RebaseCommand.setReapplyCherryPicks` picks them anyway. A rebase whose commits are all upstream moves the branch to upstream. As git's cherry_pick_list, upstream's side ends at the merge bases, and a cheap patch-id of the diff's file headers is compared first, the full one computed only where those collide. A pick that becomes empty is dropped with git's "dropping ... -- patch contents already upstream" and its AUTO_MERGE removed; a commit that was empty from the start is picked as it is. The sequencer's merge messages of a clean rebase pick are no longer reported (git shows them only for a conflicted pick).
  - **Resolve-undo (`REUC`)** follows git: kept by `commit`, `reset --soft`, `reset --mixed` (which records the conflict stages it removes) and an up-to-date merge; dropped when the index is written anew (merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase, their `--abort`, branch checkout, `reset --hard`, `--merge`, `--keep`, stash).
  - **`reset --merge` and `--keep`** (`ResetMode.MERGE`, `ResetMode.KEEP`) reset the index and the worktree as git does, refused with git's messages (`ResetRefusedError`) where they would lose local changes or untracked files; a soft or keep reset in the middle of a merge is refused. Every reset records ORIG_HEAD, and a reset or a branch checkout forgets a merge, cherry-pick or revert stopped before its commit (MERGE_HEAD, MERGE_MSG, CHERRY_PICK_HEAD, ...; a MERGE_AUTOSTASH is kept on `refs/stash`), as git's remove_branch_state. `Staging.clearResolveUndo` and `ReadTreeOptions.keepResolveUndo` are new; `GitContentFilter.renormalize` takes an attributes context.
- 7314646: **Merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase and pull write the working tree as git does.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `merge` is git's merge-ort (renames, line merge, modify/delete, rename/delete, rename/rename, binary, distinct types, file/directory placement as `file~HEAD`) written to the index and the worktree through a two-way switch from HEAD, smudged with the attributes of the resulting index. Before anything is written it refuses as git: local changes, staged changes (`  a b` / `Merge with strategy ort failed.`), untracked files in the way, unmerged entries, a merge or cherry-pick in progress.
