Conflicts leave git's markers (`merge.conflictStyle` `merge`, `diff3`, `zdiff3`; labels `HEAD`, the branch, `<abbrev> (<subject>)`, `parent of ...`), stages 1-3 and git's state (`MERGE_HEAD`, `MERGE_MSG`, `MERGE_MODE`, `ORIG_HEAD`, `AUTO_MERGE`, `SQUASH_MSG`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD`, `sequencer/`, `rebase-merge/`, `REBASE_HEAD`), so native git continues or aborts what we started and the other way round. New: `MergeCommand.setOperation(MergeOperation.ABORT | CONTINUE)`, `setAuthor`, `setCommitter`, `setAutoStash` (`merge.autoStash`); `CherryPickCommand` / `RevertCommand.setOperation(SequencerOperation.CONTINUE | SKIP | ABORT | QUIT)`, `setCommitter`, several commits through `sequencer/`; `RebaseCommand` keeps git's `rebase-merge/` state (continue, skip, abort across implementations), `setCommitter`, `setAutoStash` (`rebase.autoStash`), refuses on unstaged or staged changes, drops picks that become empty; `PullCommand.setRebase(true)` rebases (`PullResult.rebaseResult`) and a merging pull uses git's message (`Merge branch 'x' of <url>`). Results carry `output` (merge-ort's `Auto-merging` / `CONFLICT` lines) and `message` (git's error); a refused merge is `FAILED` with `failingPaths`. A content merge of files both sides changed no longer conflicts when the edits do not touch. `stash apply` shares the merge (and its conflict refinement) and records `AUTO_MERGE`.
  - **vcs-commands (what they never lose):** `merge --abort`, `cherry-pick` / `revert --abort` and `--skip` (git's `reset --merge`), and `rebase --abort` / `--skip` and the autostash (`reset --hard` as `reset_head` runs it) refuse as unpack-trees does instead of deleting untracked work: an untracked file where a file goes or on a leading path of one (`Untracked working tree file 'd' would be overwritten by merge.`), an untracked file or a nested repository in a directory a file replaces (`Updating 'd' would lose untracked files in it`), a file with unstaged changes (`Entry 'x' not uptodate. Cannot merge.`); `reset --hard` writes back tracked files deleted from the worktree. Merges, fast-forwards, picks and rebases see nested repositories in a directory a file replaces (`would lose untracked files in them`). A pick a rebase cannot write is rescheduled as git does (`It has been rescheduled`), `--continue` applies it. `merge --autostash` refuses unrelated histories before stashing, and applies the autostash back on any later failure; an autostash with a checkout that keeps no state files is refused rather than stranded. A deleted tracked file is an unstaged change for `rebase` (`--autostash` stashes it). `merge --abort` refused by the reset keeps `MERGE_AUTOSTASH` on `refs/stash`.
  - **vcs-commands (results as git's):** a rebase picks in git's order (`rev-list --reverse --topo-order`, merges dropped) and keeps `msgnum` / `end` as git counts them; `cherry-pick -n` / `revert -n` of several commits merge into the index (`revert -n` records `REVERT_HEAD`, a revert of an empty commit does not); a rename onto a path the other side added is merge-ort's rename/add (an add/add at the new path); `stash apply` labels the diff3 base `Stash base`, records no `AUTO_MERGE` when the stash is "Already up to date.", applies `--index` as `git apply --cached` (`does not exist in index`, `patch does not apply`, `ORIG_HEAD`), restores untracked files after the tracked changes (a refused or partial restore as `checkout-index --all`: `already exists, no checkout`); a refused merge's `restore_state` removes the directories it leaves empty. `add` resolves a conflict (stages 1-3 replaced by stage 0, or removed for a deleted file) and records git's resolve-undo (`REUC`); `commit` finishes a merge (`MERGE_HEAD` parents, reduced unless `MERGE_MODE` is `no-ff`, `MERGE_MSG` / `SQUASH_MSG` as the default message, no empty-commit refusal), a cherry-pick (the picked commit's author) or a revert, removes their state files (and the sequencer's after the last pick), applies `MERGE_AUTOSTASH`, refuses unmerged files as git (`Committing is not possible because you have unmerged files.`). Picks in rebase, cherry-pick and revert read each tree once, store only the directories a pick changes, plan and write only the changed paths, and merge the changed index entries into the index instead of sorting it whole; abbreviated todo ids resolve through one pass over the object store.
  - **vcs-utils:** `mergeLines` is git's `xdl_merge`: zealous conflict refinement (shared lines leave a conflict, conflicts at most three lines apart join), `style` (`merge`, `diff3`, `zdiff3`) with `baseLabel`, `favor` (`ours`, `theirs`, `union`), `markerSize`, and git's choice of CRLF markers.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Checkout` gains optional operation state files (`readStateFile`, `writeStateFile`, `removeStateFile`, `listStateDir`); `MemoryCheckout` keeps them in memory. `Staging.recordResolveUndo` (optional) and `IndexFile.recordResolveUndo` write resolve-undo records (`REUC`). `WorktreeWriteOptions.discardUntracked`: with `replaceInTheWay`, a directory in the way is removed whatever it holds (`reset --hard`, `checkout -f`); without it only one holding nothing but ignored files and empty directories.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitCheckout` reads and writes them in the git directory (`GitCheckoutFilesApi.list` lists a state directory). `FileWorktree` refuses (throws) to replace a directory holding an untracked file or a nested repository unless `discardUntracked`; the `FileStagingStore` editor applies edits path by path (a removal takes every stage of a path, an update replaces its stage-0 entry).
- 79b57ea: **Multi-pack-indexes as git reads and writes them, and repack and gc that keep them sound.**
  
  ```typescript
  // git multi-pack-index write --bitmap: the bytes native git writes for the same packs
  const written = await multiPackIndexWrite({ files, bitmap: true });
  // git multi-pack-index verify / expire / repack --batch-size, with git's messages and exits
  const { exitCode, stderr } = await multiPackIndexVerify({ files });
  // git repack -a -d -b -m: the new pack under a midx with a midx bitmap
  await repack({ ...backend, writeMidx: true, writeBitmaps: true });
  ```
  
  Native git reads a midx-covered pack only through the midx, so deleting packs a midx names
  leaves native fsck and `multi-pack-index verify` failing. Our repack and gc (and
  `PackSet.remove`) now clear such a midx first, with its bitmaps and an incremental chain, as
  git's `repack_remove_redundant_pack` does; `repack({ writeMidx })` writes the midx of the
  packs that stay before anything is removed.
  
  - vcs-core: `parseMultiPackIndex` (v1 and v2, chunks PNAM/OIDF/OIDL/OOFF/LOFF/RIDX/BTMP, git's
    refusals), `loadMultiPackIndex` (with 2.5x's `multi-pack-index.d` chain),
    `encodeMultiPackIndex` (git's entry selection, pack order, pseudo-pack order), the clear
    helpers, and `multiPackIndexBitmapPack` for midx bitmaps. The pack set uses a valid midx
    for lookups (`core.multiPackIndex`, three packs or more), falls back to the packs' own idx
    for a refused, corrupt (checksum) or stale midx (`midxErrors`), and prefers the midx's
    bitmap, refused when it names another midx. `PackFile.offsetAt`, `writePackBitmap({
    nameTags })`.
  - vcs-store-files: `multiPackIndexWrite` (`--preferred-pack`, `--stdin-packs`, `--bitmap`,
    2.5x `--incremental`), `multiPackIndexVerify`, `multiPackIndexExpire`,
    `multiPackIndexRepack`, `repack({ writeMidx })`, `readPackSetSettings`.
  - vcs-commands: fsck runs `multi-pack-index verify` per source (2.5x after each source's packs,
    object bit; 2.43 last, bit 040), checks the midx bitmap's checksum, and dies on a midx git
    refuses fatally.
  
  Crafted or damaged midxes cannot cost data or crash us: `expire` checks the checksum and that
  every pack still selecting objects opens before it deletes anything (git deletes the only
  copies left when a midx names a missing pack), PNAM entries must be `pack-<hex>.idx` names,
  incremental chains are capped and walked without recursion, a midx bitmap's `RIDX`/`BTMP` are
  checked before use, and a pack-int-id out of range is git's `fatal: bad pack-int-id` in verify
  and fsck while the pack set falls back to the packs; `midx.version=2` writes version 2 files as
  2.5x does.
- c3ee332: Add `git mv` (`MvCommand`, `git.mv()`) and `git read-tree` (`ReadTreeCommand`, `git.readTree()`).
  
  `mv` follows builtin/mv.c: every check before anything moves with git's messages (2.43's and
  2.5x's), directory moves carrying their index entries, `-f`, `-k`, `-n`, `-v`, case-only
  renames with `core.ignoreCase`, intent-to-add and symbolic-link sources, submodules (gitfile,
  `core.worktree`, `.gitmodules`) and the sparse checkout (`--sparse`, moves into and out of the
  cone). `read-tree` reads one or more trees, `-m` one-, two- and three-way (`--aggressive`,
  `--trivial`), `--reset`, `-u`, `-i`, `--prefix`, `--empty` and `--no-sparse-checkout`, with
  unpack-trees' plain refusal messages and git's cache-tree (primed for one tree, repaired
  otherwise). A three-way merge handles file/directory conflicts as unpack-trees does
  (`df_conflict_entry`: no trivial resolution against a conflicting side, no base stage for it),
  several trees without `-m` are read in traversal order, and `--prefix` drops the index entries a
  bound entry clashes with as a file or a directory; no index is written with a stage-0
  file/directory clash.
  
  `Worktree` gains optional `gitlinkRepository` (the place a submodule's gitfile names, null
  when the FilesApi cannot reach it) and `connectGitlink` (implemented by the file worktree);
  the file worktree's `rename` throws when the FilesApi refuses the move instead of reporting
  success, and the commit-graph writer no longer ignores a refused move (nor leaves its
  temporary layer behind when a split write fails); `setCacheTree(undefined)` now drops the cache-tree from the next index write;
  `repairCacheTree` computes git's `cache_tree_update(WRITE_TREE_REPAIR)` (an invalid
  directory stops its ancestors, as `update_one` does); `IndexAdder.put`
  stages a ready entry; `switchTrees` and `resetMerge` take `update`, plain-error and
  sparse-skipping options.
- bd31e0f: **git's native protocol over any byte stream: `git://` and ssh connectors on host hooks, and a git-daemon server.**
  
  - `Duplex.end?()` half-closes a stream's write side. The native clients end it where git closes its end (after a push's request, after the final flush of a fetch); a transport without it is left as it is. Node sockets that carry it need `allowHalfOpen: true` on both ends.
  - `serveGitConnection({ duplex, resolveRepository, enableReceivePack, ... })` serves a connection as git-daemon does: it reads the request line (`readGitDaemonRequest`, without losing the bytes after it), refuses as daemon.c does (`ERR access denied or repository not exported: <path>`, or the informative reasons; aliases such as `/../`, receive-pack unless enabled, a repository's `daemon.uploadpack` / `daemon.receivepack` override, upload-archive), and runs upload-pack (v0, v1, v2) or receive-pack with the repository's settings. `serveGitService` serves a session whose service, path and `GIT_PROTOCOL` arrived out of band (an ssh channel; `parseGitServiceCommand` reads `git-upload-pack '<path>'`).
  - `createGitDaemonConnector({ openSocket })` (`git://`, port 9418) and `createSshConnector({ exec })` (`ssh://`, `git+ssh://`, `ssh+git://`, `host:path`: git's `git-upload-pack '<path>'` with `sq_quote`, `GIT_PROTOCOL=version=<n>`), registered by `createDefaultTransports({ openSocket, exec })` only when the host gives the hook. `GitConnectRequest.program` carries `--upload-pack` / `--receive-pack` and `remote.<name>.uploadpack` / `receivepack`.
  - Served paths are client input: both modes refuse git-daemon's aliases (`daemon_avoid_alias`: `..`, `//`, `/./`, a trailing `/.` or `/..`), a NUL and a backslash before `resolveRepository` sees them (`isUnsafeServicePath`; an implicit session's relative and `~/` paths checked as if below `/`). `createBasePathResolver({ files, basePath, openRepository, exportAll, userPath, strictPaths, whitelist })` maps a path as git-daemon does: base-path join, `~user` only through `userPath` (an empty user, `~` or `~/x`, refused), an empty path refused, enter_repo's `.git` suffixes, the directory list, `git-daemon-export-ok` unless `exportAll`, and nothing outside the base (symbolic links followed where the FilesApi reads them).
  - Served settings layer as git's config files do: git's defaults, the operator's `settings`, what the repository's config sets explicitly (`explicitServerSettingsFromConfig`; `serverSettingsFromConfig` still gives every key), then the resolver's `settings`; hidden ref lists concatenate in layer order across `hideRefs` and the service lists (`layerServeSettings`), so a repository's `!pattern` never unhides a ref the resolver hides. An implicit session ignores `daemon.*` unless `allowOverride`, and refuses with one text unless `informativeErrors`. `serveGitConnection` takes `initTimeout` and `timeout` (git-daemon's `--init-timeout` / `--timeout`; `timeout` bounds reads and writes alike, so a client that stops reading is cut off).
  - ssh: a host or user starting with `-` is refused ("strange hostname '<user@host>' blocked"), and `sshArgs(target)` gives an ssh client's arguments with `--` before one `user@host` argument and a checked port. `parseGitServiceCommand` reads the command as git-shell's `sq_dequote` (nothing after the quoted argument, no `-` argument).
  - Smart-HTTP `info/refs` failures use git's words: "fatal: repository '<url>/' not found", "fatal: Authentication failed for '<url>/'", "fatal: unable to access '<url>/': The requested URL returned error: <code>", a `text/plain` body first as `remote:` lines.
  - `ls-remote` keeps the v2 symref and peeled data (`RemoteRef.symbolic`, `target`, `peeledObjectId`), takes the default branch from HEAD's symref, sends `ref-prefix` for `--heads` / `--tags` over v2 (with `ref-prefix HEAD`, so the default branch is still known), shows both kinds with both flags, and passes `setUploadPack` (else `remote.<name>.uploadpack`) to the connector. `fetch().setUploadPack()` and `clone().setUploadPack()` (git's `--upload-pack` / `-u`) too.
  - vcs-commands: `servedRepositoryOf(openedRepository, { hooks })` gives `serveGitConnection` a repository with the settings git's receive-pack reads from it (the `receive.*` and hidden ref ones through its `config`, so only what that sets overrides the operator's).
  - `IdleTimer` and `abortErrorOf` are no longer exported from the package root.
- 1efa875: **Paths that are not UTF-8 in pattern files, patches, command text and the SQL store, as in git.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `decodeGitPatternFile` reads a pattern file as git does (bytes through `decodeGitPath`, a leading BOM skipped); `unquoteGitPath` is git's `unquote_c_style` on bytes. The patch parser reads C-quoted names (`"a/caf\351.txt"`, in `diff --git`, `---`/`+++`, `rename`/`copy` lines and `diff --cc`) and raw names (`core.quotePath=false`) as the paths trees and the index hold, and splits two unquoted names with spaces where both halves name the same path, as `git apply` does.
  - **vcs-working-tree, vcs-store-files:** `.gitignore`, `info/exclude`, `core.excludesFile` and `.gitattributes` keep their bytes: a latin-1 rule (`caf\xe9.txt`, `*\xe9*`) matches the path bytes git matches, so status, clean, add and the `text`/`eol`/`ident` conversions agree with git.
  - **vcs-commands:** `DiffCommand.callText()` and `DiffFormatter.toString()` decode the output bytes with `decodeGitPath`: a byte that is not UTF-8 is an escape instead of U+FFFD, and `encodeGitPath(text)` is `callBytes()`. `formatStatusPorcelain` takes `{ quotePath }` (`core.quotePath=false` prints the bytes) and quotes control characters with git's letters (`\r`, `\a`, ...). `diff.orderFile` patterns keep their bytes. State files (`MERGE_MSG`, ...) are written and read as bytes, so a conflicted latin-1 path is listed as git lists it, and conflict lists and the stash and sequencer path orders follow git's byte order (`compareGitPaths`).
  - **vcs-store-sql:** paths and tree entry names in TEXT columns (`tree_entry`, `tree_delta_entry`, `staging_entry`) store a lone surrogate as U+0001 and four hex digits (`toSqlPath` / `fromSqlPath`), since drivers turn it into U+FFFD. UTF-8 names are stored unchanged, so no migration is needed. A tree rebuilt from the store keeps its id.
  - **vcs-store-sql, vcs-store-kv, vcs-store-mem:** staging entries sort in git's byte order (`compareGitPaths`) instead of UTF-16 code units.
- d489ee7: **Local remotes, rebase by name, missing stashes, attr pathspecs and exit codes.**
  
  - `rebase().setUpstream()` takes a revision (`main`, `HEAD~2`, a tag) as well as an id. It is resolved when the rebase starts and fails with git's `fatal: invalid upstream '<x>'` before anything is written. Before, the name itself was written into `.git/HEAD`. The reflog keeps the name as given (`checkout main`).
  - Every ref store refuses a direct ref value that is not a full lowercase hex object id. This covers `set`, `compareAndSwap` and `batch` in the files, memory, KV and SQL stores (`isRefObjectId`, reject reason `invalid-object-id`), so a name can no longer end up in a ref. Symbolic refs are written as before.
  - `fetch`, `ls-remote`, `clone` and `push` reach a repository named by a path or a `file://` URL, as git's local transport does. The repository is opened through the working copy's storage (`Worktree.openRepository`, which follows git's `enter_repo`), and upload-pack or receive-pack is served in process over an in-memory duplex. A path with no repository there fails with git's "does not appear to be a git repository" lines; a clone of such a path fails with "repository '<path>' does not exist". A `<helper>::` address or an unknown scheme fails with git's "unable to find remote helper for '<scheme>'".
  - `stashDrop` and `stashApply` fail as git does on a missing stash: "No stash entries found.", "error: stash@{n} is not a valid reference" (exit status 128, as in git 2.5x) and "fatal: log for 'stash' only has N entries". `setAll(true)` without a stash still returns undefined.
  - `stash push -u` with `:(attr:...)` enters an untracked directory only when the directory's own name and attributes match as a leading path, as dir.c's `treat_directory` does (`PathspecAttributes.reachable`, shared with `add`).
  - Exit codes are inferred from a message's prefixes only where git puts them: `usage:` or `error:` on the first line, and `fatal:` on the last line (or the first). A path in a listing, such as `fatal: x.log`, no longer changes the status. Error classes that always end with the same status set it explicitly (`AddFailedError`, `EmptyCommitError`, `NoMessageError`, `NotMergedError` and others).
  - `commit().callDryRun()` honours `setInclude`: it runs the same option checks, stages the named tracked paths on the held index, shows the status, and rolls the index back.
  - An initial commit, or `--amend` of a root commit, with an empty index (or one holding only intent-to-add entries) fails with "nothing to commit" unless `setAllowEmpty`.
  - When the status is not part of the template, an editor's commit decides whether there is anything to commit with git's `index_differs_from` (HEAD, or HEAD^1 when amending) instead of a status run. As a result, `--amend` with `setIncludeStatus(false)` no longer refuses a commit that changes the amended commit's parent.
  - `add().setCompatibility("2.43")` refuses `:(attr:...)` with git 2.43's message, so `stash push` under 2.43 compatibility fails after saving the stash, as git 2.43 does. `diff` of trees in a bare repository works and reads the bare attributes for `:(attr:...)`, as `log` does. `PathspecAttributes.forWorktree` without a worktree throws a `GitApiError` instead of a plain `Error`.
- d489ee7: **Local push respects every worktree and the target's config; clone refuses a non-empty destination.**
  
  - A push to a local repository refuses to update or delete a branch checked out in any worktree of the target, linked ones included, as git's `find_shared_symref` does (a worktree that is rebasing or bisecting a branch counts too). receive-pack takes these branches as `checkedOutBranches` (`serveOverDuplex` and the smart-HTTP server's `receivePack` options), so a server for a repository on disk can apply the same rule.
  - Local receive-pack and upload-pack read the target's configuration, as git's do: `receive.denyNonFastForwards` (unset, forced updates are accepted, as in git), `receive.denyDeletes`, `receive.denyCurrentBranch` (`refuse`/`true`, `false`/`ignore`, `warn`, `updateInstead`; unset, refused with git's advice), `receive.denyDeleteCurrent`, `receive.shallowUpdate`, and `transfer.hideRefs` with `uploadpack.hideRefs` or `receive.hideRefs` for fetch, ls-remote and push. `serverSettingsFromConfig` (vcs-transport) is that mapping, and `httpServerSettings` shapes it for the HTTP server. `ReceiveDenyAction` widens `denyCurrentBranch` and `denyDeleteCurrent`.
  - `receive.denyCurrentBranch=updateInstead` updates a clean worktree to the pushed commit (git's `push_to_deploy`, through `ReceivePackConfig.updateWorktree`) and refuses one with unstaged or staged changes in git's words. The push-to-checkout hook is not run.
  - A repository facade made by `createRepositoryFacade` records accepted shallow pushes (`updateShallow`) when its refs keep a shallow list.
  - `clone` refuses a destination that has refs, index entries or worktree files, or that the local source resolves to, with git's "destination path '<dir>' already exists and is not an empty directory." (exit 128), before anything is written, over smart HTTP too. `setDirectory` names the directory in that message.
  - A clone of an empty repository with `--single-branch` writes no fetch refspec, and `--branch` on it fails with git's "fatal: Remote branch <b> not found in upstream <remote>". A clone of a corrupt local source fails with git's "aborting due to possible repository corruption on the remote side" lines (exit 128). A fetch of a ref the remote does not have (a hidden one included) fails with "fatal: couldn't find remote ref <ref>".
  - `Worktree.openRepository` opens a linked worktree (a gitfile into a `commondir`) as a remote, with that worktree's own HEAD; a linked worktree's git directory is not bare. `OpenedRepository` carries `commonDir`, `config` and `worktrees`.
  - `rebase().setUpstream("")` fails with "fatal: invalid upstream ''".
  - An uppercase full object id names its object: `tag().setObjectId()` (which also takes any revision), `commit().setParentIds()` and the other setters taking ids store it lowercase, and `setHead` takes it as a commit. Loose refs, packed refs and nested HEADs holding an uppercase id read as git reads them (lowercased), so `branch -m`, `commit` and `pack-refs --all` work on such repositories.
- 2796b2d: **Pack reachability bitmaps (`.bitmap` v1), read, used and written as git does.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `EwahBitmap` (git's `ewah/`: 64-bit words held as two halves, run-length words, `ewah_set`/`ewah_add`/`ewah_xor` in git's exact buffer layout, JavaEWAH serialization) and `RawBitmap` (git's uncompressed `struct bitmap`, `bitmap_to_ewah`).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** the untracked cache and split index use the shared `EwahBitmap` (`Ewah` is now an alias of it).
  - **vcs-core:** `parsePackBitmap` reads a pack's bitmap (header and options, type bitmaps, XOR-compressed entries, lookup table, name-hash cache), checks its trailing SHA-1 and pack checksum, and refuses a damaged file with git's message (`PackBitmapError`); `PackSet.bitmap()` loads the first usable one (`bitmapErrors` holds git's error lines). `bitmapWalk` enumerates wants minus haves through it (unbitmapped tips walked down to bitmapped commits, objects outside the pack in an extended index, the `blob:none`, `tree:0` and `object:type` filters), and `listObjects({ bitmap })` and `exportPack` (`useBitmaps`, git's `pack.useBitmaps`) use it. `writePackBitmap` writes git's file byte for byte: `bitmap_writer_select_commits`' selection, the XOR choice of `compute_xor_offsets`, 2.5x's or 2.43's entry order, the lookup table and name-hash cache. `PackFile` gains `packOrder()`, `idAt()` and `indexOf()`; `PackObjectRef` and `ListedObject` take a `nameHash`.
  - **vcs-transport:** `createRepositoryFacade({ useBitmaps })`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `repack({ writeBitmaps })` (`git repack -b`, `repack.writeBitmaps`, git's default of writing for `-a` in a bare repository, `pack.writeBitmapHashCache`, `pack.writeBitmapLookupTable`, `pack.preferBitmapTips`) writes the new pack's bitmap through a temporary file, drops a stale one, refuses `-b` without `-a` (`IncrementalBitmapError`) and warns as git when alternates keep objects out; `gc` writes one in a bare repository.
  - **vcs-commands:** fsck verifies bitmap checksums as each git version does (`verify_bitmap_files`); served repositories honour `pack.useBitmaps`; push passes `setUseBitmaps` on.
  - **Fixes after review:** name hashes are git's `pack_name_hash` over the path's bytes (non-ASCII and non-UTF-8 names, only git's isspace bytes skipped), trees and blobs named by refs are listed as git's pending objects (named "", before the commits' trees; `listObjects({ tipPaths })` names index entries and cache-tree nodes), the plain walk marks only edge trees uninteresting, `pack.preferBitmapTips` is matched as each version does (`isPreferredBitmapTip`), `repack.packKeptObjects` and `push.useBitmaps` are read and local transports pass the remote's `pack.useBitmaps`, a bitmap the new pack cannot close fails the repack before anything is replaced (`BitmapWriteError`), the bitmap is not used when no have is in its pack, and bitmaps claiming more objects than their pack are refused (stray bits ignored).
- 7c63add: **Faster object reads and `log`; `log` walks as git's revision.c also without paths.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** the pack read path does less per object: `PackFile.getNow`/`get` are plain functions (no generator per read; a delta resolution is a small state machine that runs synchronously while its entries are in loaded windows and finishes through I/O otherwise), the entry header is parsed without a closure, whole objects hand out a one-chunk content object instead of an async generator, a delta's cycle set and `visiting` set are built only when a REF_DELTA needs them, and the scratch buffers of a chain are the pack's (copied out when a resolution waits). Idx lookups use a second fan-out level by the ids' second byte, built as each bucket is checked (idx files of 4096 objects or more), and a pair table to parse hex ids; `PackStats.idCompares` counts the ids compared. The pack set and `withPacks` return an in-memory object without a generator. The tree, commit and commit-graph-writer caches evict through `OrderedMap` (a `Map`'s `keys().next()` walks every deleted slot: microseconds per eviction).
  - **vcs-utils:** `OrderedMap` (insertion-ordered map with a constant-time oldest entry, for FIFO/LRU caches; its `keys()`/`values()` walk on through deletions and moves as a `Map`'s iterators do); `bytesToHex` builds an object id as one flat string.
  - **vcs-commands:** `LogCommand` routes every log, with or without paths, through one port of git's walk (get_revision_1 streaming, limit_list with excluded commits), so `log ^X`, ranges, `--since`/`--until`, `-n`, `--skip`, `--first-parent`, `--author`, `--merges`/`--no-merges` list git's commits in git's order (date order, clock skew included; shallow commits have no parents). The walk keeps commits in typed arrays (`RevTable`, a few dozen bytes each) and reaches commit-graph commits by position; commits outside the graph are read once (`parseGraphCommit`, then decoded whole only if shown). `LogPathStatistics.commitsWalked`/`walkTableBytes`. Pathspecs match raw tree entries as bytes (`treeEntryInteresting(ps, tree, start, end, mode, base)`), with each element's pattern bytes prepared once. `:(attr:...)` pathspec magic in `log` (git's match_pathspec_attrs: the worktree's `.gitattributes` with no index read, `info/attributes`, `core.attributesFile` and the system file; in a bare repository only the files outside the tree, as git 2.56 without `attr.tree`; names that are no UTF-8 looked up as the worktree names them) and `:(prefix:N)` for every pathspec, N counting bytes (also inside a character, in `log` and `clean` alike), compared case-sensitively under `icase`, parsed as git's `strtol` (empty is 0, negative is no prefix); other commands still refuse `attr`. Path bytes that are no UTF-8 (escaped in names) match by their bytes in `log`'s literal pathspecs (`log -- n\xffx`, `:(literal)`, a directory `d\xfe`), and `clean` matches every pathspec element byte by byte as git does, so an element that cuts a UTF-8 character (`*\x84`, `A/\xc3*`, under `:(exclude)` or `:(icase)`, which folds ASCII only) matches git's names: `clean` no longer removes a file a cut `:(exclude)` keeps.
  - **vcs-store-files**, **vcs-working-tree:** read through the faster paths (`withPacks.getNow`, the attributes parse cache on `OrderedMap`).
- 966768c: **Partial clones fetch absent objects lazily, as git does; gc, repack and fsck handle promisor objects.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** `GitObjects.promisor` (`ObjectPromisor`, `createObjectPromisor`, `withLazyFetch`): a read (`get`, `getNow`, `getRaw`) of an absent object asks the installed `PromisorFetcher` once and reads again; `has` never fetches. `prefetch(ids)` fetches the absent ones in one batch, `objects.withoutLazyFetch` is the store as git's `fetch_if_missing = 0` reads it, `isPromisorObject` / `promisedObjects` / `promisorPackObjects` follow packfile.c's `is_promisor_object`. A promisor remote that cannot send an object it promised throws `PromisorFetchError` (`could not fetch <id> from promisor remote`). `Blobs.prefetch` and `Blobs.mayFetch` let checkouts plan absent blobs and fetch them in one batch. `listObjects({ excludeObjects })` is `--exclude-promisor-objects`.
  - **vcs-transport:** `fetchPromisedObjects` is the lazy fetch git runs (`fetch --filter=blob:none --stdin` with the noop negotiator, no ref listing, no tags or refs written, a kept promisor pack); `FetchClientOptions.refetch` (`git fetch --refetch`); a deepening fetch under a filter checks connectivity as a partial clone's.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the files backend wraps its objects with the promisor (promisor packs known by their `.promisor` files). `gc()` and `repack()` run in a partial clone as git's: the promisor objects stay out of the walk and `repack -a` writes every object of the promisor packs into a promisor pack of its own with an empty `.promisor` file (`repack_promisor_objects`). `RepackOptions.filter` is `git repack --filter=<spec>` (the filtered-out objects in a pack beside the new one). `promisorExclusions`.
  - **vcs-commands:** every command installs a `PromisorFetcher` (`createPromisorFetcher`, `installPromisorFetcher`) that fetches from the promisor remotes in git's order (`promisorRemotes`: `remote.<name>.promisor`, `.partialclonefilter`, `extensions.partialClone` last), honouring `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` (git 2.45+: "lazy fetching disabled; some objects may not be available"). Checkout, switch, reset, clone and the other unpack-trees writers prefetch the blobs they write in one batch (`check_updates`), diff prefetches its pairs for patch and stat outputs (`diff_queued_diff_prefetch`) and rename detection its candidates (`basename_prefetch`, `inexact_prefetch`), merge-ort the blobs of its content merges, so `log -p`, status and merges fetch as often as git. `FetchCommand.setFilter` (`--filter`, `--no-filter`, partial_clone_register) and `setRefetch`; a fetch from a promisor remote uses its `partialclonefilter`. Fetch, clone, fsck, gc and repack walk with lazy fetching off. `FsckCommand` treats promisor objects as git's fsck does (reachable without being walked, never missing).
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-utils-node:** `GitHostEnvironment.noLazyFetch` (`GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`).
  - **Review fixes:** promisor-pack imports (filtered fetch and clone, the lazy fetch) run git 2.48+'s `repack_local_links`, packing the local objects a received promisor pack links to into one more promisor pack (`ObjectPromisor.localLinkObjects`, `RepositoryFacade.repackLocalLinks`), and gc/repack walk the stored objects promisor objects link to as roots, so a pushed commit the server built on is never pruned; the lazy fetch checks that every wanted object arrived (git's `did not send all necessary objects`, then `PromisorFetchError`), runs `fetch.fsckObjects`/`transfer.fsckObjects`, and transport commands use their own transports and credentials for it (`installPromisorFetcher` for later commands); walks that must not fetch read through `objects.withoutLazyFetch` / `historyWithoutLazyFetch` instead of a store-wide switch (`ObjectPromisor.withoutLazyFetch` is gone), served and received packs included; fsck dies as git on an unreadable promisor pack object (`PromisorObjectReadError`, exit 128); a remote with only `partialclonefilter` counts as a promisor remote.
- 7b3fca1: Interactive patch modes as a library API: `add -p`, `reset -p`, `checkout -p`, `restore -p` and
  `stash push -p` (`setPatch(true)` with a `setPatchSelector` callback answering git's prompts),
  backed by a port of git's add-patch.c hunk engine (`runAddPatch`, git's patch-mode tables and
  prompt texts for 2.43 and 2.5x, split, edit, goto, search, `--no-auto-advance`). `DiffCommand`
  takes `reverse` (`diff -R`) and `setTemporaryIndex` (a diff over another index, as
  `GIT_INDEX_FILE`). `git apply --cached/--index` now smudges the racily clean entries it keeps,
  as git's write without a refresh does.
  Under 2.5x compatibility `stash push -p` builds its temporary index as git's
  `create_index_from_tree` (skip-worktree and assume-unchanged paths are not offered, and a replaced
  flagged entry whose file changed fails the stash); the post-index-change hooks a selection runs
  report through the calling command's `getHookOutput()`.
- d333a4d: **`git apply`, `git format-patch` and `git am`, byte for byte as git.**
  
  - **ApplyCommand** (`git.apply()`): a port of apply.c. It reads git diffs (renames, copies, mode changes, new and deleted files, quoted names, `GIT binary patch` literal and delta hunks, `\ No newline at end of file`) and traditional unified diffs (`-p<n>` guessed or given, `--directory`). It applies them to the worktree, to the index and worktree (`--index`) or to the index alone (`--cached`), with offsets, reduced context (`-C<n>`), `--unidiff-zero`, `--recount`, `--inaccurate-eof`, `-R`, `--reject` (`.rej` files), `--3way` (`--ours`/`--theirs`/`--union`), `--include`/`--exclude`, `--allow-empty` and `-N`. `--check`, `--stat`, `--numstat` and `--summary` report without applying. Whitespace follows `--whitespace=nowarn|warn|fix|error|error-all`, `apply.whitespace`, `core.whitespace` and the `whitespace` attribute, with git's warnings. Errors and `-v` messages match git's wording, git 2.46's `<file>:<line>` locations by default and the older `line <n>` with `setLegacyMessages`.
  - **FormatPatchCommand** (`git.formatPatch()`): commits as mails. It supports numbered subjects, `--subject-prefix`, `--rfc`, `-v`, `--start-number`, `-k`, `--signoff`, `--cover-letter` (with shortlog and diffstat), `--thread`/`--in-reply-to`, `--to`/`--cc`/`--add-header`, `--from`, `--base` (prerequisite patch ids), `--zero-commit`, `--no-stat`, `--no-binary`, `--numbered-files`, `--suffix`, `--filename-max-length`, `-o` and `--stdout`, and the `format.*` settings. Signatures use an injectable version string (default `2.56.0`), and an injectable clock sets the Message-ID times.
  - **AmCommand** (`git.am()`): mailboxes applied one commit per mail. It handles mailsplit (mbox, mboxrd, `--keep-cr`) and mailinfo (RFC 2047, charsets, quoted-printable and base64, multipart, in-body headers, scissors, `-k`/`-b`, `-m`), and keeps git's `.git/rebase-apply/` state. It sets `ORIG_HEAD`, writes the `am: <subject>` reflog lines and prints git's messages and advice (git 2.4x's stdout advice with `setLegacyMessages`). It supports `--3way` through a fake ancestor and merge-ort, `--continue`/`--resolved`, `--allow-empty`, `--skip`, `--abort`, `--quit`, `--signoff`, `--empty`, `--ignore-date`, `--committer-date-is-author-date`, and passes `-C`/`-p`/`--whitespace`/`--reject` through to apply. Hooks and rerere are not run.
  - `git log`'s email format takes format-patch's mail settings (`LogOutputOptions.mail`). Diffs can show binary files as `GIT binary patch` hunks (`binaryPatch`, git's `--binary`), deflated and delta-encoded as git does. `appendSignoff` takes git's dedup flag, and `patchIdOfChanges` computes the patch-id of a change list that holds renames.
- 68ef504: **Faster commit-graph writing, fsck and path-limited log; `fetch.writeCommitGraph`; `git log -- <path>` as git walks it, with changed-path Bloom filters.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** `writeCommitGraphFile` computes changed-path filters over the trees' raw bytes (entries the same on both sides skipped after one byte comparison, no entry decoded, only differing subtrees read), keeps the trees it read lately, takes a parent's tree from the commits it parsed instead of reading the parent again, reads in-memory objects without a promise, and sorts ids as strings; commit headers parse their ids and dates without allocating. The bytes written are unchanged. `WriteCommitGraphFileResult.reads` counts the commits and trees read.
  - **vcs-commands:** `LogCommand` with paths walks as `git log -- <path>` does (revision.c): newest commit first by date (ties in queue order), TREESAME commits hidden, a merge followed only to the first parent it matches, a root shown when it has the paths, `--first-parent` comparing the first parent only; before comparing trees it asks the commit-graph's changed-path filter of the commit (git's `check_maybe_different_in_bloom_filter`, all of a path's leading directories), and it compares only the entries on the paths' way, from the trees' raw bytes. `setUseBloomFilters(false)` turns the filters off; `getStatistics()` gives git's trace2 `bloom/statistics` counts and the tree work. `FetchCommand.setWriteCommitGraph()` (`--[no-]write-commit-graph`) and `fetch.writeCommitGraph` write a split commit-graph of what the refs reach after the fetch, as git's fetch does, given the repository's files (`setRepositoryFiles`, also on `PullCommand`); `FetchResult.commitGraph` lists the chain's layers. `FsckCommand --connectivity-only` takes the commits it reaches through parents from the commit-graph (git's `parse_commit_in_graph`) and walks a tree it just parsed from those bytes instead of reading it twice; `FsckResult.statistics` counts the objects read and the commits taken from the graph.
  - **vcs-commands, ranges and pathspecs:** with excluded commits (`not`, `addRange`) a path-limited `LogCommand` limits the walk as git's `limit_list` does: one date-ordered queue of included and excluded commits, UNINTERESTING spread to parents as commits are taken, a merge's parents judged relevant (not UNINTERESTING, or an excluded commit given) with what is marked so far, so a merge TREESAME only to an uninteresting side stays shown and one TREESAME to its relevant parent is hidden, `--since` turned into UNINTERESTING, the early stop of `still_interesting`, and the output filtered by the final marks. Paths are git pathspecs: wildcards and `:(glob)`/`:(literal)`/`:(icase)`/`:(exclude)` magic match as `tree_entry_interesting` does in a pruned tree diff, `excludePath` is an `:(exclude)` element folded into TREESAME, and exclusions, icase and pathspecs without a literal leading directory leave the Bloom filters out as git does. A filter of another hash version than `commitGraph.changedPathsVersion` counts as not present (git's `get_bloom_filter`); a missing tree or parent fails with git's `unable to read tree (<id>)` / `cannot simplify commit <id> (because of <id>)`.
  - **vcs-core:** the commit-graph writer reads a streamed tree (a loose tree over 64 KB) once, to the end of its content, instead of reading it again and leaving the first read open; its tree cache (and `LogCommand`'s) is bounded by bytes as well as by count.
- 979230c: **git's proc-receive hook and report-status-v2.**
  
  - receive-pack hands the commands `receive.procReceiveRefs` matches (`[a][d][m][!]:<prefix>`, as git's `proc_receive_ref_matches`) to the proc-receive hook instead of updating them: after pre-receive and the publication of the pushed objects, over git's proc-receive protocol (`version=1` with the `atomic` and `push-options` features, the commands, the push options the hook asks for), reading its `ok`/`ng` report and its `option refname|old-oid|new-oid|forced-update|fall-through` lines with git's errors for a broken hook (`cannot find hook 'proc-receive'`, `proc-receive version 'N' is not supported`, `fail to negotiate version with proc-receive hook`, `proc-receive reported status on unknown ref`, `proc-receive failed to report status`, ...). Its commands skip the update hook and the ref transaction; an atomic push fails as git's does (`fail to run proc-receive hook`, the transaction's error given to the hook's commands too); post-receive is told each report. Wired on the local push, the served (HTTP, git://, duplex) receive path and `remoteHookSession` (found as `find_hook` finds it: no configured hook). `ReceivePackHooks.procReceive`, `ReceivePackConfig.procReceiveRefs`, `runProcReceive`, `parseProcReceiveRef`, `procReceiveRefMatches` are exported; `serverSettingsFromConfig` reads `receive.procReceiveRefs`.
  - **Behaviour change:** receive-pack advertises `report-status-v2`, as git does, and answers in it when the client asks (the hook's rewrites as `option` lines); report-status otherwise.
  - send-pack asks for `report-status-v2` when offered and keeps the server's rewrites (`PushRefUpdate.reports`, `RemoteRefUpdate.reports`): the push report prints one line per rewrite as git's `print_ref_status` (` * [new reference]   HEAD -> refs/pull/123/head`), the remote-tracking refs follow each, and `PushResult.porcelain` (`formatPushStatus(..., { porcelain: true })`) gives `git push --porcelain`'s stdout.
  - `HookRunner.start` (vcs-working-tree) starts a hook with its stdin and stdout piped while it runs; `createNodeHookRunner` implements it, and `HookSession.start` (vcs-commands) uses it.
- 979230c: **proc-receive: the review's fixes.**
  
  - `PushCommand` checks the remote-tracking refs a server's report names before writing them, as git's `update_one_tracking_ref` / `refs_update_ref`: an `option new-oid` of an object the client does not have, or an `option refname` no ref may have (`refs/heads/a..b`, `refs/heads/../../x`), is refused with git's error line (`error: update_ref failed for ref '<ref>': trying to write ref '<ref>' with nonexistent object <id>` (2.43: `cannot update ref '<ref>': ...`), `refusing to update ref with bad name '<ref>'`; a deletion `error: refusing to update ref with bad name '<ref>'`) in `PushResult.errors`; the push still succeeds, as git's exits 0.
  - **Behaviour change:** every ref store's `set` refuses a name git's ref transaction refuses (`check_refname_format` with one level allowed): the files store wrote such names as loose files (one outside the refs for `..` components), memory, KV and SQL stores kept them.
  - send-pack fails a push whose report has an `option` line without its `ok` (`error: 'option' without a matching 'ok/ng' directive`, `PushClientResult.reportFailed`, `PushResult.failed`, no porcelain `Done`) and warns of reports on refs it did not send (`warning: remote reported status on unknown|unexpected ref: <ref>`), as git's `receive_status`.
  - receive-pack's `hookTimeoutMs` covers the proc-receive hook: past it the hook is stopped (`ProcReceiveProcess.kill`, `HookProcess.kill`, the Node runner kills the child) and the commands it did not report fail `fail to run proc-receive hook` (every command of an atomic push). A proc-receive `ng <ref> ` keeps its empty reason, so the client shows `[remote rejected] ... ()` as with git.
  - proc-receive's stderr reaches the pushing user as the hook writes it (`HookRunner.start(invocation, { onStderr })`, git's `copy_to_sideband`) instead of being kept whole until it exits.
  - `receive.procReceiveRefs` without a value makes receive-pack die before its advertisement with git's lines (`error: missing value for 'receive.procreceiverefs'`, `fatal: bad config variable ... in file 'config' at line N`; `ServerConfigSettings.receivePackConfigError`, `ReceivePackConfig.configError`; HTTP answers 500).
- 4170514: **Interactive rebase as git 2.5x runs it.**
  
  ```typescript
  const result = await git
    .rebase()
    .runInteractively({
      editTodo: (todo) => todo.replace(/^pick (\w+) # two$/m, "edit $1 # two"),
      modifyCommitMessage: (message) => message,
    })
    .setExecHandler((command) => runInShell(command))
    .setUpstream(mainId)
    .call();
  // result.status === RebaseStatus.EDIT: "Stopped at <abbrev>...  # two"
  ```
  
  The interactive handler now plays git's editors: `editTodo` gets the todo list as git's
  sequence editor shows it (abbreviated ids, rebase.abbreviateCommands, the help text), and
  `modifyCommitMessage` gets what git's message editor would (a reword's message, a squash
  chain's `# This is a combination of N commits.` text). The edited list is parsed and checked
  as git does (invalid lines, merges where a pick cannot take one, rebase.missingCommitsCheck,
  HEAD left at upstream for `--edit-todo`, the new `RebaseOperation.EDIT_TODO`).
  
  All todo commands run as git's sequencer runs them: `edit` stops with git's state
  (`stopped-sha`, `amend`, `message`, `author-script`, REBASE_HEAD) and `--continue` amends
  staged changes or takes a `commit --amend` made meanwhile; `reword` amends after the pick;
  `squash`, `fixup`, `fixup -C`, `fixup -c` assemble the message in `message-squash`,
  `message-fixup` and `current-fixups` exactly as git (a failed fixup stops for amending,
  `--skip` takes it off the chain); `exec` runs through the injectable `setExecHandler`
  (`Executing: <command>`, failures and changes left in the worktree stop the rebase,
  `--reschedule-failed-exec` puts the command back); `break`, `drop`, `noop` and
  `update-ref` work, with `setUpdateRefs` (rebase.updateRefs) adding the `update-ref` lines,
  the `update-refs` state file and the refs moved at the end (branches checked out in other
  worktrees left alone).
  
  New options: `setExec` (`--exec`, also with `--rebase-merges`), `setEmpty` (`--empty=
  drop|keep|stop`, git 2.5x defaults: stop when interactive, keep with `--exec`, else drop;
  picks that become empty stop with git commit's advice), `setKeepEmpty` (`--[no-]keep-empty`),
  `setAutosquash` (rebase.autoSquash for interactive rebases; `fixup!`, `amend!`, `squash!`
  reordered), `setRescheduleFailedExec` and `setRoot` (`--root`, with or without an upstream
  as `--onto`; `reset [new root]` of `--rebase-merges`). Commits are recorded as rewritten
  through `rewritten-pending` as git does (fixup chains, the base of skipped picks).
  
  A handler that throws fails as git's editor does: a failing `editTodo` (or `prepareSteps`)
  ends the rebase before it starts (state removed, autostash applied), a failing
  `modifyCommitMessage` stops at the reword or squash as git commit's failure leaves it. A fixup
  or squash whose amend would leave the commit empty stops for amending, as git commit refuses
  it. The edited list's abbreviations are read back against the list's own commits, the rest
  looked up in the object store together (a prefix several objects share is refused as git
  refuses it), so `--edit-todo` and `prepareSteps` no longer read every object per name.
- 5b7852b: **`rebase --rebase-merges`, and `%(trailers)`, `%(describe)`, `%S`, `%(count)` in pretty formats.**
  
  - **Rebase:** `RebaseCommand.setRebaseMerges(true | "rebase-cousins" | "no-rebase-cousins" | false)` (default `rebase.rebaseMerges`) keeps the branch's merges as git 2.5x does. The todo list is git's `make_script_with_merges`: `label onto`, each branch picked from where it forked (`reset onto`, `reset <label>`, a cousin forked below the merge base kept on its fork point unless `rebase-cousins`), merged branches' tips labeled (by the branch pointing at them, else from the merge's message: `Merge branch 'x'`, `Merge pull request #n from x`, sanitised, numbered when taken), branch points labeled, `merge -C <merge> <label> # <oneline>`, `# Branch <label>` comments in the backup; the list written as the editing leaves it (comments dropped, blank lines kept and appended to `done`). The commands run as git's sequencer runs them: `label` (`refs/rewritten/<label>`, listed in `refs-to-delete` and deleted at the end or on abort), `reset` (`rebase (reset): '<label>'`), `merge` (merge-ort with the original message and author, `MERGE_HEAD`/`MERGE_MODE`, `rebase (merge): <subject>`; a fast-forward to the original merge when HEAD and the heads are its parents; nothing when the head is already in HEAD; a conflict stops with `Could not apply <abbrev>... <label> # <oneline>`, `--continue` commits the merge with both parents; `merge -c` re-commits the message cleaned up as an edited one). Todo files with `label`/`reset`/`merge` (and their `l`/`t`/`m` abbreviations) written by native git are resumed. An octopus (`merge -C <merge> <label> <label>...`) runs as git runs `git merge -s octopus --no-ff -F MERGE_MSG`: git-merge-octopus's messages, `ORIG_HEAD`, the reflog line `merge <heads>: Merge made by the 'octopus' strategy.`; its conflicts with the last head (`MERGE_HEAD` listing every head, `--continue` commits all the parents) or its giving up ("Should not be doing an octopus.") stop the rebase there. `reset [new root]` is refused. An unknown `rebase.rebaseMerges` mode dies with git's `fatal: Unknown rebase-merges mode: <value>`.
  - **Rebase, also without merges:** "Current branch <b> is up to date" ("HEAD is up to date" when detached) only where git's `can_fast_forward` says so: upstream in HEAD's history *and* no merge on the first-parent line since, so a branch with merges on top of its upstream is rebased (linearized, rebuilt with `--rebase-merges`); forced (date options, `--signoff`) it says "..., rebase forced." and rebases. The first picks whose parent is where the rebase starts are skipped as git's `skip_unnecessary_picks` skips them (HEAD starts at the last, they go to `done`); later ones are fast-forwarded (git's `allow_ff`, `rebase: fast-forward`) unless the dates are rewritten or the commits signed off; comment and blank lines of a todo file are kept and appended to `done`; a commit empty from the start is listed `pick <id> # <subject> # empty`; `--continue` records the stopped commit in `rewritten-list` whether or not it commits, and leaves `REBASE_HEAD` (as git) when it continued the last command; `--skip` runs as git's (sequencer_continue): the stopped commit mapped to HEAD in `rewritten-list`, `AUTO_MERGE` removed, `REBASE_HEAD` left after the last command; a pick that commits or is dropped removes `MERGE_MSG` (what a merge already in HEAD left); abort and skip remove `MERGE_HEAD`/`MERGE_MODE`.
  - **Rebase `--signoff`:** `RebaseCommand.setSignoff(true)` (kept in `rebase-merge/signoff`, implies `--force-rebase`) appends git's `Signed-off-by: <committer>` to each pick as `append_signoff` does (unless the trailer block already ends with it), cleans the message up as whitespace, and never fast-forwards; recreated merges are not signed off, as in git. `appendSignoff` is exported from the trailers module.
  - **Pretty formats:** `%(trailers[:only,unfold,keyonly,valueonly,separator=,key_value_separator=,key=])` follows git 2.5x's trailer.c (the block rules: all trailers, or a git-generated `Signed-off-by: ` / `(cherry picked from commit ` with a quarter trailers; continuation lines, comments, the scissors line, `://` not a separator, `trailer.separators`, `trailer.<name>.key`); `trailerConfigOf(workingCopy)` reads the configuration and `appendTrailers` / `formatTrailersOf` are exported. `%(describe[:tags,abbrev=,match=,exclude=])` runs git describe's search (`createDescriber`: nearest annotated tag by commit distance, lightweight with `tags`, ten candidates, misnamed tags in long form). `%S` shows `PrettyFormatOptions.source` and `%(count)`/`%(total)` `PrettyFormatOptions.count` (git 2.5x; without, git's "not supported by this command"). `rebase.instructionFormat` reads the trailer configuration and describes with the repository's tags.
- 32ab2c4: **Several refs deleted in one transaction, the ref lock timeouts from the config, `cherry-pick -e` / `revert -e`, and the root refs in the reference-transaction hook.**
  
  - New optional `Refs.removeAll(names, { legacy, message })` (`RefRemoveAllOptions`), git's `delete_refs`: `FileRefStore.removeAll` deletes all the refs in one transaction as git 2.5x does (none deleted when one cannot be), or with `legacy` as git 2.43's files backend (the refs dropped from `packed-refs` in a transaction of the packed store first, `packed-refs` rewritten even when absent, then each ref in a transaction of its own, going on past a failure). `branch -d/-D`, `tag -d`, `remote remove` and `fetch --prune` use it (legacy when the hook runner follows 2.43); `branch -d` and `tag -d` check every name first and still delete the others when some are refused, as git does; they throw the first refusal (its class as before), carrying every refusal in the new `GitApiError.refusals` and git's stderr for each in `GitApiError.messages` (`error: the branch 'x' is not fully merged` with its hint as each git version prints it, `error: branch 'x' not found`, `error: tag 'x' not found.`). The reflog of a deleted ref always goes with it.
  - The files ref store locks the refs a multi-ref batch updates in name order (then the refs that only log the update, HEAD, as a single update locks them after its ref), so two batches naming the same refs in opposite orders cannot deadlock under a negative `core.filesRefLockTimeout`; the hook still hears the batch's order, and a lock it cannot take tells it `aborted` for the packed store's transaction of the deletions git would have locked before that ref. A batch of deletions only is committed as git commits it (packed-refs rewritten only when it names one of them, then the loose files removed). A batch without deletions no longer needs `packed-refs.lock`: when another writer holds it, the batch commits its loose refs as git does instead of failing.
  - `createGitFilesBackend` reads `core.packedRefsTimeout` (default 1000 ms) and `core.filesRefLockTimeout` (default 100 ms) from the repository's config when the options are not given; a negative timeout waits forever, 0 tries once.
  - `CherryPickCommand.setEdit` / `RevertCommand.setEdit` (`-e`, `--no-edit`) with `setEditor`: each pick that applies cleanly is committed by `git commit -n -e`, as git's sequencer runs it: the message in git commit's template through the editor, `prepare-commit-msg` told `merge`, `post-commit`, no `pre-commit` or `commit-msg`, `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` set for the hooks. Either setting is saved in `sequencer/opts` (`edit = true` / `edit = false`, as git's `save_opts`) for a run of several commits and read back by `--continue` and `--skip`. `--continue` commits a resolved conflict as git's `continue_single_pick` does, through `git commit` (its `pre-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg` told `merge`, `commit-msg` and `post-commit` hooks): with the editor under `--edit`, else `--no-edit --cleanup=strip`. A cherry-pick records `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` before it commits a clean pick, as git does. `GitCommand.shareHooksOf(source, env)` takes the variables such a child run exports.
  - The files store tells its reference-transaction hook about the root refs git updates in transactions (`isRootRefName`: `ORIG_HEAD`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD`, `REBASE_HEAD`, ...; `AUTO_MERGE` for git 2.5x only), written with `set` (or a one-command `batch` with an old value) and deleted with `remove`. Commands delete them as git does: a commit, a reset and a branch switch delete `AUTO_MERGE` whether it exists or not, a cherry-pick's commit `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`; a reset (and a sequencer's `--abort` / `--skip`) writes `ORIG_HEAD` given the value it read (`reset: updating ORIG_HEAD`; through a symbolic `ORIG_HEAD` to the ref it names, the old value read through it, an `ORIG_HEAD` naming `HEAD` writing HEAD's branch, one naming a missing ref with git's `warning: ignoring dangling symref ORIG_HEAD`; the hook's update lines carry the new `RefTransactionUpdate.through` for the symbolic refs on the way, and `viaHead` for a ref HEAD names reached through another symbolic ref) and moves HEAD expecting the commit it left, reporting an `ORIG_HEAD` it cannot write (`error: update_ref failed for ref 'ORIG_HEAD': ...` in `ResetCommand.getWarnings`) and moving HEAD all the same, as git; the sequencer's commits move HEAD in one transaction expecting its parent (a clean pick writes `MERGE_MSG` with `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` before its commit, and a commit whose ref lock another process holds throws a `GitApiError` with git's `error: cannot lock ref 'HEAD': ...` and its exit status, both left as git leaves them); merge's hooks all see its `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION`.
  - Deleting a root ref (`AUTO_MERGE`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, ...) with no reference-transaction hook installed unlinks it under its own lock, without taking `packed-refs.lock`, reading `packed-refs` only when its size or modification time changed (the new optional `AtomicFiles.stats`); a root ref `packed-refs` lists is deleted from it as git does; with a hook, git's nested packed-refs transaction is still emulated for the hook's lines.
- 68c571d: **Reflog lines as native git writes them, and `git worktree` from a bare repository.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** every ref update of the porcelain writes git's reflog line, with the old and new ids, git's committer identity and git's message: `checkout: moving from main to feat` (HEAD only; a detached HEAD named by its full id), `commit: <subject>` / `commit (initial)` / `commit (amend)` / `commit (merge)` / `commit (cherry-pick)` (branch and HEAD), `reset: moving to <rev>`, `merge <names>: Fast-forward` / `Merge made by the 'ort' strategy.`, `rebase (start): checkout <onto>`, `rebase (pick)` / `(continue)`, `rebase (finish): <branch> onto <id>` and `returning to <branch>`, `rebase (abort): returning to <branch>`, `cherry-pick: <subject>`, `revert: <subject>`, `pull[ --rebase]: Fast-forward` (a `pull --rebase` that only fast-forwards merges `--ff-only --autostash`, as git; `--rebase` / `--autostash` are in the message only when given with `setRebase` / `setAutoStash`, not from the configuration), `clone: from <url>` (HEAD, the branch, `refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD`; the remote's refs unlogged), `fetch[ --atomic][ <remote> <refspecs>]: fast-forward` / `forced-update` / `storing head` / `storing tag` / `storing ref` / `updating tag` (also under `fetch --atomic`, each ref its own message), `fetch` for `refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD` set by `fetch.followRemoteHEAD`, `branch: Created from <start>` / `Reset to <start>`, `Branch: renamed a to b` (the log carried over), `update by push`, the `reset: moving to HEAD` of `stash push` and of `merge --abort`, `remote: renamed <old> to <new>` (a ref's log, the remote HEAD's included, carried over as git 2.5x does; a ref without a log gets none), and `tag: tagging <abbrev> (<subject>, <date>)` / `(tree object)` / `(blob object)` / `(other tag object)` (logged only with core.logAllRefUpdates=always). One command reads the clock once, so its lines share a time (a rebase's finish logs the branch and HEAD at one time, as git's single default date). The identity is git's `git_committer_info`: `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL/DATE` from the host environment, else `user.name` / `user.email` and the clock (a commit's committer for the commands that commit). The messages live in one place (`reflogMessages`, `committerIdent`, `parseGitDate`); `GitCommand.setReflogAction` is git's `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION`. `stash push` writes its index and untracked commits' messages with git's line end (the same stash ids as git). **`git pull` (changed):** without `setRebase`, `branch.<name>.rebase` and then `pull.rebase` decide whether a pull rebases, as in git (`isRebase()` reports only the option). A rebasing pull refuses a dirty tree before fetching with git's `cannot pull with rebase: You have unstaged changes.` (or `Your index contains uncommitted changes.`) unless it autostashes: `PullCommand.setAutoStash` is `--autostash` / `--no-autostash`, default `pull.autoStash`, then `rebase.autoStash`; the refusal is a result with `rebaseUsed: true`, `successful: false` and `rebaseResult.status` `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, and `PullResult.fetchResult` is now optional (undefined then). A rebasing pull that fast-forwards still reports `rebaseUsed: true`, with `rebaseResult.status` `FAST_FORWARD` (and the merge's `mergeResult`). `RebaseCommand.checkCleanWorkTree(action)` is git's `require_clean_work_tree`. `FetchCommand.setAtomic` is `git fetch --atomic`. `Git.fromWorktreeAdmin` runs `worktree add/list/prune/lock/unlock/remove/move/repair` from a bare repository (`list` shows it `(bare)`); commands that need a working tree refuse there with git's `this operation must be run in a work tree`.
  - **vcs-core:** `Refs.set` and `Refs.setSymbolic` take an optional reflog line (`RefLogOptions`: `message`, `ident`); `setSymbolic` with a message logs as git's `create_symref` (old resolved id to the target's id, nothing for an unborn target); with `SymbolicRefLogOptions.reflog` it writes the lines given instead (a renamed symbolic ref's log). `RefCommand.message` gives one update of a batch its own reflog message. `symrefReflogWrites`. `BatchOptions.skipReflog` writes no reflog line (git's initial transaction). `symrefReflogLine`, `logsByDefault`, `defaultReflogIdent`. `MemoryRefs` logs both.
  - **vcs-store-kv, vcs-store-sql:** `set` and `setSymbolic` write the reflog lines they are given (`set` as a one-ref batch, `setSymbolic` as create_symref), so the porcelain logs on these backends too.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the files store and a linked worktree's refs honour those reflog lines; `worktree add` logs the new worktree's HEAD by the calling repository's `core.logAllRefUpdates` default, as git 2.5x writes it from the caller (from a bare repository nothing is logged); `GitLinkedWorktrees.openAdmin` gives a bare repository's history and configuration without a working copy.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `WorktreeAdmin` and `LinkedWorktrees.openAdmin`; `GitHostEnvironment.committer` carries git's committer variables.
  - **vcs-transport:** fetch writes git's per-ref reflog messages (`reflogAction`, `reflogIdent`, `skipReflog`; `fetchReflogWhat`).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` reads `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME`, `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL` and `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`.
- 2523eff: **Repository ownership checks as git: `safe.directory`, "detected dubious ownership" and
  `safe.bareRepository`.**
  
  ```typescript
  const environment = createNodeGitHostEnvironment(); // effectiveUid: process.geteuid()
  const files = createNodeFilesApi({ rootDir: "/" });
  // git's setup discovery: refuses another user's repository unless safe.directory lists it,
  // and a bare repository found by walking up while safe.bareRepository=explicit
  const found = await discoverRepository({ files, cwd: process.cwd(), environment });
  ```
  
  `discoverRepository` (vcs-store-files) walks up from a directory as git's
  `setup_git_directory_gently` does and applies `ensure_valid_ownership` (gitfile, worktree and
  git directory owned by the current user, root's `$SUDO_UID`, `GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER`)
  and `safe.bareRepository`; an explicit `gitDir` skips both, as `$GIT_DIR` does. The
  protection is opt-in: library callers get git's local checks only by calling
  `discoverRepository`; a `FileWorktree` or `createGitFilesBackend` constructed directly is
  unchecked. The owner checks need an environment with `effectiveUid`. The protected
  configuration is read only when an owner check fails or a bare repository is found.
  `safe.directory` and `safe.bareRepository` are read only from the protected configuration
  (`readProtectedConfig`: system, global, command line; read without a git directory, so
  `includeIf.gitdir:`, `gitdir/i:` and `onbranch:` never hold there, while
  `hasconfig:remote.*.url:` matches the remote URLs of those sources, as git), with git 2.5x's matching (`*`, real
  paths, `<path>/*`, `.`, `%(prefix)/`, `~/`, an empty value resetting, the "not absolute"
  warning) or 2.43's (`compatibility: "2.43"`: literal paths). Refusals carry git's text
  (`DubiousOwnershipError`, `UnsafeBareRepositoryError`, `ProtectedConfigError`, exit 128).
  
  `Worktree.openRepository` checks the repository as git's `enter_repo` (receive-pack,
  http-backend, git-daemon) before it reads its config or alternates, unless
  `checkOwnership: false`; served refusals answer as git's (HTTP 500 as http-backend's
  `die_webcgi`, a dropped git:// connection as git daemon's dying child); `OpenedRepository.checkOwnership()`
  checks later. A local push refuses another user's repository (receive-pack's lines plus
  "Could not read from remote repository"), a local clone refuses to copy one, while local
  fetch, ls-remote and `clone --no-local` read any owner's (git 2.48+'s upload-pack).
  
  `GitHostEnvironment` gains `effectiveUid`, `sudoUid`, `assumeDifferentOwner`,
  `commandLineConfig` and `prefix`; `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` fills them from the process
  (`effectiveUid: null` turns the checks off) and parses `$GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` /
  `$GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` (`commandLineConfigOf`: what git cannot parse is git's fatal
  "unable to parse command-line config", kept as `commandLineConfigError`), and expands
  `~user/` through `expandUserHome` (`/etc/passwd` read through the FilesApi). Owners come from the FilesApi's `lstat`
  (`LinkStats.uid`); without an effective uid or owners (browser, memory, KV/SQL, Windows) the
  checks pass, as git's do without file owners.
- ba9da8b: **Unique abbreviations everywhere, revision-or-path arguments, config load checks and git's config parser.**
  
  - **vcs-commands, abbreviations:** what the sequencer, rebase and merge write abbreviates ids as git's `find_unique_abbrev` (`core.abbrev`, lengthened until unique) instead of 7 digits: cherry-pick / revert / rebase conflict labels (`<abbrev> (<subject>)`, `parent of ...`), `sequencer/todo`, `could not apply <abbrev>...`, merge-ort's single merge base label, the `Merge:` lines of `SQUASH_MSG` and checkout -m's autostash base. The sync `abbrev()` of merge-support is gone; `squashMessage` takes an abbreviator and `VirtualBaseContext` an `abbreviate`. Rebase writes its todo as git 2.5x does (`pick <id> # <subject>`) and says `Could not apply <abbrev>... # <subject>`.
  - **vcs-commands, revision or path:** `RevParseCommand.addArgument` reads arguments as `git rev-parse` does: `--` and what follows printed as given (`bad revision` before it), the first argument that is no revision printed as a path that must exist, diagnosed as git's `verify_filename` / `maybe_die_on_misspelt_object_name` do (`path 'x' does not exist in 'HEAD'`, `path 'x' exists on disk, but not in 'HEAD'`, `path 'd/x' exists, but not 'x'` with its hint, the `:path` index variants, `invalid object name 'x'.`, `x: no such path in the working tree.`). `RevListCommand.addArgument` is `git log <args>` without `--`: revisions that also name a file die with `ambiguous argument 'x': both revision and filename`, the paths go to the walk. `'x' is outside repository at '<worktree>'` names the worktree's host path. New `revision-filenames.ts` (`checkFilename`, `looksLikePathspec`, `verifyFilename`, `verifyNonFilename`, `parseRevisionAndPathArgs`), `RevisionResolver.dieOnMisspeltObjectName`, `ResolveOptions.onlyToDie`, `RevisionSources.fileExists` / `worktreeHostPath`.
  - **vcs-commands, config load:** every command checks `core.disambiguate` in `checkCallable()`, before it reads or writes anything (`InvalidConfigError` with git's `error:` / `fatal: bad config variable ... at line <n>`), as git dies on any command, `status` included (`configLoadError`): a commit, tag, merge, rebase, cherry-pick, stash, branch, reset or checkout refused this way changes no ref, index or file. Setters, which call `checkCallable()` too, throw it as well.
  - **vcs-commands, paths as git's lstat:** `check_filename` and the `<rev>:<path>` diagnoses look a path up as git's `lstat` does, through the worktree (`lstatExists`, `WorktreeLookup`): a trailing slash asks for a directory (`a.txt/` is no path, `HEAD:a.txt/` does not exist in 'HEAD'), each leading component must be a directory, a link to a directory is followed (`lnk/c.txt`), `..` steps up from where the components led (`nope/../a.txt` and `lnk/../a.txt` are read as the kernel reads them), and a path may leave the worktree and come back through its own name (`../repo/a.txt`).
  - **vcs-commands, remote names:** `RemoteAddCommand` and `CloneCommand.setRemote` refuse a name git's `valid_remote_name` refuses (`'<name>' is not a valid remote name`) before writing anything; `isValidRemoteName` is exported.
  - **vcs-store-files, config parser:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig` parses as git's `config.c`: backslash line continuations inside and outside quotes, quoted values, the escapes `\n` `\t` `\b` `\"` `\\`, comments after values, blanks kept as written (git 2.5x), several headers or a header and a variable on one line; a file git refuses fails to load with `ConfigParseError` (`bad config line <n> in file <file>`: an unknown escape, a quote open at the end of a line, a bad header such as `[ sec]` or `[sec "x" ]`, a bad variable name, `name ; comment` without `=`). Line numbers (origins, errors) follow git's count past continuation lines; a variable spanning lines is rewritten whole and every other byte is kept. New `listWithOrigin()` (`git config --list --show-origin`). Blanks are git's (space, tab, newline, carriage return; `\v` and `\f` are kept in values and refused where only a blank may stand), a value ends at a NUL as git's C string does, and a variable written before any section header is listed under its bare name (as git 2.5x lists it) while no key reaches it. `set`, `add` and `unset` validate keys as git's `git_config_parse_key` (`key does not contain a section: <key>`, `invalid key: <key>`, `invalid key (newline): <key>`, ...; a value holding NUL is refused), `renameSection` / `copySection` the new name as `section_name_is_ok` (`invalid section name: <name>`, also for a newline in the subsection), and `save()` refuses to write a file its own parser would refuse. `remote..url` is `[remote ""]`, no longer `remote.url`. `get` / `getAll` read a key through an index instead of scanning the chain. `GitStashStore` abbreviates its messages' `<abbrev>` uniquely (option `coreAbbrev`, which takes the config's value as read: `12` or `false` count as their text).
- b7426bc: **Revisions: abbreviated ids, `describe` names, ranges and revision sets, as git reads them.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** object stores look ids up by prefix. `GitObjects.listPrefix` (and `listObjectsWithPrefix`, which falls back to `list()`), `StagingBlobStore.listPrefix`, `RawStorage.keysWithPrefix` (`rawKeysWithPrefix`; the memory, compressed and composite stores implement it), `Pack.idsWithPrefix` (a binary search in the idx, as git's `find_short_packed_object`) and `PackSet.listPrefix`; quarantine views include their objects. `findAbbrevLengths` asks the store for the ids sharing the start length instead of listing every object when it can. `MINIMUM_ABBREV_LENGTH` is git's 4.
  - **vcs-store-files / vcs-store-kv / vcs-store-sql:** prefix lookups read one loose fan-out directory plus the packs' idx (files backend, `FileRawStorage`), a KV prefix scan, and an SQL key range.
  - **vcs-commands:** the revision resolver reads abbreviated ids (`get_short_oid`: 4 to 40 hex digits; the type the context needs or `core.disambiguate` picks among several objects; git's `error: short object ID ... is ambiguous` with its sorted `hint:` candidate list; `warning: refname 'x' is ambiguous.` for a ref that is also a short id), `git describe` names (`<ref>-<n>-g<abbrev>`, with git 2.5x's check of the ref and count parts (2.43 takes any `-g<abbrev>`)), and `./` / `../` paths in `<rev>:<path>` and `:<path>` relative to `RevParseCommand.setPrefix`. Commands that need a commit read abbreviations as committishes. New `git.revList()` (`RevListCommand`) lists the commits of `a..b`, `a...b`, `^a`, `x^@`, `x^!`, `x^-<n>` and `--not` (`parseRevisionArgs`, a port of `handle_revision_arg`); `revParse().addArgument()` prints arguments as `git rev-parse` does, `^<id>` lines included, and `--verify` prints a range before refusing it as git does. `RevisionError.output` holds what git printed on stdout before dying. `abbreviateId` abbreviates as git prints ids; merge's `Updating a..b` and `Created autostash:` and rebase's autostash, skipped-commit and todo-backup lines use it.
  - **vcs-commands (review fixes):** `revList()` reads its arguments as `git log` does (`--not` alone falls back to HEAD, a lone `..` is `HEAD..HEAD` as before `--`) and documents that its order is `LogCommand`'s walk, not yet git's date order. `lookup_commit_reference`'s `error: object <id> is a <type>, not a commit` is printed where git prints it: `<rev>~<n>` / `<rev>^<n>` of a tree or blob, a symmetric difference with such a side (`rev-parse` prints both sides first, as git; `log` dies with `Invalid symmetric difference expression`) and `rev-parse`'s parent shorthands; `rev-parse` without `--verify` reads an unknown argument again quietly before dying, as git's `maybe_die_on_misspelt_object_name` does. A `core.disambiguate` git does not know dies as git: `error: unknown hint type for 'core.disambiguate': <value>` (or `missing value`) and `fatal: bad config variable 'core.disambiguate' in file '<file>' at line <n>`. Commands count the objects once for the automatic abbreviation length however many ids they print (`createIdAbbreviator`, `GitCommand.abbreviateObjectId`).
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig.getAllWithOrigin(key)` gives every value with the file and line it was read from and its text as written.
- ea42e69: **git's revision syntax, `switch -m`/`checkout -m`, `checkout -b` refusing an existing branch, several `-m`, `merge --no-commit -m`.**
  
  - One resolver (`RevisionResolver`, used through `resolveRef` by checkout, switch, reset, merge, rebase, branch, diff, cherry-pick, revert and worktree add) reads git's revision syntax as `get_oid` does: `@`, `@{-N}`, `[<branch>]@{upstream}`/`@{u}` (`branch.<b>.remote`/`.merge`, the remote's fetch refspecs, `remote = .`), `[<branch>]@{push}` (`push.default`, `remote.pushDefault`, `branch.<b>.pushRemote`, push refspecs, mirror), `[<ref>]@{<n>}` and `[<ref>]@{<date>}` (git's `read_ref_at`, with its "only goes back to" warning and "only has N entries" refusal), `~`/`^`, `^{commit|tree|blob|tag|object|}`, `^{/<regex>}` and `:/<regex>` (POSIX extended, youngest reachable match first, `!-` and `!!`), `<rev>:<path>`, `:<path>` and `:<n>:<path>`. New `RevParseCommand` (`git.revParse()`, `rev-parse --verify`), `RevisionError` for git's `fatal:` lines (`no upstream configured for branch 'x'`, `HEAD does not point to a branch`), and `approxidateCareful` (git's approxidate) in the date module.
  - `switch().setMerge(true)` and `checkout().setMerge(true)`: git 2.55+'s `-m`, local changes the switch would lose are stashed, the switch made and the stash applied with the labels `<target>` / `local` / the branch left; a conflict keeps the stash entry (`autostash while switching to '<target>'`). `stashApply().setLabels({ ours, theirs, base })` is `stash apply --label-ours/--label-theirs/--label-base`.
  - `addMessage()` on `commit()`, `merge()` and `tag()`: several `-m` joined by a blank line.
  - `merge().setCommit(false)` with a message writes `MERGE_MSG` as git does (the message plus a newline).
  - Switch and checkout read a name's branch syntax as git does (`loc@{u}` is the local branch `main` when `loc` tracks it with `remote = .`; `switch @{u}` is refused as `a branch is expected, got remote branch 'origin/main'`).
  - **Behaviour change:** `checkout().setCreateBranch(true)` on an existing branch is refused as `git checkout -b` refuses it (`RefAlreadyExistsError`, `fatal: a branch named 'x' already exists`); the new `setForceCreateBranch(true)` is `checkout -B` (create, or reset the branch to the start point), which `setCreateBranch` used to do.
  - Commands that need a commit refuse a revision that names a tree, a blob or a missing object as git does (`NotACommitError`): reset (`error: object <id> is a tree, not a commit` / `fatal: Could not parse object '<rev>'.`), branch (`fatal: not a valid branch point: '<rev>'`), rebase (`fatal: invalid upstream '<rev>'`), merge (`merge: <rev> - not something we can merge`), cherry-pick and revert (`error: <rev>: can't cherry-pick a tree`), checkout and switch (`fatal: Cannot switch branch to a non-commit '<rev>'`; git 2.5x's `fatal: unable to read tree (<id>)` for a blob). They used to move the branch to the tree or blob. Reset with paths, diff and path checkout take any tree-ish.
  - `checkout -m`/`switch -m` never lose the autostash: once the changes are stashed the index and worktree are reset as `reset --hard` resets them, and whatever happens to the switch the stash is applied or stored (`Applied autostash.`, git's conflict advice, or `error: could not apply autostash; your changes are safe in the stash`). Untracked files in the way take git 2.55+'s path (stash, refuse, apply back). `checkout().setForced(true).setMerge(true)` is refused (`fatal: '-f' cannot be used with '-m'`); the base label and stash messages abbreviate as `core.abbrev` and uniqueness ask.
  - `checkout -b <branch> <start>`: a start point that names nothing is refused first (`fatal: 'nope' is not a commit and a branch 'feat' cannot be created from it`).
  - Revisions: a full 40-hex id resolves whether the object exists or not (as `rev-parse --verify`); `<rev>:<path>` matches components as git's `get_tree_entry` (`HEAD:a.txt/`, `HEAD:d//b.txt` name nothing); warnings as git 2.56 prints them: `refname 'x' is ambiguous.` (also for 40-hex refnames, with git's advice unless `advice.objectNameWarning=false`; `core.warnAmbiguousRefs`), `ignoring dangling symref <ref>`, `log for ref <ref> has gap after <date>`, `log for ref <ref> unexpectedly ended on <date>`. Names that are no valid ref names are not looked up as refs.
  - `approxidateCareful` keeps now's summer-time flag as git's `mktime` does (dates across a change differ by an hour, as in git), and dates written with numbers refuse the future relative to the given now.
  - Several `-m`: an empty one adds no blank line (`-m "" -m b` is `b`).
- d676bf4: **upload-pack serves partial clone filters as git does.**
