  - **vcs-core:** `parseObjectFilter` parses `--filter` specs as git's list-objects-filter-options.c (`blob:none`, `blob:limit=<n>[kmg]`, `tree:<depth>`, `sparse:oid=<blob-ish>`, `object:type=<type>`, percent-encoded `combine:`), refusing with git's messages (`ObjectFilterError`; 2.43's where they differ). `listObjects({ filter })` and `ExportOptions.filter` omit what the filter omits: through the bitmap for the filters git's bitmaps apply (`canFilterBitmap`: blob:none, blob:limit, tree:0, object:type, combine of those; `bitmapWalk({ objectFilter })`), else in a walk in git's order whose filters (`startObjectFilter`) answer as list-objects-filter.c's, the order-dependent `tree:<depth>`, `sparse:oid` and `combine:` included; the wants and what their tags peel to are always sent.
  - **vcs-transport:** `uploadpack.allowFilter` advertises `filter` (v0) and `fetch=... filter` (v2); `uploadpackfilter.allow`, `uploadpackfilter.<choice>.allow` and `uploadpackfilter.tree.maxDepth` refuse with git's `ERR` lines (`UploadPackFilterPolicy`, read by `explicitServerSettingsFromConfig`, `serveOverDuplex({ uploadPackFilter })`, the smart-HTTP servers). The facade resolves `sparse:oid` names (an id, a ref, `<rev>:<path>`) and fails the pack as pack-objects dies (`PackObjectsError`). The fetch client asks for the `filter` capability in v0 and, from a server that takes none, drops the filter with git's warning (`onWarning`) and checks the fetch as a full one. The v0 server no longer reads past the wants' flush for a filter.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `sparseFilterMatcher` matches a `sparse:oid` blob's patterns as git's `path_matches_pattern_list` (non-cone).
  - **vcs-commands:** served repositories (`servedRepositoryOf`, the local transport) apply their filter configuration and parse `sparse:oid` patterns.
  - **Hardening and partial clones:** the filtered and plain object walks run on explicit stacks (linear at any depth) and enforce git's `core.maxTreeDepth` (default 2048; `TreeDepthError`, served as pack-objects' `fatal: exceeded maximum allowed tree depth`), `sparse:oid` blobs over 100 MiB are refused with git's warning, names resolve as get_oid does (abbreviated and upper-case ids, `~`/`^` suffixes), an unreadable `uploadpack.allowFilter`/`uploadpackfilter.*` value refuses to serve (`UploadPackFilterPolicy.configError`), and v0 checks the filter line as it reads it; on the client a filtered pack is kept with a `.promisor` file (`markPromisorPack`), the new tips are walked allowing only the objects the filter leaves out (`filterPromises`: a server that sends no trees for `blob:none` is refused with rev-list's `bad tree object`), and `CloneCommand.setFilter` records `remote.<name>.promisor`/`partialclonefilter` with repository format 1 as git's partial_clone_register (absent objects are not fetched lazily yet).
- e8828f6: **`git sparse-checkout`, and index-writing commands that honour the sparse checkout.**
  
  - New `SparseCheckoutCommand` (`git.sparseCheckout()`): `init`, `set`, `add`, `list`, `reapply`, `disable` and `check-rules`, from git's command line (`setArguments`) or typed setters. Cone-mode pattern files are written byte for byte as git's `write_cone_to_file` writes them, non-cone patterns verbatim; `core.sparseCheckout` and `core.sparseCheckoutCone` go to `config.worktree` with `extensions.worktreeConfig` set; directories are checked as git's `sanitize_paths` checks them; messages and exit statuses are git's. The sparse index is not supported (`--sparse-index` is refused).
  - Applying the patterns (git's `update_sparsity`): entries going out of the checkout become skip-worktree and their files are removed with the directories left empty, a changed file stays with git's "not up to date" warning, entries coming back are written.
  - `checkout`/`switch`, `reset --hard/--merge/--keep`, `merge`, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `rebase`, `am` and `clone --sparse` (new `CloneCommand.setSparse`) apply the patterns as unpack-trees does, in its order: entries that stay outside (skip-worktree ones, new ones) are neither checked nor written, so an untracked, ignored or changed file at such a path is kept; files leaving the patterns are checked against the index as the command found it (racily clean changes included) before anything is written. A conflicted path's file is written whatever the patterns say where its entry was outside the checkout (skip-worktree, or new), as merge-ort's `record_conflicted_index_entries` does; one whose entry was in the checkout and now leaves it is unlinked as any leaving file (its content is in the index stages). An ignored file where an entry comes back into the patterns is overwritten (a directory holding untracked files keeps it out, silently, as unpack-trees' ERROR_NOT_UPTODATE_DIR); a directory where a leaving file cannot be unlinked prints git's "unable to unlink" warning. The warnings are on the results (`CheckoutResult.warnings`, `MergeResult.warnings`, `CherryPickResult.warnings`, `RevertResult.warnings`, `ResetCommand.getWarnings()`).
  - In cone mode `sparse-checkout set/add/reapply/init` remove the directories outside the cone left with ignored files only and warn about those holding untracked files (git's `clean_tracked_sparse_directories`); a file reached through a symlinked directory is kept with the "not up to date" warning, never removed.
  - `sparse-checkout set/add/reapply` (`update_sparsity`) never treat an ignored file as expendable: one where an entry comes back is kept and reported, a directory holding one keeps the entry out.
  - The ignore rules read a `.gitignore` missing from the worktree from the index when its entry is skip-worktree, as git's `add_patterns` does (`read_skip_worktree_file_from_index`), for status, add, clean and every walk alike: `clean_tracked_sparse_directories` keeps a file such a `.gitignore` un-ignores (new `Worktree.setSkipWorktreeIgnoreReader`, installed by working copies).
  - `reset --hard` and `checkout -f` record the stat of the files they write (git's `check_updates`), so `diff-files` and a following sparse update find them clean; `reset --hard` removes the directories its deletions leave empty, as git's `unlink_entry` does.
  - `rm` matches git's pathspecs (`rm --cached -r .` removes everything) and checks them as `cmd_rm` does before anything changes: a pattern naming a directory needs `-r` (new `RmCommand.setRecursive`; "fatal: not removing 'd' recursively without -r"), one matching nothing dies ("fatal: pathspec 'x' did not match any files") unless `setIgnoreUnmatch(true)`; with 2.43 compatibility an element that named an entry exactly matches no later entry (`rm 'b*'` removes only `b*`).
  - `rm` without `--force` refuses, before anything changes, to drop content only the index holds, as git's `check_local_mod` does (new `RmCommand.setForce`): with `--cached` an entry whose staged content differs from both its file and HEAD (an intent-to-add one excepted); without it also staged changes against HEAD and local modifications, with git's messages (singular or plural, each with its hint unless `advice.rmHints` is off). Without `--cached` the files are removed too and the directories they leave empty pruned (git's `remove_path`); a directory standing at a file stops the first removal with "fatal: git rm: 'f': Is a directory".
  - A stopped merge's autostash (`MERGE_AUTOSTASH`) is kept on `refs/stash` with git's "Autostash exists; creating a new stash entry." lines by an "Already up to date." merge, the new `merge --quit` (`MergeOperation.QUIT`), `reset`, a branch switch and `am` (git's `remove_merge_branch_state`); the lines are in `MergeResult.output`, `ResetCommand.getWarnings()` and `CheckoutResult.warnings`.
  - A refused merge's restore (`restore_state`'s `read-tree --reset -u` and `stash apply --index`) removes a directory standing at any tracked file, a nested repository included, before writing the file (a symlink to a directory is replaced as a file), so a staged change there is restored instead of failing with "would lose untracked files"; a file that cannot be written is reported, never thrown.
  - A conflict whose stage-0 entry stays skip-worktree (a rebase or merge pick the two trees agree on, a delete on their side) has its file written, as `record_conflicted_index_entries` checks it out; `stash apply` honours the sparse checkout as unpack-trees does (files leaving the patterns unlinked, skip-worktree flags set, `StashApplyResult.warnings`).
  - A local change is seen through a symbolically linked directory (`verify_uptodate`'s `lstat` follows it), so `checkout`/`reset --merge` refuse to switch such a file.
  - A cherry-pick or revert that commits removes `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and `MERGE_MSG` (git's `do_commit`), a stopped empty pick's included; an "Already up to date." merge removes `AUTO_MERGE` and the other merge state (`remove_merge_branch_state`); a refused merge whose saved changes equal HEAD (a tracked file replaced by a directory, which `stash create` passes over) records no `AUTO_MERGE`, and its reset writes the file over that directory as git's does.
  - `clearSkipWorktreeFromPresentFiles` stats in batches, a missing sparse directory costing one stat.
  - `diff` reads skip-worktree entries whose files are present as in the checkout (without writing the index); `rm` refuses paths outside the patterns with git's advice (`RmFailedError`) unless `setSparse(true)`.
  - `parseSparseCheckoutPatterns` reports git's warnings for patterns cone mode cannot read (`warnings`), and gives the cone's hashsets (`coneSets`) and the lines read; new `sparsePatternsFromLines`, `sparsePatternsFromCone`, `splitPatternLines`.
  - `GitWorkingCopyConfig.set`/`add` write the variable name as spelled (`sparseCheckout`), as git does.
  - `rm` passes over every skip-worktree entry unless `setSparse(true)`, with or without `core.sparseCheckout`, giving git's "outside of your sparse-checkout definition" advice as `cmd_rm` does; a path removed loses every stage (an unmerged one's kept as resolve-undo, git's `remove_file_from_index`).
  - `rm` refuses a populated submodule whose own worktree holds modified, staged or untracked files (ignored ones aside) with "the following file has local modifications" (git's `bad_to_remove_submodule`); a clean one's directory is removed where its repository lives in the superproject (a gitfile), its `.gitmodules` section removed and the file staged again (`remove_path_from_gitmodules`, `stage_updated_gitmodules`), and a `.gitmodules` holding unstaged changes stops it ("please stage your changes to .gitmodules or stash them to proceed"). A submodule with its own `.git` directory keeps its directory (git absorbs the repository first).
  - `rm` sees a path reached through a symbolically linked leading directory as `lstat` does: a file there is a local modification, a directory stops the removal ("Is a directory"); nothing is ever removed through the link. A removal failing after another one succeeded is passed over (`RmResult.warnings`) and the index written, as git does.
  - `stash create` (and so a refused merge's `save_state`) takes an assume-unchanged entry from the index without looking at its path, a path beyond a symbolic link included, so such a merge returns FAILED and resets instead of throwing.
- f62ca9b: Read and write git's split index (`link`) and sparse index (`sdir`) as git does. A split index is merged with its `sharedindex.<id>` on read and written split again (`core.splitIndex`, a new shared index past `splitIndex.maxPercentChange`, old ones removed per `splitIndex.sharedIndexExpire`, modes per `core.sharedRepository`), the same edits giving the index and shared index bytes native git writes; `core.splitIndex=false` writes it whole. A sparse index is expanded on read and collapsed on write when `index.sparse`, cone mode and the entries allow it (`convert_to_sparse`), with its `TREE` and `sdir` extensions. `sparse-checkout --sparse-index` now records `index.sparse=true` and converts the index (`--no-sparse-index` expands it, with git 2.5x's `advice.sparseIndexExpanded` hint), writing `config.worktree` only once the worktree config is on. Stagings take an `IndexHost` (`setIndexHost`), report `indexLayout()` and can `holdWrites()`; gc and fsck read split and sparse indexes (`readIndexFileEntries`). Other required extensions are still refused. A sparse directory is only ever written as the tree its entries make (an intent-to-add entry below keeps it expanded, a stale cache-tree is computed again), `core.splitIndex=true` on a sparse index fails with git's `cannot use split index with a sparse index`, and a linked worktree's sparse index is read with the repository's trees.
- f0d8ddd: **`stash push -- <pathspec>` fails as git does after saving; `DA` for intent-to-add over HEAD; unreadable-directory and sparse-checkout messages; skip-worktree entries present in the worktree and the sparse-checkout patterns.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `stash push -- <pathspec>` reproduces git's failures after the stash is saved (`StashPathspecResetError`, carrying `stashCommit`): `git apply --index -R` refusing a path HEAD has, the index does not and the worktree holds untracked ("error: b.txt: already exists in working directory", the index updated by git's `add -u`, the worktree untouched); with `-u`/`-a`, git's `git add -- <pathspec>` dying on an element that matches nothing or naming ignored files (the others added); with `--keep-index`, `git checkout` naming an element the index tree lacks (the paths reset, the index tree not checked out); git's `git apply` of an empty patch, when the add brings the named paths back to HEAD (`git rm --cached c.txt; git stash push -u -k -- '*.txt'`: "error: No valid patches in input (allow with "--allow-empty")", c.txt re-added and kept, no `--keep-index` checkout). Pathspec errors name the elements as git hands them on: `:(prefix:0)x` (git 2.5x) or `:(,prefix:0)x` (`setCompatibility("2.43")`), also in the "did not match any file(s) known to git" check; `prefixOriginElement` spells them.
  - **vcs-commands:** status shows an intent-to-add entry over a path of HEAD as git does: `DA` (`DD` once the file is gone), with HEAD's side in porcelain v2, `removed` listing it, and a rename from it keeping only its worktree side.
  - **vcs-commands:** status, add and clean report git's "warning: could not open directory 'u/': Permission denied" for directories the scan cannot list (`Status.warnings`, `AddResult.warnings`, the new `CleanResult.warnings`); a real clean removes an empty unreadable directory (rmdir) and warns "failed to remove u: <reason>" for the others, the reason rmdir's ("Directory not empty", "Permission denied", ...).
  - **vcs-commands:** `add` (also `--chmod`, `--refresh`, `-u`, `--dry-run`) advises as git does on pathspec elements that match only skip-worktree entries ("The following paths and/or pathspecs matched paths that exist outside of your sparse-checkout definition…", hint off with `advice.updateSparsePath=false`), writes the index and rejects with `AddFailedError` (git's exit status 1). `AddCommand.setCompatibility("2.43")` words the advice's last hint as 2.43 does ("git config advice.<name> false"). With `core.sparseCheckout`, add asks the sparse-checkout patterns too (git's `path_in_sparse_checkout`, cone and non-cone): tracked paths outside them are not updated, untracked files outside them are advised on instead of added; the index is written only when the add changed it (git's `SKIP_IF_UNCHANGED`). An embedded repository is asked and advised on as the directory `sub/`, as git does. `add --chmod` on an entry already of that mode still writes the index and invalidates the entry's cache-tree path (git's `chmod_index_entry`).
  - **vcs-commands:** `stash push -- <pathspec>` without `-u` stops as git does when its `git add -u` fails after the stash is saved (an entry outside the sparse checkout: git's advice, the worktree untouched); the add words its hints for the stash's `setCompatibility`. `StashCreateCommand.setQuiet(true)` (`git stash push -q`) leaves git's "Cannot save the current worktree state" line out of the error.
  - **vcs-commands:** in a sparse checkout, every index-reading command (those that re-read the index at their start, and status) clears the skip-worktree flag of entries whose files are present in the worktree, as git's `clear_skip_worktree_from_present_files` (unless `sparse.expectFilesOutsideOfPatterns`), conflict stages included: `add --refresh d/b` of a recreated `d/b` refreshes it and exits 0; `rm` writes the cleared flags with its removal.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `clearSkipWorktreeFromPresentFiles` (one stat per missing directory, as git's `path_found`), `parseSparseCheckoutPatterns` (`path_in_sparse_checkout`: a trailing `/` asks for a directory; `ignoreCase` folds ASCII case as `core.ignoreCase`; lines read as git's `add_patterns_from_buffer`, so a line of spaces is an empty pattern that turns cone mode off; repeated cone directories counted as git's hashmap does), the optional `Staging.clearSkipWorktree` (flags cleared as part of the read: not an edit, written by the next `write()` or `refreshStats()`); an index editor finished without edits changes nothing. `WorktreeWalkOptions.onUnreadable` receives the listing's error; `findUntrackedFiles` takes `onUnreadable`; `unreadableDirectoryWarning` words git's warning.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree.getEntries` without `lstatMany` keeps at most 48 `getEntry` calls in flight (slow asynchronous FilesApis); and stops taking paths once one fails; the staging editor's in-place merge falls back to the full sort when a path's stages are out of order (a malformed index). `GitLinkedWorktrees.pathState` takes a directory it cannot list as not empty (git's `is_empty_dir`), and `deleteAdminRootIfEmpty` ignores a failed removal (git's `delete_worktrees_dir_if_empty`).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `list()` of a directory that cannot be read (EACCES) now throws, as `listKinds` does, instead of listing nothing; a missing directory still lists nothing.
  - **vcs-utils:** the `FilesApi.list` contract documents that an unreadable directory fails the listing.
- 0b1ccae: **Racily clean entries stay protected after a commit shows its false index; `status -v`/`-vv` and the commit template follow git more closely; ahead/behind counts stop at the merge base.**
  
  - `Staging.saveState()` / `restoreState()` (new, optional; `GitStaging` and `FileStagingStore` implement them) put a staging back exactly: the same entry objects, the entries kept as read, the pending cache-tree and untracked cache. `CommitCommand` uses them where it showed a partial commit's false index in the editor's template or the dry run, and where a failed commit puts back unwritten edits, so the entries read from the index are still smudged when racily clean (before, an editor `--only` commit made `git status` miss a change). The index builders of both stagings keep a copy of an entry kept as read as read (git copies the entry with its flags). New `IndexBuilder.finish({ upToDate: true })` counts every entry the builder took as set in this session instead; the merge family's index rewrite (merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase, read-tree, apply) uses it, so a merge over a racily clean index again reads only the paths it writes, not every file.
  - `status -vv` (and `commit --dry-run -vv`, the `-vv` template) print the unstaged diff only when a path that is not unmerged changed (`wt_status_check_worktree_changes`), and diff a conflicted path as `diff-files` does (`* Unmerged path`, then its stage 2): new `DiffCommand.setCombineMerges(false)`. The verbose diffs apply `submodule.<name>.ignore` and `diff.ignoreSubmodules` as git does, and show `-dirty` only for a submodule at its entry's commit (new `DiffCommand.setDirtySubmodules(false)`); `git diff` now honours `submodule.<name>.ignore` too.
  - `CommitCommand.callDryRun()` takes the index lock as git's `prepare_index` does: an `index.lock` another process holds fails with `IndexLockFailedError` (exit code 128, git's "Unable to create '…/index.lock': File exists." message) and is left in place. A dry run of an `--only` commit stores no tree for the false index.
  - `CommitCommand.setTemplate()` (`-t`, default `commit.template`) starts the editor from a template file read through the host; an untouched template aborts with "Aborting commit; you did not edit the message.". `COMMIT_EDITMSG` is written before an empty commit is refused, as git writes it.
  - `countAheadBehind` (vcs-core) and the optional `Commits.countAheadBehind` count `rev-list --left-right --count ours...theirs` with one walk that stops at the merge bases (commit-graph generations order it where there is one); `git status`'s tracking report uses it instead of collecting every ancestor of both sides.
- 0b1ccae: **`git status` takes full pathspecs and prints every text format; `git commit` gets `--dry-run` and git's editor template.**
  
  - `StatusCommand.addPath` takes pathspec elements as `git status -- <pathspec>` does (pathspec.c): wildcards (`fnmatch` without `WM_PATHNAME`), `:(glob)`, `:(literal)`, `:(icase)`, `:(exclude)` / `:!` / `:^` (an implicit `.` when every element excludes), `:/` and `:(prefix:N)`, and the `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_NOGLOB_PATHSPECS` and `GIT_ICASE_PATHSPECS` variables of the host environment (new `GitHostEnvironment.pathspecs`). Tracked paths match as `ce_path_match` matches them (a gitlink as a directory); a path below a submodule no longer selects the submodule. `:(attr:...)` is refused with git's "pathspec magic not supported by this command". Bad elements fail with git's messages (`InvalidArgumentError`).
  - `scanUntracked` accepts any pathspec through the new `matcher` option (`UntrackedScanPathspec`): dir.c's `common_prefix_len`, `treat_leading_path`, `simplify_away` and `match_pathspec_item` with its wildcard and leading-pathspec branches. The untracked cache stays unused whenever a pathspec is given.
  - `StatusCommand.callText()` prints the status as `git status` does: the long format (branch line, tracking report with `status.compareBranches`, merge / am / rebase / interactive rebase / cherry-pick / revert / bisect / sparse checkout lines, section headers, `advice.statusHints`, `status.displayCommentPrefix`, the closing line, `--show-stash`, `-v` / `-vv`), `short`, `porcelain` and `porcelain-v2`, with `--branch` and `-z`, and the deferred `status.short`, `status.branch`, `status.aheadBehind` and `status.showStash`. `formatStatusPorcelain` takes `z`. New `setReferenceCommit` and `setWriteIndex`.
  - `CommitCommand.callDryRun()` is `git commit --dry-run` (and `--short`, `--porcelain`, `--long`, `-z`, `--branch`, `-u<mode>`, `-v`) with `-a`, paths and `--amend`, returning git's exit code (1 when nothing is committable).
  - `CommitCommand.setEditor()` runs an editor over git's commit template written to `COMMIT_EDITMSG`: the message so far, the commented help for the cleanup mode, the merge / cherry-pick notice, `Author:` / `Date:` / `Committer:` lines, the commented status (`commit.status`, `--no-status`) and with `-v` the diff below the scissors line, with `core.commentChar` / `core.commentString` (`auto` too). The edited message is cleaned up as git cleans it (`default` strips comments, `scissors` cuts at the scissors line). New `setEdit`, `setIncludeStatus`, `setVerbose`, `setUntrackedFiles`, `setStatusFormat`, `setShowBranch`, `setNullTermination` and `setAheadBehind`.
- 8a603c0: **Status detects renames and reads submodules as git; `clean -X`; racy smudging on every staging store.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (behaviour change):** `git.status()` detects renames as git's default `status.renames=true`: a path removed from the index paired with one added to it is one entry `R  old -> new` (porcelain v2 `2 R. ... R<score> new<TAB>old`), a path deleted from the worktree paired with an intent-to-add file is ` R old -> new`; untracked files are never paired. Scores and pairing follow git's `diffcore_rename` (exact renames, unique basenames at 75%, then the best pairs at 50%, `diff.renameLimit`/`status.renameLimit`, default 1000). `status.renames` (else `diff.renames`) and new `setRenames(boolean)` (`--[no-]renames`) and `setRenameLimit(n)` control it. New `Status.renamed` (new path to old path), `StatusFileEntry.renamedFrom`/`renameScore`/`intentToAdd`, status code `R`. The paths stay in `added`/`removed`/`missing` too.
  - **vcs-commands (behaviour change):** a populated submodule's own index and worktree are read (as git's `is_submodule_modified`): tracked changes inside show ` M sub` with v2 `S.M.`, untracked files `S..U`, with a new commit `SCMU`; nested submodules count as git counts them. `diff.ignoreSubmodules`, `submodule.<name>.ignore` (config, then `.gitmodules`) and new `setIgnoreSubmodules("none" | "untracked" | "dirty" | "all")` (`--ignore-submodules`) behave as git; `setUntracked(false)` also stops looking for untracked files inside submodules. New `StatusFileEntry.submoduleModified`/`submoduleUntracked`, exported `submoduleToken()`.
  - **vcs-commands:** new `CleanCommand.setIgnoredOnly(true)` is `git clean -X`: only ignored files go (with `-d`, ignored directories and directories holding only ignored content, as git; path filters as git's).
  - **vcs-commands:** stash apply's rename detection uses the same git-exact detection (`detectRenames`, git's similarity estimate).
  - **vcs-store-files:** an absolute submodule gitdir (`gitdir: /abs/.git/modules/sub`, older git, `--separate-git-dir`) inside the FilesApi's root directory is read (status sees its commit and content; `clean -d` removes a directory whose absolute gitfile names a place without HEAD); outside the root it stays unreadable (shown clean, kept by clean). New `FileWorktree.openGitlink(path)` opens a populated submodule as a working copy through the same FilesApi.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** new optional `Worktree.openGitlink(path)`.
  - **vcs-utils:** new optional `PosixFilesApi.rootDirectory` (the real directory a local FilesApi is rooted at), which `PosixNodeFilesApi` already exposes.
  - **vcs-store-mem, vcs-store-kv, vcs-store-sql:** the staging stores implement `setRacyCheck`: every write (editor, builder, `write()`) smudges racily clean entries that changed (size 0) against the time of the store's previous write, as `GitStaging`/`FileStagingStore` do. The KV and SQL stores keep that time with their entries (`staging:__meta__`; new SQL migration 8, `staging_meta`) and `read()` restores it.
- 7a834b2: **`git status` untracked and ignored modes, with the untracked cache in normal mode.**
  
  - **Behaviour change:** `StatusCommand` now lists untracked files in git's default `normal` mode: an untracked directory shows as `dir/` (porcelain `?? dir/`) and one holding nothing untracked is not shown. Callers that need every untracked file ask for `setUntrackedFiles("all")` (the old default); `setUntracked(true)` keeps meaning `all` and is deprecated.
  - `setUntrackedFiles("no" | "normal" | "all")` and `status.showUntrackedFiles`, read as git 2.5x reads them (`true`/`yes`/`on`/`1` or a variable without a value are `normal`; `false`/`off`/`0`/an empty value are `no`); a value git cannot read fails with git's `Invalid untracked files mode` message (`InvalidConfigError`). `parseUntrackedFilesMode` and `parseIgnoredMode` are exported.
  - `setIgnored("no" | "traditional" | "matching")` (a boolean still works: `true` is `traditional`): ignored paths as git's `DIR_SHOW_IGNORED_TOO` (an ignored directory as `!! dir/`) and `DIR_SHOW_IGNORED_TOO_MODE_MATCHING` find them, in every untracked mode; `matching` with `-uno` fails with git's "Unsupported combination of ignored and untracked-files arguments" (`IncompatibleOptionsError`).
  - `scanUntracked` (vcs-working-tree) is a port of dir.c's `read_directory` with `treat_directory`'s flags (`DIR_SHOW_OTHER_DIRECTORIES`, `DIR_HIDE_EMPTY_DIRECTORIES`, `DIR_SHOW_IGNORED_TOO`, `DIR_SHOW_IGNORED_TOO_MODE_MATCHING`), its `check_only` walks and a literal pathspec (`treat_leading_path`, `simplify_away`), and returns the ignored paths too. The pathspec given to `StatusCommand.addPath` now prunes the untracked scan as git's does.
  - The index's untracked cache (`UNTR`) is used and written in normal mode (dir flags 6) as git does, `check_only` directories included; a cache of another mode is replaced unless it is the configured mode's (`new_untracked_cache_flags`). The bytes equal native git's on 2.43 and 2.5x.
  - `walkUntrackedScanHost` runs the scan on a worktree without a scan host of its own (in memory).
  - `git rm` of a submodule runs its inner status with `-uall`, as git's `bad_to_remove_submodule`: an untracked file in the submodule refuses the removal whatever the submodule's `status.showUntrackedFiles` says. The submodule state status reads (`S..U`, `-dirty` in diffs) runs in the submodule's configured mode (`-uno` where untracked content is ignored), and `worktree remove` checks the worktree in its own mode, as git's child processes do. A `status.showUntrackedFiles` the inner status cannot read fails each of them with git's messages (`could not run 'git status' in submodule`, `'git status --porcelain=2' failed in submodule`, `failed to run 'git status' on`), nothing removed.
  - `walkUntrackedScanHost` works out the directories from the files' paths when the worktree's walk yields no directory entries.
  - The untracked scan reads every directory in readdir order, as git: its check-only walks open the directories git opens, and warn about the unreadable ones git warns about, in git's order.
  - `status -- f.txt/` matches no tracked file `f.txt` (an element ending in `/` names a directory), nor does `a/b` match a file `a`, as git's pathspec matching.
  - `FileWorktree` decides nested repositories as git's `treat_directory`: a `.git` must name a repository (`is_nonbare_repository_dir`: a directory with a valid HEAD, objects and refs, or a gitfile or link naming one), and one resolving to the superproject's own git directory is none. An empty `.git` directory or a gitfile of garbage no longer hides a directory's files from `status`, `add`, `stash -u` and `checkout`; `clean` and `stash -u` remove such a `.git` with its directory, and `clean` still skips a directory whose `.git` links to the superproject's ("Would skip repository"). A `.git` naming a place beyond the FilesApi's root still counts as a repository (it cannot be checked).
- 21b0bd6: **Submodule summaries as git shows them: `diff --submodule`, `status.submoduleSummary` and `git submodule summary`.**
  
  - `DiffCommand.setSubmoduleFormat("short" | "log" | "diff")` (and the `submoduleFormat` text option) shows a submodule's change as git's submodule.c does: `log` prints `Submodule <path> <a>..<b>:` (`...` when neither side is an ancestor of the other, ` (rewind)` when the new side is older, or "(new submodule)", "(submodule deleted)", "(commits not present)") and the commits of a first-parent left/right walk inside the submodule as `  < subject` / `  > subject` lines, after "contains modified content" / "contains untracked content" for a dirty one; `diff` prints the diff of the submodule's own files under `a/<path>/` and `b/<path>/` (its worktree when it holds modified content). Without the option `diff.submodule` decides, else `short`. It applies to `git diff`, `--cached`, `log -p`/`show`, `status -v` and the `commit -v` template; `format-patch` and the stash patches keep `short`, as git does.
  - `DiffCommand.setIgnoreSubmodules(mode)` overrides `diff.ignoreSubmodules` and `submodule.<name>.ignore`.
  - `SubmoduleSummaryCommand` (`git.submoduleSummary()`) is `git submodule summary`: `setCached`, `setFiles`, `setForStatus`, `setSummaryLimit` (`-n`), `setCommit` and `addPath`, with git's counts, `Warn: <path> doesn't contain commit` lines, and the `(blob)->(submodule)` / `(submodule)->(blob)` type changes.
  - The long status (and the commit template) print "Submodule changes to be committed:" and "Submodules changed but not updated:" when `status.submoduleSummary` is true or a limit (`StatusCommand.setSubmoduleSummary` overrides it); a value that is neither a boolean nor a number fails as git fails.
  - `Worktree.openGitlink(path, { name })` takes the submodule's name and falls back to the repository git keeps in `<gitdir>/modules/<name>` when the path holds none (a submodule not checked out, or whose directory is gone). Without `name` it opens what it opened before: the repository at the path, never a `.git` that holds none.
  - `diff.submodule` is read as git reads it: the last value it knows wins, each other value warns (`DiffCommand.warnings`: "warning: Unknown value for 'diff.submodule' config variable: '<value>'"), and a variable without a value fails with git 2.5x's "missing value" error.
  - A submodule whose ancestry misses a commit gets the "(corrupt repository)" header, as git 2.45 and later print it.
  - `git diff <commit>` (a tree against the worktree) reports a submodule checked out at another commit than its index entry even when that commit is the tree's (`M sub`, git's null id), with git's `--stat` for it, and no longer pairs such a submodule as a rename; `git submodule summary` uses the same comparison.
  - `commit --only <submodule>` (and its dry run) commits the commit checked out in the submodule instead of refusing it as a directory.
- b31fadc: **Fetch and push summaries as git prints them, `rebase.instructionFormat`, grafts and replace refs in history walks.**
  
  - **vcs-transport:** the ids of fetch and push summaries (`a..b`, `+ a...b`) are abbreviated as git's find_unique_abbrev under `core.abbrev` (new `abbrev` option of fetch and push; `RepositoryFacade.abbrevLengths`, implemented by `createRepositoryFacade`), no longer cut to 7 digits. Fetch and push results carry `abbrevLengths` and `summaryWidth` (git's `transport_summary_width`). `formatPushStatus` renders git's push report (`To <url>`, ` * [new branch]  main -> main`, ` ! [rejected]  main -> main (fetch first)`, `Everything up-to-date`); `fetchUpdateDisplay`, `formatFetchDisplay` and `fetchRefcolWidth` render git's fetch lines (full and compact formats). A forced tag update's summary is now `[tag update]` (git's `t` line), and a new ref is named after the remote ref (`[new tag]` for a remote tag), as git does. `FetchClientResult.backfilledTags` lists the tags found in the fetched history.
  - **vcs-commands:** `FetchResult.display` and `PushResult.display` hold the stderr lines git prints for the refs. `rebase.instructionFormat` formats the todo lines (and the `Could not apply` line) through git's pretty format, `# ` put before a format that does not start with `#` (git 2.45+); the todo's text is kept as written when read back. `formatCommitPretty` is git's user format (`%H %h %T %t %P %p`, `%an %ae %al %ad %aD %ar %at %ai %aI %as` and the committer's, `%s %f %b %B %e`, `%n %% %x..`, colors, `%+ %- % ` magic). Log, rev-list, revision parents (`HEAD~2`) and merge bases apply replace refs (`refs/replace/<id>`, `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`) and grafts (`info/grafts`), as git's parse_commit does; `setUseReplaceRefs(false)` is `--no-replace-objects`, and `core.useReplaceRefs=false` and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS` turn them off. `fsck` walks grafted parents (a parent grafted away is dangling; `bad graft data` and `bad graft parent` errors as git's).
  - **vcs-core:** `loadObjectReplacements`, `createReplacedCommits`, `parseGraftFile` and `replacementOf`; `Refs.readGrafts` (optional) reads `info/grafts`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the file ref store and linked worktrees' refs read `info/grafts`.
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-utils-node:** `GitHostEnvironment.noReplaceObjects` and `replaceRefBase`, read from `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS` and `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`.
  - **Replace refs and grafts in every porcelain read:** commands read commits, trees, blobs and annotated tags through replace refs and grafts as git does (merge bases and fast-forward decisions of merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, pull; the trees checkout, switch, reset, status, diff and merge read; `branch --contains`/`--merged`; commit parent checks), while fsck and gc read the objects as stored (`GitCommand.readsReplacedObjects`). vcs-core adds `createReplacedTrees`, `createReplacedBlobs`, `createReplacedTags` and `overlayMethods`; a replace ref's object is the last component of its name (`refs/replace/foo/<id>`), an empty `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE` makes every ref a candidate, `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` names the grafts file (`GitHostEnvironment.graftFile`), and a shallow commit has no parents even when `info/grafts` gives it some. `replacementMessages()` returns what git prints while reading them (`bad replace ref name`, the grafts advice, `bad graft data`); rev-parse puts them in its warnings.
  - **gc keeps what grafts reach:** gc, repack and `GcOrchestrator` walk a grafted commit's grafted parents as well as its recorded ones (`listObjects` and `collectReachableObjects` take `grafts`; `readGraftParents`), as git's `repack --keep-true-parents` and prune together keep both; before, an old history reachable only through `info/grafts` was deleted.
  - **Fetch:** when a ref update is refused (non-fast-forward, a tag it would clobber) every line is printed with a summary width of 0, as git 2.5x does; a ref the store refuses to write keeps its summary with `!` and `(unable to update local ref)` (`FetchRefUpdate.storeError` holds the store's reason); a fetch into a branch a worktree has checked out dies before fetching (`FetchIntoCheckedOutBranchError`, `fatal: refusing to fetch into branch '<ref>' checked out at '<path>'`) unless `setUpdateHeadOk(true)` (which pull passes); a refspec destination outside `refs/` is a branch (`main:side` writes `refs/heads/side`).
  - **Push:** the report names the source as written (`HEAD~1 -> y`, `HEAD -> z`, an object id; `PushRefUpdate.source`).
  - **Pretty formats:** `%d`, `%D` and `%(decorate:…)` (`loadRefDecorations`, with git log's default filter or the sequencer's none), `%m`, `%ah`, `%G?` and the other `%G` parts, `%N`, `%gd %gs %gn…`, padding and truncation (`%<(N)`, `%>(N)`, `%><(N)`, `%>>(N)`, `|` columns, `trunc`/`ltrunc`/`mtrunc`), `%w(…)` wrapping, `%C(always,…)` and colors when asked; `%x80`–`%xff` write raw bytes (the byte strings' U+DC80–U+DCFF); git's ASCII-only isspace (a no-break space is never trimmed, in subjects and in `info/grafts`).
  - **Replaced objects in the remaining porcelain reads:** `worktree add` checks out a replaced HEAD's replacement tree and blobs (and prints its subject), so status agrees with native git; `log -- <path>` compares replaced trees; fetch and `clone --branch <tag>` peel tags through replace refs.
  - **gc and `GIT_GRAFT_FILE`:** gc, repack and `GcOrchestrator` keep what the grafts of `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` reach (new `readGraftFile` option of `gc()`, `repack()`, `GcRunOptions` and `ReachabilityRoots`; `gc()` takes it from its `environment`; `createGitFilesBackend({ environment })` makes `refs.readGrafts` read that file; vcs-core `graftFileReader(environment)`, and `readGraftParents` takes a reader). A shallow commit's graft is ignored (the shallow file overrides it), and a grafted parent that is missing aborts gc with nothing pruned, as native gc dies ("Could not read <id>").
  - **Fetch into a checked-out branch:** the check covers git's ref map only (the refs the refspecs took and their opportunistic updates): with `remote.origin.fetch=+refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*`, `fetch origin feature` on `main` updates `refs/heads/feature` instead of refusing for `main`. `FetchClientOptions.beforeFetch` (vcs-transport) receives the ref map.
  - **Pretty formats:** truncation keeps zero-width characters (combining marks) attached as git's strbuf_utf8_replace does, and widths follow git's unicode-width tables (vcs-utils `gitCharWidth`); `%w` counts a control character as -1 column as git does. `loadRefDecorations` reports `warning: invalid replace ref <name>` (`onWarning`) for a replace ref whose name is not an object id below the base; rebase prints it.
- 38bc5ac: **`git switch`, `checkout --detach` and `@{-N}`, `branch -c`, git's message cleanup, fetch and pull reflog actions with their options.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:**
    - New `SwitchCommand` (`git.switch()`), with `git switch`'s rules and messages. It switches to `<branch>` or to `-`, creates a branch with `-c`/`-C <new> [<start>]` (`setCreateBranch`, `setForceCreateBranch`, `setStartPoint`), detaches with `--detach [<commit>]`, and starts an empty unborn branch with `--orphan <new>`. It supports `--guess`/`--no-guess` (a name only a remote-tracking branch has creates the local branch tracking it; `checkout.defaultRemote` picks among several remotes), `--track`/`--track=inherit`/`--no-track` (`TrackMode`; any of them without `-c` names the new branch after the remote-tracking one, as git does), `--discard-changes` (untracked files still protected), `-f` and `--ignore-other-worktrees`. `SwitchRefusedError` carries git's refusals: `fatal: a branch is expected, got tag 'v1'` (also `commit`, `remote branch`) with git's hint, `invalid reference`, `a branch named 'x' already exists`, `cannot switch branch while merging` (also while rebasing, cherry-picking or reverting), and the option conflicts. `--merge` is not supported.
    - `CheckoutCommand` gains `setDetach` (`checkout --detach [<commit>]`, logged `checkout: moving from main to HEAD`), `-` and `@{-N}` as names and as start points (the reflog lines, `branch: Created from feat` included, name the branch they stand for, and that branch is tracked), `setTrack` and `setGuess`. Only the name as given is guessed into a remote-tracking branch, so `-` naming a deleted branch is refused. `setName("HEAD")` without `setDetach` leaves HEAD as it is. A branch it creates now tracks a remote-tracking start point as `branch.autoSetupMerge` says, and is created after the worktree and the index are switched, as git does it; `--track` from a start point that is no branch is refused before the branch is made. Names resolve as revisions (`HEAD~1`, a peeled annotated tag, `origin/dev`).
    - Rev parsing (`resolveRef`) understands `@{-N}`: it reads HEAD's reflog for `checkout: moving from` lines. It also understands `refs/remotes/<name>` and `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD`.
    - New `CopyBranchCommand` (`git.branchCopy()`, `branch -c`/`-C`): it copies the branch, its reflog (with git's `Branch: copied refs/heads/a to refs/heads/b` line) and its `branch.<name>.*` section. HEAD stays where it is. `branch -m` now renames the branch's configuration section, and `branch -d` drops it, as git does.
    - Messages follow git's `-m`. `commit` ends the last line, then cleans up as `whitespace` (`commit.cleanup` and the new `setCleanupMode(CleanupMode.*)` choose otherwise). `merge` cleans up the same way without ending the line first. `tag` uses `strip` by default (comment lines go, `core.commentChar`), adds no newline in `verbatim` mode, refuses `CleanupMode.DEFAULT` by name as git does, and `setMessage("")` alone makes an annotated tag. The comment character is the last of `core.commentChar`/`core.commentString` set; for a commit, `auto` picks one no line of the message starts with. A commit of a prepared `MERGE_MSG` honours `commit.cleanup` and the comment character. A merge whose message cleans up to nothing is refused (`Empty commit message.`), the merge state left for `commit`. `--amend` without a message cleans up the amended message again. A commit whose message cleans up to nothing is refused (`Aborting commit due to empty commit message.`) unless `setAllowEmptyMessage(true)`. `stripspace` (git's `strbuf_stripspace`) is exported.
    - Fetch reflog lines carry the options git's argv would: `fetch --atomic --force --prune --prune-tags --tags --depth=<n> --unshallow --append --no-write-fetch-head --refmap=<spec> <remote> <refspec>...`. Pull lines add `--ff`/`--no-ff`/`--ff-only`, `--strategy=<s>` and `--tags`/`--no-tags`.
    - **Behaviour change:** a commit or tag made with a message that has no trailing newline now stores one, as native git does, so its id changes. Callers that compared `commit.message` with the string they passed now see `"<message>\n"`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig.copySection(old, new)`, git's `git_config_copy_section`: the copy goes right after the section, with its lines kept (a variable on the header line is not copied). `renameSection` and `unsetSection` edit the file's lines as git's `config.c` does: headers are matched as written, a rename onto an existing section keeps both in file order, and a removed section's comments and blank lines go with it. New `getLastOf(keys)`: the value set last among several keys.
- 5558be8: **Pluggable transports: remote URLs resolved as git resolves them, and reached through a registry of connectors.**
  
  - New `connect/` module in vcs-transport: `parseGitUrl` reads a remote URL as git's `transport_get` and connect.c's `parse_connect_url` do (scp-like `host:path`, `[ipv6]:path`, `ssh://host:22/~user/x`, `file://`, `<helper>::<address>`), returning a `GitUrl`. A `GitTransportConnector` turns a `GitConnectRequest` into a `GitSession` (a native duplex, with or without git-daemon's request line, or a smart-HTTP endpoint behind a `fetch` function); `GitTransportRegistry` picks one per URL, `createDefaultTransports()` registers smart HTTP, and `openUploadPack` / `openReceivePack` open the pack protocol over a session.
  - Breaking: the JGit-style URL parser of `utils/` is now exported as `parseGitUri` (type `GitUri`); `parseGitUrl` and `GitUrl` name the new parser.
  - `encodeGitProtocolRequest` writes extra parameters after a second NUL, as git does.
  - vcs-commands: fetch, pull, push, clone, ls-remote and `remote set-head -a` reach non-local remotes through the registry: `TransportCommand.setTransports(registry)` or `Git.fromWorkingCopy(workingCopy, { transports })` (default: smart HTTP, as before). Paths and `file://` URLs stay git's local transport.
  - `url.<base>.insteadOf` and `url.<base>.pushInsteadOf` rewrite remote URLs as git's remote.c does (longest match, push rewrites only for remotes without `pushurl`, URLs given directly included); an empty `remote.<name>.url` clears the earlier ones (git 2.5x).
  - `protocol.allow`, `protocol.<name>.allow`, `GIT_PROTOCOL_FROM_USER` and `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` are honoured with git's defaults and errors ("fatal: transport 'ext' not allowed"); `protocol.version` is read (default 2), the local transport included.
  - A remote name is told from a URL as git's `remote_get` does (a configured remote wins), so a remote whose name holds `@` works.
  - New `git.remoteGetUrl()` (`git remote get-url [--push] [--all]`) and `LsRemoteCommand.callGetUrl()` (`git ls-remote --get-url`).
  - `GitHostEnvironment` gains `protocolFromUser` and `allowProtocol`, read by `createNodeGitHostEnvironment` from the environment.
  - Remote URLs that look like options are refused as git refuses them before running a command: "fatal: strange pathname '-r' blocked" (ssh and local paths), then, after `protocol.ssh.allow`, "fatal: strange hostname '-oProxyCommand=x' blocked" (ssh). `parseGitUrl` follows `get_host_and_port` (the first colon past the host starts the port, read as `strtol` reads it; `[user@host:22]:repo`; `host:/~user/x` asks for `~user/x`; `host:` names the empty path), keeps `%00` as written and stops at a NUL; `gitDaemonRequest` refuses a NUL in the host, path or extra parameters.
  - A connector is never asked for protocol v2 for receive-pack (`protocolVersionFor`, as git_connect); `protocol.version=1` is spoken: the client sends `version=1` (git-daemon parameter, `Git-Protocol` header on every smart-HTTP request) and reads the server's `version 1` line, and our servers answer it (`serveOverDuplex({ announceVersion1 })`, the smart-HTTP handler, the local transport).
  - `openDuplexConnection` takes `timeout` and `signal` (as `openDuplexReceivePackConnection`), passed from the request by `openUploadPack`; a native session's stream is closed once.
  - `createHttpConnector` serves `https::<url>` with the helper's address as its URL; `ls-remote` lists over the session as fetch does (ls-refs over v2).
  - `ls-remote` without a remote picks git's default remote (`branch.<current>.remote`, the only remote, else `origin`), else "fatal: No remote configured to list refs from.".
  - `clone` tells a path from a URL first, as clone.c: an existing repository path is recorded absolute (`remote.origin.url`, the reflog) and `insteadOf` applies to that absolute path; a name without a colon that is no repository path is "repository '<name>' does not exist", not rewritten.
  - `RemoteConfigStore.read()` (and `remoteList()`) report URLs as `git remote -v` does: `insteadOf`/`pushInsteadOf` applied, an empty `url` clearing the earlier ones.
- 819e88c: **`git worktree` as git does it: add, list, prune, lock, unlock, remove, move, repair.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `git.worktreeAdd()` (`-b`/`-B`, `--detach`, `--force` once or twice, `--lock --reason`, `--orphan`, `--no-checkout`, the branch named after the path, `--orphan` inferred in an empty repository; checks the files and index out as git's `reset --hard`, with ORIG_HEAD and git's reflog lines), `worktreeList()` (default, `-v`, `--porcelain`, `-z`, `--expire`; byte for byte as git 2.53+, `setLegacyColumns` for older git), `worktreePrune()` (`-n`, `-v`, `--expire`, duplicates), `worktreeLock()` / `worktreeUnlock()`, `worktreeRemove()` (refuses dirty, untracked, locked and submodule worktrees unless forced), `worktreeMove()` and `worktreeRepair()` (a worktree beyond the storage's root is never pruned, and `remove`/`move` refuse it), with git's refusals and messages (`WorktreeError`). A backend without linked worktrees gives `WorktreesUnsupportedError`. Other commands respect worktrees: `branch -d/-D` and `branch -f` refuse a branch another worktree uses (`BranchUsedByWorktreeError`, git's message), `branch -m` moves other worktrees' HEAD along (through their own refs, with git's `Branch: renamed` reflog line) and refuses a branch being rebased or bisected elsewhere, `checkout` (also `checkout -B`, as git 2.44+) refuses a branch checked out elsewhere unless `setIgnoreOtherWorktrees(true)`, and `gc` prunes gone worktrees after `gc.worktreePruneExpire` (default `3.months.ago`; `GCCommandResult.worktreesPruned`). Clone's checkout is shared as `checkoutFreshTree`.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `LinkedWorktrees` (the storage side of `git worktree`) and `WorkingCopy.worktrees` (optional; `MemoryWorkingCopy` takes it as an option). `LinkedWorktrees.reachable` and `updateHead`. `AddWorktreeOptions.ident` sets the reflog identity.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `openGitLinkedWorktrees` keeps worktrees as git's `worktree.c` does (`should_prune_worktree`, `validate_worktree`, `find_worktree`, repair) and opens a working copy of any of them (`openCurrent`, `open`; refused for a bare repository, and for an unlisted linked worktree unless `workTree` names its directory). A FilesApi rooted at `/` maps worktrees; a worktree outside the root (or written through a symbolic link's real path) counts as present. `createWorktreeRefs` gives a linked worktree its own HEAD, pseudo refs, `refs/bisect|worktree|rewritten/` and HEAD reflog, the branches shared, so commands run in a linked worktree move its HEAD, not the main one's; a `noDeref` update of its HEAD logs the branch's id as old value (`resolveOutside`), and a linked worktree of a bare repository logs as git does (core.logAllRefUpdates defaults to true there). `sanitizeRefnameComponent` and `HostPaths` are exported. `GitWorkingCopyFactory.addWorktree` names the admin directory as git's `sanitize_refname_component` does and writes the HEAD reflog line git writes on add (unless core.logAllRefUpdates is false); with `GitWorkingCopyContext.files` its working copy is the new worktree's own and the factory's working copies get `worktrees`.
- 0798366: **A faster worktree stat pass; `add --chmod` / `add --refresh`; `stash push -- <pathspec>` and `--staged`.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `PosixFilesApi` gains two optional members: `lstatMany(paths)`, which lstats many paths in one call (a path beneath a link, or with nothing there, answers undefined), and `listKinds(path)`, which lists a directory with each entry's kind from `readdir` (git's `d_type`) and no stat, throwing for a directory it cannot read.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** the Node FilesApi implements both with synchronous calls (`lstatSync`, `readdirSync`; names read as strings, re-read as bytes when one is not UTF-8), and joins plain paths without `path.resolve`.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Worktree.getEntries(paths)` (optional) and `getWorktreeEntries`; `withWorktreeEntries` stats index entries a chunk at a time (git's `preload_index`), and `compareWithWorktree` takes such a prefetched entry. `WorktreeWalkOptions.skipFile` passes files over before any stat or ignore check (the untracked scan passes the index's paths, as git's `treat_path`). Status, `diffIndexWithWorktree` and the stash scan use them. `StashTreesSource.pathFilter` limits the worktree and untracked trees and the check for changes to a pathspec.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree` implements `getEntries` (one `lstatMany`, the ignore flag worked out when asked) and walks with `listKinds`, statting only the entries it yields. The staging editor applies a few edits to a large index in place instead of regrouping and sorting every entry.
  - **vcs-commands:** status, diff (worktree), `add -u` / `-A` / `.`, `commit -a` and stash stat the index's files in one batch; status takes HEAD's directories whose cache-tree node matches as the index's without reading them (git's `cache_tree_matches_traversal`); `add` passes a tracked file whose stat matches before `verify_path`, as git's `add_to_index`; diff of the worktree leaves unchanged entries out before pairing. On a 50k-file repository status, `add -u` and `commit -a` after one change run about twice to four times as fast.
  - **vcs-commands:** `AddCommand.setChmod("+x" | "-x")` (`git add --chmod`, git's `chmod_pathspec` and its "cannot chmod" errors; `chmodIndexEntries` for an `update-index --chmod`), `setRefresh(true)` (`git add --refresh`: fresh stat only, "needs merge" lines, porcelain lines with `setVerbose(true)`, "pathspec did not match any files"). `StashCreateCommand.addPath()` stashes and resets only the matching paths (`git stash push -- <pathspec>`: no ORIG_HEAD, git's "did not match any file(s) known to git" check without `-u`) and `setStaged(true)` stashes the staged changes alone (`git stash push --staged`: the staged diff applied in reverse to the worktree, the index reset; `NoStagedChangesError`, `StashWorktreeRemovalError`).

### Patch Changes

- a8277da: **`git add` handles linked worktrees, files turned repositories, symbolic links and `--renormalize` as git does.**
  
  - A linked worktree made inside the repository (`git worktree add`, absolute gitdir, branches in the main repository's `commondir`) is staged as a gitlink with git's warning, so `add .`, `-A` and globs no longer fail on it. A directory whose `.git` names a place the worktree's storage cannot reach is skipped with a warning instead of failing the whole add.
  - A tracked file replaced by a repository without a commit fails as git fails ("'a/' does not have a commit checked out", "fatal: adding files failed"), leaving the index as it was; it used to drop the file from the index. Replaced by a repository with a commit, it is staged once as a gitlink, with git's embedded-repository warning.
  - A directory whose `.git` holds no repository (an invalid HEAD, a gitfile naming nothing) is an ordinary directory: its files are added and the tracked file it replaced removed. The worktree checks HEAD as git's `validate_headref` does.
  - A pathspec element reaching through a symbolic link fails with "fatal: pathspec 'x' is beyond a symbolic link", and one ending in "/" names a directory only, before anything changes.
  - `--renormalize` needs a pathspec unless `-u`/`-A` is given ("Nothing specified, nothing added."), reports `add '<path>'` for every file it adds again, honours `--ignore-missing`, stages a file turned repository as its gitlink (or reports it), and fails with git's "fatal: unable to stat" on a missing tracked file.
  - A tracked file replaced by a FIFO, socket or device fails `add -u`, `add .` and `commit -a` with git's "can only add regular files, symbolic links or git-directories" instead of being removed (new optional `Worktree.isSpecialFile`).
  - `AddFailedError.output` keeps the `--dry-run` / `--verbose` lines printed before a failure; `--dry-run --ignore-missing` reports a missing element under an ignored directory as given (`Worktree.isIgnored` takes an optional `asDirectory`).
  - `encodeIndexFileBytes` and `encodeIndexFile` hand out buffers of their own, so changing them cannot reach a later encode.
- a8277da: **`git add` finds nested repositories as git's `is_git_directory` does, and reports failures with what git printed before them.**
  
  - A tracked file replaced by a repository whose HEAD is a symbolic link (`core.preferSymlinkRefs`) or whose `.git` is a link to a repository is staged as a gitlink by `add -u`, `-A` and `.`; `add -u` used to drop the file silently, and `-A` / `.` failed.
  - A `.git` (directory, gitfile or link) names a repository only when its common directory (`commondir`, relative or absolute) has `objects` and `refs`: a gitfile naming a directory with only a HEAD, a linked worktree whose commondir is gone, or a link to a plain directory is no repository, so the files inside are added. An empty `commondir` fails with git's "fatal: failed to read <gitdir>/commondir". A drive-letter commondir counts as absolute only on Windows.
  - New optional `Worktree.gitlinkHeadState` (and the `GitlinkHeadState` type) tells "no commit" (the tracked file is removed, as git's `check_removed`) from "cannot be read here" (kept, with a warning).
  - `add -n .` reports a tracked file turned repository twice (`add 'a'`, `add 'a/'`), as git does.
  - A path git's `verify_path` refuses (a `.GIT` component) fails with "error: invalid path", "error: unable to add … to index" and "fatal: adding files failed", and `--ignore-errors` goes on (exit status 1); `--dry-run` lists it.
  - `--renormalize` keeps the errors printed before its "fatal: unable to stat", and `AddFailedError` has a new `warnings` field with the warnings printed before the failure.
- d333a4d: **`git am --abort` keeps staged work, and more of `apply`, `am` and `format-patch` as git.**
  
  - `git am --abort` cleans the index as git's `clean_index` does. Only conflicted entries go back to HEAD; staged changes and staged deletions stay as they are. The index then moves to ORIG_HEAD with git's up-to-date and untracked checks, so a staged change with unstaged edits on top fails with "Entry 'a.txt' not uptodate. Cannot merge." and "failed to clean index" instead of losing the edits.
  - `git am -3` builds a pure rename's fake ancestor with a regular file's mode (`create_ce_mode`), so only the paths that really differ are listed.
  - `git am` converts mails to `i18n.commitEncoding` ("cannot convert from koi8-r to ISO-8859-1" where they do not fit) and writes the `encoding` header into the commit. Reflog subjects keep their bytes.
  - Charsets convert as strictly as glibc's iconv: bytes windows-125x leaves undefined, and bytes outside the GB2312, Shift_JIS and Big5 ranges, fail to convert.
  - `git am` refuses a single StGit series with git's "could not open '<dir>/<patch>' for reading" message. A vertical tab or form feed does not make a mailbox blank.
  - `git apply --whitespace=fix` keeps the bytes of postimage lines cut at the end of a file, as git 2.5x does. Deleting a path below a regular file reports "Not a directory".
  - `git format-patch` writes the cover letter's shortlog in the log output encoding, and reads `format.from` as a boolean or an ident (`git_parse_maybe_bool`).
- d333a4d: **`git apply`, `git am` and `git format-patch` closer to git.**
  
  - `git apply` refuses an index that would hold a path as both a file and a directory ("'a.txt/x' appears as both a file and as a directory"). It reads the old file keeping CRLF when the patch context has CRLF, and renormalises it otherwise. It applies submodule patches as git does: a directory stages as a gitlink, checking a gitlink out makes its empty directory, and removing one removes that directory when empty. Errors and warnings go through git's `vreportf` (control characters shown as `?`, cut at 4096 bytes). Bad binary hunks report zlib's and base85's errors. A repeated `--whitespace` keeps `error-all`'s full count. `--cached --reject` writes `.rej` files. Write errors use the system's wording. Many hunks apply in linear time: 10,000 hunks on a 200,000-line file take about 0.4 s, against 5 s for native git.
  - `ContentConversionOptions.keepCrlf` skips the line-ending step of a clean conversion (git's `CONV_EOL_KEEP_CRLF`).
  - `git am` detects the patch format of a named mailbox (`addMbox(bytes, name)`), and `setPatchFormat` sets it: StGit and Mercurial patches are converted, and "Patch format detection failed." and empty mailboxes fail as in git. Author names and emails lose their crud. `--ignore-date` and mails without a `Date:` use the clock (`setClock`), not `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE`. 8-bit commit text that is not UTF-8 is read as Latin-1, with git's warning. Charsets convert strictly ("cannot convert from X to UTF-8"; ISO-8859-1 is Latin-1). The `--allow-empty` advice shows on `--continue`. `--abort` cleans the index as git does and fails with "failed to clean index".
  - `git format-patch` ignores `diff.noprefix` and honours `format.noprefix`. `format.encodeEmailHeaders=false` covers the `From:` name too. Mails are written in `i18n.logOutputEncoding`/`i18n.commitEncoding`, converting to UTF-8 or ISO-8859-1. The cover letter's shortlog is in UTF-8, and with `--from` it takes that ident dated at the epoch, as git 2.54 and later do.
- d5158af: **Faster `git blame`, with the same output.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `BlameCommand` splits, hashes and numbers the lines of each version of the file once, through one `LineInterner` for the whole walk, instead of again in every diff; lines in the tail a diff trims (`trim_common_tail`) are not read at all, and a blob read as one chunk is not copied. The algorithm, its blame entries and tie-breaks are git's as before. On a 1000-commit history of a 3000-line file blame takes about 40% of the time it took (about 1.6× native git, from 3.7×). The interner's memory is bounded: past 64 MiB of distinct lines (`BlameCommand.setInternerByteLimit`) blame starts a new interner, and a version from the old one is interned again when compared with one from the new.
  - **vcs-utils:** `LineInterner` numbers distinct lines (under xdiff's whitespace flags) across diffs (`byteSize` tells how many bytes of lines it holds), and `InternedText` reads a file's lines on demand; `XdiffOptions.interner` lets xdiff classify lines by those numbers (the same classes, numbered alike), and `xdiffHunks` takes either side as a `LineSource` (`ByteLines` or `InternedText`). `ByteLines.prefix` gives the lines of a prefix sharing the line table and hashes. xdiff's line classification uses a typed hash table, and `trimCommonTail` compares four bytes at a time. The pooled inflater resets a state without allocating new code tables (pako's reset allocated two per object), which makes reading small objects such as commits and trees about 20% faster.
- d84c354: **A staged change carried over by a merge, checkout or pick is no longer lost by the next commit.**
  
  A fast-forward `merge` or `merge --squash` (and every operation that switches the index with git's two-way unpack-trees: picks, rebases, `am`) wrote the index's cache-tree from the target tree. A staged change on a path the two trees agree on stays in the index, so the cache-tree claimed directories the index does not hold, and the next commit (or native `git write-tree`) took the target's tree there: the staged change was dropped, or the commit refused as empty.
  
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `invalidateCacheTreeAgainstIndex` invalidates in a cache-tree computed from a tree every path where the index differs from that tree (an entry kept, added or removed), reading only the directories the index's previous cache-tree does not vouch for. The stagings' `writeTree` reuses a valid cache-tree node only when it counts the entries under it and its tree is stored (git's `update_one`), else it computes it again.
  - **vcs-commands:** the two-way switch derives the cache-tree it writes from the index it leaves.
  - **vcs-core:** a tree entry with an empty mode or another character than an octal digit fails to read with git's "malformed mode in tree entry" (`decodeTreeBytes`, `TreeEntryCursor`), instead of reading as a gitlink.
  - **vcs-store-sql:** the native tree store gives a tree's stored bytes (`raw`, modes as written), and `exportToGit` copies a tree through them, so a tree holding a 100664 blob keeps its id; a tree that would be written under another id fails loudly.
- d84c354: **Tree entry modes read as git reads them (`canon_mode`), everywhere.**
  
  A tree that stores a blob as 100664 (older git wrote it) now reads as 100644, as git's `decode_tree_entry` reads it, so a repository with such trees behaves as with native git: `status` is clean after `reset --hard`, and `merge`, `checkout`, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `rebase` (patch-ids), `stash`, `reset`, `diff` and `log` no longer see a mode change between 100664 and 100644, or refuse with "local changes would be overwritten".
  
  - **vcs-core:** the tree store's `load`, `entries` and `getEntry` give canonical modes (`canonTreeMode`: a regular file 100644 or 100755 by its owner's execute bit, a symlink 120000, a directory 40000, anything else a gitlink); `raw` still gives the stored bytes, and serializing a stored tree (`SerializationApi`) copies them. `decodeTreeBytes(bytes, { canonicalModes: true })` reads canonical modes (without the option, modes as stored), and `treeModesRewritten(entries)` says whether such a list came from a tree written with other modes (encoding it again makes another tree). fsck keeps reading the modes as stored (`badFilemode` for 100664 under `--strict`).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** the files and git stagings' `writeTree` reuses the directories the index's cache-tree has valid, as git's write-tree does (a tree primed by a reset keeps its stored 100664 modes). `updateCacheTree` computes a directory as the canonical tree its entries make: where the stored tree holds other modes, that tree is valid only if stored (git's `WRITE_TREE_REPAIR`), else invalid.
  - **vcs-commands:** `reset --hard` primes the cache-tree with the target tree (`prime_cache_tree`) and `reset --mixed` keeps it with the changed paths invalid; `stash apply` invalidates the paths it merges. A combined diff's `-U0` hunk header prints a count below zero as git's `%lu` does (`+1,18446744073709551615`), in `diff` and `log --cc`.
  - **vcs-store-sql**, **vcs-transport-adapters:** syncing a tree to the SQL store and an object's size read the stored bytes; the native SQL tree store reads canonical modes.
- 8a603c0: **`clean` reads the worktree as git's directory scan does: no data loss on path filters, ignored nested repositories or nested negations.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fixes):** `clean` is now a port of git's `read_directory` (with the flags `git clean` sets) and `correct_untracked_entries`, then `remove_dirs`. A glob path filter matching a directory's name no longer takes the directory (git descends and matches the files); a plain path next to a glob no longer removes the plain-named directory whole; an ignored nested repository (repository, gitfile, `.git` link) keeps its untracked parent under `-ff` without `-x`; a submodule entry wins over index paths below it; a nested repository the index tracks paths in is entered as git enters it. Path filters follow git's pathspec matching (`*` and `?` match `/`, a trailing slash names a directory, the common prefix starts the scan).
  - **vcs-working-tree (ignore rules):** a rule matches the path itself, as git: a name-only pattern its last component, a directory-only pattern only a directory. A negated directory rule (`!*/`) re-includes directories, never the files below them that a parent rule excludes.
- 8a603c0: **Clean never removes what it has not walked: fake `.git` directories as git.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fix):** `clean -d`, `-dx` and `-X -d` no longer remove a directory holding a `.git` that is no repository whole and unwalked: like git's `remove_dirs`, they enter it (and the fake `.git`), skip the nested repositories in it ("Would skip repository", `-ff` removes them whole), remove the files one by one and the directories once empty. Recursive removal is used only for a nested repository under `-ff`. A directory whose listing failed is never removed. Plain `clean` now works on the same tree model as `-X`: a directory removed only in part reports its removed subdirectories without the trailing slash, as git.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `WorktreeWalkOptions.enterNonRepositoryGitDirs` (list and enter a `.git` that names no repository) and `onUnreadable` (directories whose listing failed); `Worktree.remove` takes `WorktreeRemoveOptions` (`nonRepositoryGitDirs` accepts such `.git` components; a real repository's `.git` is still refused), `removeEmptyDirectory` too.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree` implements those options; a dangling `.git` link whose target lies inside the FilesApi is no repository (git removes its directory with `-d`).
- 05a5cae: **`git clone` writes its refs in git's initial transaction, and shallow repositories can be served.**
  
  - `CloneCommand` writes the cloned remote-tracking refs, tags and a bare or mirror clone's heads in one initial ref transaction (new `BatchOptions.initial`, `FetchClientOptions.initialTransaction`): the files store puts them straight into packed-refs, under git's header and peel lines, with no loose file and no reflog, and tells the reference-transaction hook as each git version does (2.43: `prepared`, `committed`; 2.5x: `preparing`, `prepared`, then the packed store's `preparing`, `prepared`). The followed tags of a single-branch clone are written loose one by one, then the remote HEAD, HEAD and the branch through HEAD, in git's order. Other stores apply an initial batch as a plain one.
  - The files store lists loose and packed refs merged in name order, as git iterates them (`git fetch --prune` reports the refs it deletes in git's order after a clone).
  - upload-pack serves a shallow repository as git does: the repository's shallow commits are advertised after its refs (v0), a v2 fetch that deepens nothing gets the shallow commits it reaches, depths and `--unshallow` stop at the repository's own boundary (git's depth-first walk, lines repeated as git repeats them), and every pack is cut there (`withServedShallow`, `prepareServedShallow`).
  - The fetch client reads a shallow server's commits (v0 advertisement, v2 `shallow-info`): a clone records them (`FetchClientOptions.cloning`), a fetch rejects each ref whose new history needs one, with git's "rejected … because shallow roots are not allowed to be updated" warning, unless `FetchCommand.setUpdateShallow(true)` (`git fetch --update-shallow`) records them: only those git's update_shallow keeps (reached from a fetched ref's new history short of the local refs, and, for commits already here, reached by no local ref), so a fetch into a complete repository records none. A v2 fetch from a shallow server that deepens nothing never unshallows the client's own shallow commits, wants below the server's boundary are refused, and a shallow repository whose facade has no commit graph is not served. A local clone of a shallow source fetches through upload-pack, with git's warnings.
- 1777896: **Commit-graph walks give a full walk's answers on graphs whose generations cannot be trusted.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** `CommitGraph.generationsTrusted` checks the whole graph once, on first use: generations cut and order walks only when the graph has generation numbers (`generationNumbersEnabled`), every record decodes, none is 0 and each is above its parents'. Otherwise every generation is Infinity (git reads a stored 0 as GENERATION_NUMBER_INFINITY), so `isAncestor`, `findMergeBase` and merge-ort's merge bases are right on graphs with zero levels (git before 2.18), a corrupt record, or corrected dates git 2.43 truncated past 2^32. `getHeader(id, { checkExists: true })` also looks the commit up in the object store (git's GIT_COMMIT_GRAPH_PARANOIA). A graph that fails to load is not loaded again until `invalidate()`. `listObjects` reads its wants, so a missing one throws even when a stale graph lists it. A split write gives the layers it merged away a new mtime, as git's `mark_commit_graphs`, and expires them with one clock read (the default expire time is that same now), so they are always expired as in git. `isAncestor` and `findMergeBase` read one graph for the whole walk: a graph written and loaded while they run is not mixed in (commits outside the walk's graph are read from the object store). `Commits.headerReader()` gives a header reader bound to the graph loaded now. `createGitFilesHistory` takes and exposes the `commitGraph` provider.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `gc` reports a failed commit-graph write in `commitGraphError` and keeps what it did before, as `git gc`. The backend, `gc` and `writeCommitGraph` take an `environment`: the commit-graph settings are then read from the system and global config too. `createGitFilesHistoryFromFiles` passes the backend's commit-graph on (`History.commitGraph`); `FileWorktree` takes an `environment` for the submodules `openGitlink` opens. The backend reloads the graph after its ref store changes the `shallow` file or a replace ref.
  - **vcs-commands:** the merge-base queue is ordered by generation only when the graph trusts them, and one merge-base computation reads one graph (`commitsBaseSource` takes a `headerReader` once). `InitCommand.setEnvironment` passes the host's environment to the repository it opens, so a global `core.commitGraph` is honoured.
  - **vcs-transport-adapters:** `getCommitInfo(oid, { checkExists: true })` does not report a commit that only a stale commit-graph lists; without it (upload-pack's walks) the graph is read unchecked, as git does by default.
  - **vcs-transport:** `RepositoryFacade.getCommitInfo` takes `{ checkExists }`; receive-pack asks for it for the type of a new tip and in its connectivity check.
- 20c317e: **Faster `commit -a`, `commit` and `log -p`, same output.**
  
  - `commit -a` / `add -u`: the stat pass goes a chunk at a time (`worktreeEntryChunks`), and an entry clean by its stat is settled in the loop (`IndexAdder.unchangedByStat`) instead of an `add` call and a promise per entry. The index's entries are grouped and the adder opened without a promise per entry, and the adder looks paths up by binary search instead of building a set of every path.
  - `writeTree` skips a valid cache-tree node's entries by its entry count (git's `update_one`) instead of grouping every entry at each directory level; `verifyTreePaths` checks an index in order in one pass (each directory looked up once by the entry that opens it), with the same refusals and messages.
  - Index writes copy the bytes of the entries read or last written (`parseIndexFile(data, { keepEncoding: true })`, `IndexEncoding`, `encodeIndexFileBytes(..., { source })`), in runs, so a write encodes only the entries changed; reading an index builds no object id or path view per entry, and the stores' "as read" entry set is built only when asked.
  - Packs: `applyGitDelta` decodes copy operands with integer shifts (it computed a floating-point power per operand byte) and copies short runs byte by byte.
  - `log -p`: the shared blob cache lets a blob go at its second read (a version is read as the old side of one change and the new side of the next, then no more) and keeps up to 64 MiB.
- eadd78c: **`commit --only` takes the listed paths literally under the index lock, and no staging writes a tree with two entries of one name.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `commit --only` lists paths as git's `list_paths` (pathspec magic and globs such as `d/`, `*.md`, `:(glob)d/**`, `:!d/x`, `./a`, matched against the index and HEAD, each element reported when it matches nothing) and takes each one as `add_remove_files` does: a file staged, a path gone removed, and a path that is now a directory refused before anything changes (`'a' does not have a commit checked out`), where it used to leave both `a` and `a/inner` staged and commit a corrupt tree. A glob character in a path no longer stages untracked files it happens to match.
  - **vcs-commands:** `commit --only` and `commit -a` hold `index.lock` from before they edit the index until HEAD has moved, as git does: the new index goes into the lock and is renamed over the index after the ref update; a concurrent writer is refused meanwhile, and a failed commit leaves the index (and the instance's unwritten edits) as they were.
  - **vcs-commands:** `add` replaces the entries a new one clashes with as a file or a directory (git's `check_file_directory_conflict`: adding `a/b` removes `a`, adding `a` removes `a/...`), and a path given an entry or removed has its cache-tree path invalidated as git's index edits do, so `TREE` matches git's byte for byte while conflicts remain.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Staging.lockIndex()` (optional; `GitStaging` and `FileStagingStore` implement it) holds the index lock for a write committed or rolled back later, on top of `IndexFile.lock()`. `verifyTreePaths` refuses a file that is also a directory ("You have both a and a/b"), checked by every staging's `writeTree`. `RemoveStagingStages` is the edit `IndexEditor.remove(path, stage)` records. A cache-tree set with `setCacheTree` is what `getCacheTree` returns until the write.
  - **vcs-store-files, vcs-store-mem, vcs-store-kv, vcs-store-sql:** `removeEntry(path, stage)` and `editor.remove(path, stage)` remove only that stage, and `editor.remove(path)` every stage of a conflicted path.
  - **Tests:** the staging suite (vcs-testing) checks stage-specific removal and the file/directory refusal of `writeTree`, and runs for `FileStagingStore` and `GitStaging` too.
- e1c44ca: **Faster `git log -p` / `--stat`, and a faster index read, with the same output.**
  
  On a 3,000-commit history (built bundles, `node scripts/bench-porcelain.mjs`) `log -p` takes about 2.4 s instead of 3.0 s and `log --stat` about 2.1 s instead of 2.6 s; the diffs of the commits alone take about half the time.
  
  - **vcs-utils:** a typed array of its own costs a backing store outside the heap (a microsecond or two), more than the loops of a small diff over it. `allocInt32` (beside `allocBytes`) carves small `Int32Array`s from the shared slabs, and xdiff takes its per-diff arrays from it or from scratch buffers reused across diffs (the classification's table, the class counts, the Myers arrays, the discard actions). A buffer's lines are split and hashed in one pass (`LineHasher`). `encodeGitPath` encodes a short ASCII path into a slab view, and hunk headers and hunk bodies are written without buffers of their own.
