  - **vcs-working-tree:** `parseIndexFile` checks a short ASCII path (no empty component, no `.git` one, no NUL) as it reads its bytes, so only the other paths go through `validatePath` afterwards.
  - **vcs-commands:** a command looks up its entry points (`call`, `callText`, ...) once per class, not once per instance (`git log -p` makes a diff command per commit); a tree-to-tree diff reads the submodule settings (`.gitmodules`) only when a submodule changed.
- 7f697fd: **The served HTTP handler serves the dumb protocol as `git http-backend` does, checks the request's content type, and appends to the alternates a quarantined pre-receive is given.**
  
  - **Security note (default change):** `createServedHttpHandler` / `createServedFetchHandler` now answer the dumb protocol by default (`http.getanyfile`, as http-backend), where they refused it before. The object files are not filtered by hidden refs, so they fail closed: while any hidden-ref pattern applies (the operator's `settings.hideRefs` / `uploadPackHideRefs`, the repository's `transfer.hideRefs` / `uploadpack.hideRefs`, the resolver's settings), every `objects/...` path is refused (403) unless the operator passes `getAnyFile: true`, or the repository sets `http.getanyfile=true` explicitly while only its own config hides refs (a repository's opt-in never re-opens what the operator's `settings` or the resolver's settings hide); `HEAD` and `info/refs` (which leave hidden refs out) are still served. A server that relies on `uploadpack.allowAnySHA1InWant` staying off without hiding refs should pass `getAnyFile: false`.
  - The dumb protocol (`getAnyFile: false` or the repository's `http.getanyfile=false` turns it off): `objects/info/packs` generated from the repository's own packs (youngest first, no `update-server-info` needed), `objects/info/alternates`, `objects/info/http-alternates`, loose objects, packs and indexes, with http-backend's content types and caching (`no-cache`, or a year for objects and packs), `Content-Length` and `Last-Modified`; a missing file is a 404. A native client with `GIT_SMART_HTTP=0` clones and fetches from it. The dumb protocol is still served only where upload-pack is, and `info/refs` still leaves out hidden refs. A `HEAD` request for a file returns its headers without opening it, and a `GET` opens the file at the first read of its body.
  - The files come from the new `ServedRepository.files` (`servedGitFilesOf(files, gitDir)`; `servedRepositoryOf` sets it from the new `OpenedRepository.files`, which `Worktree.openRepository` fills). Only files of the object directory are served: a file whose links lead outside `<git dir>/objects` (an alternates file linked to `../config`) is not; an absolute link is followed only below the FilesApi's `rootDirectory`. A repository without files (key-value, SQL, memory stores) answers `HEAD` and `info/refs` only and every `objects/...` path 404.
  - A POST to `git-upload-pack` / `git-receive-pack` whose `Content-Type` is not exactly `application/x-git-<service>-request` is answered 415 with http-backend's message.
  - A quarantined pre-receive's `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` is the host's (new `GitHostEnvironment.alternateObjectDirectories`, read from `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` by `createNodeGitHostEnvironment`) with the repository's objects appended, as tmp-objdir.c's `env_append`.
  - A local push (`git push <path>`) runs the receiving repository's hooks without the pushing side's repository variables (git's `local_repo_env`: `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, ...; new `LOCAL_REPO_ENV`, `remoteHookSession(..., { localPush: true })`, `HookHost.unsetEnv`), as connect.c starts receive-pack; the host's alternates are appended for served HTTP and git:// pushes only.
- 35239a9: **Small deviations from git fixed: empty config booleans, worktree pointer files, `addWorktree`'s admin directory.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `parseGitBool`, git's `git_config_bool`: a key with no `=` is true, an empty value (`key =`) is false, the spellings in any case, and integers as `git_parse_int` reads them (`0x10`, `010`, `1k`); anything else is undefined (git refuses it).
  - **Booleans everywhere read through it.** `core.ignorecase`, `core.filemode`, `core.symlinks`, `core.autocrlf`, `core.safecrlf`, `core.quotePath`, `filter.<driver>.required`, `merge.*`, `diff.*`, `blame.*`, `status.renames`, `status.showUntrackedFiles`, `extensions.worktreeConfig`, `core.bare` and the other boolean settings: an empty value used to read as true in several of them and now reads as false, as in git.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a `.git` file's `gitdir:` and `$GIT_DIR/commondir` are stripped of their trailing `\n`/`\r` only (`read_gitfile_gently`, `get_common_dir_noenv`), never trimmed; a `C:/…` commondir is absolute. New `parseGitfile`, `parseCommonDir`, `parseWorktreeGitdir`, `isAbsoluteGitPath`. `addWorktree` writes `worktrees/NAME/commondir` (`../..`), absolute host paths in the `.git` file and the `gitdir` back-pointer when the FilesApi's root is known, numbers the name when it is taken (`wt1`), and takes `lock` (`git worktree add --lock [--reason]`), so native git can use the worktree. `objects/info/alternates`, `FETCH_HEAD` and the merge/rebase state files are read and written as bytes (non-UTF-8 survives).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `AddWorktreeOptions.lock`. With `core.ignoreCase` a directory's `.gitignore` applies to its path in any ASCII case (git's `fspathncmp`).
  - **vcs-commands:** `init` keeps non-UTF-8 bytes in the config when it sets `core.bare`/`core.ignorecase`.
  - **vcs-store-sql:** `findByNamePattern` returns entries in git's tree order (a subtree compares as `name/`).
  - **vcs-transport:** HTTP Basic auth sends the credentials' UTF-8 bytes (a lone surrogate as its byte); `btoa` threw on characters above U+00FF.
- 32f7288: **Path matching, case folding and git directory pointers as git does them, bytes included.**
  
  - **vcs-working-tree:** ignore and attribute patterns compare their literal prefix and `*suffix` as bytes when the pattern holds bytes that are no UTF-8, so `caf\xc3*` or `*\xa9` takes the UTF-8 `café` as in git (`wildmatchBytes` and `sameBytes` are exported next to `wildmatch`). Ignore rules now honour `core.ignoreCase` (`IgnoreRule.matchesPath(path, isDirectory, ignoreCase)`, `IgnoreNode.checkIgnored(..., ignoreCase)`, `IgnoreManager.setIgnoreCase`, `createIgnoreManager({ ignoreCase })`, `Worktree.setIgnoreCaseReader`, set by working copies from their configuration). Attributes with `core.ignoreCase` fold ASCII letters only, as git's `fspathncmp` does, so `É` no longer matches `é`.
  - **vcs-utils:** `foldAsciiCase` lowers ASCII letters only (git's `tolower` / `strncasecmp`).
  - **vcs-commands:** `:(icase)` pathspecs in `clean`, the case-insensitive name lookups of the overwrite checks and of merge-ort's unique paths fold ASCII only, as git's `strncasecmp` and `memihash`.
  - **vcs-core:** `.git` / `.gitmodules` spellings (HFS+, NTFS) fold ASCII only, as git's `verify_path`; no accepted or refused path changes.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a `.git` file's `gitdir:`, a `.git` link's target, `commondir`, a submodule's HEAD and refs, and the config file are read as bytes (`decodeGitPath`), and `addWorktree` writes the `gitdir:` file and the back-pointer as bytes: a git directory whose path is no UTF-8 is found, and rewriting the config keeps a latin-1 value's bytes.
  - **vcs-store-sql:** `SQLStaging.listEntriesUnder` is a range query: no longer ASCII case-insensitive and no `_` / `%` wildcards (`src` no longer lists `SRC/…`, `a_b` no longer lists `aXb/…`). `searchMessage` takes `%`, `_` and `\` literally. `findByNamePattern` returns entries by tree id and then by name in git's byte order.
- df0147c: Hooks closer to git 2.5x: the reference-transaction hook hears symbolic-ref writes
  (`<zero> ref:<target> <ref>`: checkout, switch, `rebase --abort`, `remote set-head`), the old
  values git passes for HEAD and ORIG_HEAD in merge, am and rebase (with rebase's
  CHERRY_PICK_HEAD, REBASE_HEAD and AUTO_MERGE transactions), and pushes served through
  `serveGitConnection` (`servedRepositoryOf(..., { hooks })`); post-index-change runs after index
  writes (`<updated-workdir> <updated-skipworktree>`); configured hooks (`hook.<name>.event` /
  `.command` / `.enabled`) run before the hooks directory's script; `gc().setAuto(true)` decides
  as `git gc --auto` and asks pre-auto-gc first. packed-refs is written as git writes it: the
  `peeled fully-peeled sorted` header and a peel line for each annotated tag. A rewrite
  of a packed-refs whose traits leave entries unpeeled (an older `sorted`-only file, JGit's
  `peeled`) peels them again, as git 2.43 does, and never claims `fully-peeled` for entries it
  could not peel. vcs-utils adds `parseGitInt` (git's `git_parse_int`).
- 8a603c0: **Ignore rules as git's wildmatch; clean never removes an unverifiable repository; pathspec magic.**
  
  - **vcs-working-tree (data loss fixes):** `.gitignore` rules are matched as git matches them (`match_basename` / `match_pathname` over a port of git's `wildmatch`): a name-only rule tests the last component (`foo` then `!foo/` keeps `foo/foo` ignored), a directory-only rule ending in `**` never matches a file, a backslash makes the next character literal (`\*.txt`, `\[x\]`, `\#hash`, `\!bang`, `sp\ `), unescaped trailing spaces go. New `wildmatch()` export and `IgnoreRule.matchesPath()`. A pattern git accepts is never dropped (an unclosed bracket simply matches nothing).
  - **vcs-store-files, vcs-working-tree:** new `Worktree.repositoryState(path)` (`"repository" | "none" | "unknown"`); `FileWorktree.isIgnored` decides a directory by `lstat` (an empty one too).
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fixes):** `clean -ff` never removes a directory whose `.git` cannot be checked (a link or gitfile naming a place beyond the root): it is kept as a possible repository. Path filters take git's pathspec magic: `:!`/`:^`/`:(exclude)` (an implicit `.` when only excludes are given), `:/`/`:(top)`, `:(literal)`, `:(glob)`, `:(icase)`; `.` and `..` are normalised as git does and a path above the top is refused, as is `attr` magic.
- eadd78c: **Index flags and the cache-tree survive index rewrites as in git, and more sequencer leftovers match git.**
  
  - `reset --hard`, `reset --mixed`, `checkout -f`, `reset --merge`/`--keep` and the autostash reset of `merge --autostash` and rebase keep skip-worktree and assume-unchanged flags as unpack-trees does. An entry the target leaves unchanged keeps its flags. A changed entry keeps its skip-worktree bit. A skip-worktree file the tree keeps is not written. Before this, `git ls-files -v` showed every flag dropped. `Staging.readTree` takes `keepMatching` (`true` keeps the old entry, `"flags"` keeps only its flags) for this one-way merge.
  - An entry whose flags cheat is checked against its file by its stat, as git's `verify_uptodate_1` does. `reset --hard` and `checkout -f` refuse such an entry when they would change or delete it over a changed file ("Entry 'f' not uptodate. Cannot merge."). A branch switch refuses an assume-unchanged file whose stat changed, even when its content did not.
  - `stash apply --index` applies the stash's index as git does: a `diff-tree --binary` of it, run through `apply --cached`. A hunk whose context the index no longer has now fails with "error: patch failed: ...", "patch does not apply" and "conflicts in index. Try without --index.", where we used to merge it.
  - `am --skip` cleans the index as git's `clean_index` does (the same as `am --abort`): a refusal ends with "failed to clean index", and a merge state left behind is removed.
  - The cache-tree (`TREE`) now survives index edits as in git. When entries change, the paths that changed go invalid and every other directory stays valid. Before this, the whole extension was dropped. So a commit after an edit keeps an untouched subtree stored as 100664, and the commit ids match native git. `commit` writes the index with a fully valid cache-tree (git's `prepare_index`), and `checkout`, `reset --merge` and `reset --keep` update it before they write (`cache_tree_update` with `WRITE_TREE_REPAIR`). The `TREE` bytes match native git's.
  - `verifyCacheTree` (vcs-working-tree) checks that every valid cache-tree node describes the index entries below it, for tests and fsck.
- eadd78c: **`add -N` no longer turns a tracked file into a deletion, and `commit --only` commits what git commits.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `add -N` leaves a path with a stage-0 entry (tracked or already intent-to-add) as it is, as git's `ADD_CACHE_NEW_ONLY`; before, a tracked file became an empty intent-to-add entry and the next commit deleted it. A conflicted path still has its stages replaced, as git does.
  - **vcs-commands:** `commit --only <paths>` is git's partial commit: the paths the given ones name in the index or HEAD are taken from the worktree (cleaned; a file gone removed, an intent-to-add file with its content) into the index and into HEAD's tree, which is committed; the index keeps its other staged changes and is written once the commit is made. A path nothing knows fails with git's `pathspec ... did not match`, a partial commit during a merge, cherry-pick or rebase is refused, other unmerged paths do not stop it, and an amend starts from HEAD.
  - **vcs-commands:** every index-writing command (add, rm, commit, reset, checkout, switch, merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase, am, pull, stash push/apply, apply `--index`/`--cached`, and clean for reading) reads the index again at its start when another writer replaced it, as git does, and refuses with `IndexChangedError` before touching anything only when the instance holds unwritten edits. `commit` now re-reads instead of refusing.
  - **vcs-working-tree, vcs-store-files, vcs-store-mem, vcs-store-kv, vcs-store-sql:** the stagings' builders and editors copy the whole stat data of an entry (nanoseconds, uid, gid), so an index rewritten around a few paths (`rm` of many entries, `commit --only`) no longer leaves the others stat-dirty. `Staging.hasUnwrittenChanges()` (optional) tells whether an instance holds edits made since it read or wrote the index.
- eadd78c: **A staged deletion survives `stash apply --index`, and `rm` keeps the flags of the other entries.**
  
  - `stash apply --index` could write a cache-tree that no longer described the index: a path removed from the index outside the merged paths stayed in a valid directory node, and the next commit (ours or native git's) put the deleted file back. The index it leaves now has git's fully valid cache-tree (`reset_tree`'s `cache_tree_update`).
  - A cache-tree set with `setCacheTree` no longer goes stale when entries change before the write. The staging records the entries it describes, and the write invalidates every path that changed since, as it already did for the cache-tree read from the index.
  - Intent-to-add entries are left out of every tree, as git's `update_one` leaves them out, even when they are also skip-worktree. Before this, `git add -N new; git update-index --skip-worktree new` committed `new` as an empty file.
  - `rm` keeps the other entries as they are. Before this, it rebuilt them from path, mode and id: intent-to-add entries became real entries naming a blob never written, and skip-worktree, assume-unchanged and the stat data were dropped. `commit --only` also gives the index its own entries back once it has written its tree.
  - `commit` refuses an index that another writer replaced since this instance read it (`IndexChangedError`), as index writes do, instead of committing the stale entries.
  - `rebase` goes on over a dirty assume-unchanged or skip-worktree file, as git's `require_clean_work_tree` does. The stat check of flagged entries counts an intent-to-add entry as changed and finds no racy entry in an index without a timestamp, as git's `ie_match_stat` does.
- 5a488ed: **`index.version` and `feature.manyFiles`, `core.untrackedCache` in every index writer, case folding in the unpack-trees callers.**
  
  - **Index version:** a new index (no index file when it was read) is written in the version read-cache.c's `get_index_format_default` gives: `$GIT_INDEX_VERSION` (`GitHostEnvironment.indexVersion`), else `index.version`, else 4 under `feature.manyFiles`, else 3 (written as 2 without extended flags); an index read from a file keeps its version, a split index's shared index included. A value outside 2..4 falls back to 3 with git's warning (`IndexHost.warn`, `takeIndexWarnings`); an `index.version` that is not a number makes every command refuse as it starts, before it reads or writes anything, as git dies in `prepare_repo_settings` (`IndexVersionConfigError`, an `InvalidConfigError` with git's `bad numeric config value`; `indexVersionConfigError`). `IndexSettings.version`/`versionWarning`, `indexFormatDefault`.
  - **Untracked cache in every index writer:** `core.untrackedCache` (default `true` under `feature.manyFiles`) is applied to every index written through the staging's host, as git's `tweak_untracked_cache` applies it to every index it reads: `true` adds an empty cache (ident and the flags of `status.showUntrackedFiles`, no root) when there is none or it names another worktree, `false` removes it (`IndexSettings.untrackedCache`/`untrackedIdent`/`untrackedFlags`). `add` writes the index when only that changes (git's `SKIP_IF_UNCHANGED`; also for `core.splitIndex`), through the new `Staging.wantsRewrite`. Only `status` fills the cache, as in git. `Worktree.hostPath` is part of the interface.
  - **Case folding in the unpack-trees callers:** under `core.ignorecase`, read-tree `-m -u` (one and two trees, `--prefix`, three-way additions), merge (fast-forward and the result's checkout), rebase, cherry-pick, revert, am, stash apply and `reset --merge`/`--keep` ask the source index folding case where git's `verify_absent` does (`icase_exists`, `verify_clean_subdirectory`'s `read_directory`): a case variant of a tracked file in a directory a file replaces goes with the directory, removed after every refusal check (`IndexCaseFolding`, `removeCaseVariants`); nested repositories are still refused.
  - **stash apply:** an entry the merge removed that clashes with a file the stash adds (a directory replaced by a file, or the reverse) stays out of the index with git's `error: '<path>' appears as both a file and as a directory`, instead of leaving both in the index.
- 9cf798e: **Checkout into an unborn index, as git (P4.67).**
  
  - `isIndexUnborn(staging)` (git's `is_index_unborn`): no entries and no index file read or written yet, as `clone --no-checkout` and `worktree add --no-checkout` leave it. File-backed stagings decide it from whether their last read found an index file (`Staging.isUnborn`, from the index checksum), so a FilesApi without modification times no longer makes an existing index look unborn; memory, KV and SQL stores are never unborn.
  - checkout and switch run unpack-trees' initial checkout there (`topts.initial_checkout`): every entry of the target is written and staged, `git checkout <current branch>` included, where ours used to read the missing index as HEAD's files staged as deleted and write nothing. Untracked files in the way still refuse the switch with git's message (the index stays unborn); sparse-checkout patterns apply (entries outside are skip-worktree, an untracked file there keeps its entry tracked). `checkout --orphan` checks HEAD's files out into the new branch's index.
  - `CheckoutCommand.setOnlyMergeOnSwitchingBranches` (git's `only_merge_on_switching_branches`, set by `SwitchCommand` and to be set for a bare `git checkout -b <new>`): a switch naming no target (a new branch without a start point, `--detach` without a commit) only moves HEAD, leaving index and worktree alone.
  - The two-tree `read-tree -m` takes every entry of the second tree as new in an unborn index (`SwitchTreesOptions.initialCheckout`).
  - rebase's clean check counts no staged changes in an unborn index (`has_uncommitted_changes`); a refused checkout of the upstream on a fast-forward ends with `error: could not detach HEAD`, as git's merge backend (`checkout_onto`).
  - The autostash of `merge --autostash`, `rebase --autostash` and 2.5x's `checkout -m` / `switch -m` stashes nothing in an unborn index (create_autostash: no unstaged changes, `has_uncommitted_changes` 0), so a refusal stands as git's with no `Applied autostash.`. `CheckoutCommand.setCompatibility("2.43")` (and `SwitchCommand.setCompatibility`) reproduce 2.43's `-m`: a refused switch with changes staged against HEAD dies with `fatal: cannot continue with staged changes in the following files:` (exit 128).
  - `checkout --orphan <new> <start-point>` merges the index and worktree to the start point's tree (checks it out into an unborn index), where ours ignored the start point; `--orphan` refuses an existing branch name (`fatal: a branch named '<new>' already exists`).
- 8329312: **Linked worktrees run the reference-transaction hook and peel packed-refs as git does; served repositories can confine their config includes.**
  
  - A linked worktree's ref store (`createWorktreeRefs`) now has `setTransactionHook`: commits (HEAD told as asked, then its branch), branch and tag creation and deletion (`removeAll`, one transaction as 2.5x or 2.43's legacy order), the worktree's own refs (`refs/bisect/`, `refs/worktree/`, ORIG_HEAD), its symbolic HEAD (2.5x's `ref:` line), `pack-refs` and gc run the repository's hook from the worktree, as native git 2.43 and 2.56 do. Hooks run in a worktree reached through a gitfile get `GIT_DIR` as git exports it (the real path, through the new `GitHostEnvironment.realPath`; Node: `realHostPath`), a bare repository's hooks `GIT_DIR=.` (they now run from `Git.fromWorktreeAdmin` too), and `worktree add`'s post-checkout runs without `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` (new `HookInvocation.unsetEnv`). Concurrent batches each tell the hook their own updates (`BatchOptions.transactionHook`); `removeAll` deletes none of the worktree's refs when the repository's transaction is refused (as 2.43, `legacy`, only when the packed store's transaction is: a refusal of one ref's own transaction leaves the others, the worktree's included, going; new `RefTransactionAbortedError.packed`). A sequencer's "cannot lock ref" message names the lock under the git directory's logical path, as git's `absolute_path` keeps `$PWD` (`HookSession.hostGitDir({ real: false })`).
  - A commit refused in `preparing` tells `aborted` HEAD as asked, not its branch (new `RefTransactionContext.asAsked`).
  - packed-refs written from a linked worktree is peeled as the main worktree writes it (`peeled fully-peeled sorted`, a `^` line per annotated tag); `peelTag` is exported.
  - New `Worktree.openRepository(location, { configIncludes })` (`OpenRepositoryOptions`) and `GitConfigOptions.includes: "files"`: a server can keep a repository's `include.path` / `includeIf.*.path` inside the repository's own git directory (`includeRoots`; no `~/` path, links followed and checked, files up to 1 MiB, read no further than that through `ConfigFilesApi.readPrefix`, none where the storage cannot tell links apart or give a file's size) or follow none, also for the settings `createGitFilesBackend({ configIncludes })` reads (`configFilesApiOf`). The repository format check follows no include whatever the rule, as git's `read_repository_format` (`readRepositoryFormat` lost its `includes` option). The default stays git's; see the security notes in `docs/transports.md`. A linked worktree's git directory opened this way reads its `config.worktree`, its own HEAD for `includeIf.onbranch:` and its host path for `includeIf.gitdir:`.
- 2ce29f8: **`git log --cc` diffs with the diff options, and tree comparisons see canonical modes, as git does.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `log -p --cc` / `-c` diffs each parent with the diff options (`-w`, `-b`, `--ignore-space-at-eol`, `--ignore-cr-at-eol`, `--ignore-blank-lines`, the algorithm, and the indent heuristic, on by default through `diff.indentHeuristic`), as combine-diff.c passes `xdl_opts`; before, it diffed with none of them, so hunks could sit a line off from git's. Tree diffs (`diff`, `log -p`/`--stat`/`--name-status`, history simplification by path, combined diffs) compare entry modes as git's walk reads them: a 100664 entry is 100644, so the same blob under both is no change. The small-blob cache a `log -p` shares counts a blob read by concurrent opens once.
  - **vcs-core:** `canonTreeMode` (git's `canon_mode`); `TreeEntryCursor.mode` is canonical, and the commit-graph writer's changed-path walk compares canonical modes. `BlobRead.bytes` is documented read-only (a cache may hand the same buffer to every reader).
  - **vcs-utils:** `hashLine` and `LineHasher` give the one line hash of `ByteLines` and `InternedText`: an `InternedText` diffed against plain bytes or `ByteLines` (with or without the interner) now gives the plain diff, where its lines hashed differently before. Buffers passed to `unifiedDiffHunks` must not change afterwards (their lines are remembered by buffer).
- 8274d13: **`git log` / `git diff` output: follow-up fixes.**
  
  - Combined diffs (`log -c`/`--cc`, and `DiffCommand`'s combined diffs of conflicted paths) abbreviate their `index` lines to git's default length whatever `--abbrev` says; `DiffCommand`'s `abbrev` is clamped as git's `--abbrev=<n>` (below 4, 0 too, is 4; above 40 is 40).
  - `--full-diff` runs every commit's diff (git's `revs->diff`), so `--diff-merges=first-parent --full-diff` and `-m --full-history --full-diff` print what git prints.
  - `-m` / `--diff-merges=separate` with `--first-parent` diff merges against the first parent only.
  - `-c`/`--cc` with `--follow` show nothing of a merge when a parent before the last finds the file renamed or copied, and `--name-status` marks a copy found by the last parent `C`.
  - `--first-parent --graph` keeps the lane git keeps for a merge whose first parent was rewritten away.
  - The combined diff of a path a merge deletes prints its hunk, as git does.
- c80355f: **Merge, rebase, cherry-pick and revert do work proportional to what they change, not to the repository.** Output is unchanged; in a repository of 5000 files on the files backend a merge of five changes a side goes from about 1.1 s to about 0.3 s, a 200-commit rebase from 58 s to 10 s, and a rebase onto 1000 upstream commits from 7.6 s to 1.9 s.
  
  - **vcs-commands:** a merge over stored trees reads them as git's merge-ort does: a directory all three trees hold with the same id is one entry, never read (`prunedFlatTrees`), and a directory taken whole from one side is that side's tree. Only when directory renames are found (or the base is a virtual merge of several bases) are the whole trees merged, as before. The result is stored by rewriting only the changed directories, HEAD and the result are compared by tree ids (`treeDelta`), so only the paths that change are checked against the index and worktree and written, and "the index matches HEAD" compares tree ids computed from the index (as git's cache-tree) instead of reading HEAD's whole tree. A worktree file whose stat matches its index entry, on an entry older than the index (not racy), is no longer hashed (git's `ie_match_stat`); the clean-worktree check of a rebase stats files several at a time. The index is read and rewritten without an await per entry.
  - **vcs-core:** trees are cached by id once read or stored (and concurrent reads of one tree read it once), commits once parsed. The caches never serve an object the store no longer holds: the stores report each object removal (`noteObjectRemoved`, a process-wide `objectRemovalEpoch`), and a cache hit read before the last removal is checked with `objects.has` first, so a tree or commit that gc, prune, a repack or a discarded quarantine removed in the same process reads as absent on every backend; decoding and encoding trees no longer re-encode names per comparison or convert ids through regular expressions. Pack reads go through a shared cache of 64 KiB windows (long entries still stream from the file). A write of an object held in memory is hashed first and skipped when the object is already stored (git's `freshen_object`); `StagingBlobStore.isFresh` (optional) tells the object kernel.
  - **vcs-commands, the index as git leaves it:** merge, cherry-pick, revert and rebase write the cache-tree (`TREE` extension) unpack-trees writes, from the index's own cache-tree with only the changed directories computed again (conflicted paths and the directories of intent-to-add entries invalid); a refused merge leaves the index restore_state leaves. An entry the merge updates keeps its skip-worktree bit. `cherry-pick -n` and `revert -n` rewrite the index as git's `write_index_as_tree` does when its cache-tree is not fully valid, so an entry as new as the rewritten index is racy and a same-size local edit behind a matching stat is refused, as git refuses it; the stat-match skip trusts a matching stat only on an entry older than the index (an exact stat match on a racy entry is read too). A refused merge drops the change of an assume-unchanged file, as restore_state's `reset --hard` does.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** the index is written in version 3 exactly when an entry has an extended flag (skip-worktree, intent-to-add) and in version 2 otherwise, and only such entries carry the extended flags word (git's `do_write_index`); the cache-tree is read and written (`CacheTree`, `cacheTreeOfTree`, `updateCacheTree`, `Staging.getCacheTree` / `setCacheTree`, optional). The tree of an index leaves out its intent-to-add entries, as git's write-tree (also in the memory, KV and SQL stagings). An index rewrite encodes only the entries that changed (the bytes of an unchanged entry are reused) and compares entries instead of hashing them to decide whether the cache-tree survives; the checksum is converted to hex once. `Staging.entryList()` (optional) gives every entry at once.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a loose object is read without checking first that its file exists, a ref without an extra existence check (a files API whose read throws for a missing file still reads it as absent), and the index builder's stage check is linear.
  - **vcs-utils:** `bytesToHex` uses a lookup table.
- 9e462a8: **Paths that are not valid UTF-8 keep their bytes; octopus merges refuse binary files as git does.**
  
  ```typescript
  import { decodeGitPath, encodeGitPath } from "@statewalker/vcs-utils/encoding";
  
  decodeGitPath(new Uint8Array([0x63, 0x61, 0x66, 0xe9])); // "caf\udce9" (latin-1 caf\xe9)
  encodeGitPath("caf\udce9");                              // the same four bytes
  ```
  
  Git paths are bytes. A tree or index entry named `caf\xe9.txt` (latin-1, made by native git) was decoded with `TextDecoder`, which turned the byte into U+FFFD: the name was lost, a tree or index written back got another id, and distinct names could collapse into one. Paths stay strings, now through a reversible encoding at the byte boundaries (Python's `surrogateescape`): valid UTF-8 decodes as before, and each byte outside a valid UTF-8 sequence becomes the lone surrogate U+DC00 + byte.
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `decodeGitPath`, `encodeGitPath`, `compareGitPaths` (git's byte order) and `hasEscapedBytes` in `@statewalker/vcs-utils/encoding`. `ByteSink.text` writes escaped bytes as the bytes they stand for.
  - **vcs-core:** tree entry names are decoded and encoded with them (and sorted by their bytes), so reading a tree and storing it again keeps its id; `listObjects` and the structural tree deltas keep the names too.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** index entries (versions 2 to 4; reading version 4 now counts the shared prefix in bytes, as git writes it, which a non-ASCII path got wrong), the cache-tree (`TREE`) and resolve-undo (`REUC`) extensions keep the bytes, and the index is sorted by them; wildmatch matches the bytes; a C-quoted `.gitattributes` pattern with octal escapes unquotes to the same bytes.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the staging store sorts by bytes.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** the Node `FilesApi` hands such a path to the operating system as its bytes and lists names as bytes, so on Linux the worktree file has the original name (checkout, status, add). Hosts whose names are Unicode cannot hold it: the browser file system APIs store U+FFFD for each escaped byte, and macOS and Windows refuse or rewrite the name; trees, the index and commits keep the bytes there too.
  - **vcs-commands:** diff (`patch`, `stat`, `numstat`, `name-only`, `name-status`, renames) and status quote the names as git does (`"caf\351.txt"`), and with `core.quotePath=false` print the bytes themselves; diff, status, rename detection and clean sort paths in git's byte order.
  
  Octopus merges (`include("a").include("b")`): a binary file the last head changes is refused as `git merge-file` refuses it (`error: Cannot merge binary files: .merge_file_XXXXXX`, then `ERROR: content conflict in <path>`): ours stays in the worktree, with stages 1 to 3 in the index, where conflict markers were written before. The conflict markers git-merge-one-file writes carry the names of its temporary files (`<<<<<<< .merge_file_XXXXXX`, also on `|||||||` with `diff3`/`zdiff3`), random in git; ours are numbered. A `MergeResult` of an octopus left with conflicts now carries what git-merge-one-file printed on stderr in `message` (`ERROR: content conflict in f`, `fatal: merge program failed`).
- 98be9b5: **Object alternates in the files store; `clone --shared`, `--reference`, `--reference-if-able` and `--dissociate`.**
  
  - `createGitFilesBackend` reads the repository's alternates as git does: `$GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` (`:`-separated, C-quoted entries) then `objects/info/alternates` (comments, relative entries from the objects directory), each followed by its own alternates down to git's depth limit, the objects directory itself and repeats skipped, with git's error lines (`objects.alternates.errors`, `onAlternatesError`). Paths are read through the FilesApi (absolute ones inside its `rootDirectory`). New `GitObjects.alternates` (`list`, `add`, `refresh`, `refs`, `dissociate`), options `alternates`, `alternatesFromEnvironment` and `workTree` (where relative variable entries are resolved: the worktree top, as git), and `OpenRepositoryOptions.alternates`; `openRepository` and submodules opened with `openGitlink` never apply the environment variable. `alternates.add()` writes the file under `alternates.lock`; `refs()` leaves out an alternate's refs to objects it lacks. A repository without alternates reads through its own store, no layer added.
  - Reads, `has`, listings, prefix lookups (abbreviations, disambiguation) and the approximate count include borrowed objects; writes skip an object an alternate holds and go to the repository's own objects only.
  - `repack()` copies borrowed objects unless the new `local` (`git repack -l`); `gc()` passes it (`repack -d -l`) and, like `GcOrchestrator`, prunes only the repository's own objects; `prunePacked` also removes loose copies an alternate's pack holds. gc, repack, prune and `notes prune` no longer refuse repositories with alternates (`assertNoAlternates` is removed). An alternate git cannot read either (gone, a path that cannot be normalized, nesting too deep) is reported in the new `GcResult.errors`/`RepackResult.errors` and they go on, as git; they refuse, deleting nothing, only where git reads alternates this store does not (`objects/info/alternates` under `alternates: false`, an absolute entry outside the FilesApi's root: `AlternatesUnreadableError`), and a ref, HEAD (a linked worktree's too) or index entry whose object is missing is fatal (`MissingRootError`: `bad object <ref>`, as git dies), where it used to be dropped from the roots.
  - `fsck` reads and checks the alternates' objects too, as git's default `--full`, in each version's order.
  - receive-pack advertises the alternates' refs as `.have` (`RepositoryFacade.alternateRefs`, `core.alternateRefsPrefixes`), and fetch offers them as negotiation tips and complete history, as fetch-pack does.
  - `CloneCommand.setShared`, `addReference`, `addReferenceIfAble` (messages in `CloneResult.messages`) and `setDissociate`, with git's alternates lines and errors (`computeAlternatePath`).
  - Dumb HTTP's `info/refs` now peels a tag only an alternate holds.
- af93342: **Renames merge as git's merge-ort with directory renames; a rebase pick costs about half as much; `core.ignorecase` follows the filesystem.**
  
  The merge redoes itself once the renames are known when reading the trees it deferred would triple the paths it reads (merge-ort's `redo_after_renames`, with its cached renames, targets and irrelevant sources), keeps a directory a known rename goes into out of the trivial directory merges, and leaves a rename that a directory rename moves back onto its source where it is. A differential fuzz of rename/rename (1to2, 2to1), rename/add and rename/delete mixed with directory renames, under `merge.directoryRenames` `true` and `conflict`, now matches `git merge-tree --write-tree --messages` (tree, conflicted stages and messages) on git 2.43 and 2.56.
  
  A rebase or cherry-pick pick touches fewer files: reflogs, `rebase-merge/done` and `rewritten-list` are appended to (`O_APPEND`) instead of rewritten, a ref update reads each loose ref once per look (the plan, then under the locks), the index is written as one buffer created in its lock (its checksum by Web Crypto) and rewritten without copying the entries it keeps, loose objects are written with an exclusive create and one rename, and the small state files git writes with `write_file` (`msgnum`, `end`, `head-name`, ...) are written in place. The todo lists, messages and `author-script` (`git-rebase-todo`, `sequencer/todo`, `message`, `MERGE_MSG`, `SQUASH_MSG`, ...) are still written to a temporary renamed into place, as git's lock files, so a crash never leaves them missing or partial; a pick writes `git-rebase-todo` before it appends to `done`, as git's `save_todo`, and a rebase whose todo is gone fails (`RebaseTodoMissingError`) instead of finishing with commits dropped. A loose object whose exclusive create read the content and still failed is refused instead of written empty, and `PosixNodeFilesApi`'s directory creation reports a directory removed while it is made as missing (`ENOENT`). `FilesApi` gains an optional `append` (implemented by the Node, in-memory, mounted and simulated-link FilesApis; `appendByRewrite` for the others), `Checkout` an optional `appendStateFile`, and `encodeIndexFileBytes` writes an index at once. `PosixNodeFilesApi` checks paths with synchronous `lstat`s, as git does: the async round trip cost some 25 times the call.
  
  With `core.ignorecase`, a restore (checkout, reset, merge, stash, clone) refuses or reports case variants only where the worktree folds case (`restoreCaseOptions`, from the worktree's new `foldsCase`, which looks without writing); on a filesystem that keeps case a file beside a directory of another case checks out as native git checks it out, and an untracked file in another case of a path the switch replaces is refused as in the way unless it is that path's own file (its stat matches the index entry, as git's `icase_exists`). `foldsCase` looks at the worktree's own entries, and at the git directory only when it lies inside the worktree.
- f1cab9d: Path checkouts and resets write the index git writes. `reset -N` (with or without paths) and `apply -N` store the empty blob their intent-to-add entries name, so native `git status` and `git write-tree` read the result. A stage-0 entry that `reset <tree-ish> -- <paths>`, `checkout <tree-ish> -- <paths>` or `restore --source` adds replaces only the stage-0 entries it clashes with as a file or a directory (git's `has_file_name`/`has_dir_name`): conflict stages stay, so `restore` then refuses the unmerged path as git does. No-overlay removals prune the emptied directories of a file already gone (never beneath a symbolic link) and warn where git's `unlink`/`rmdir` fails ("unable to rmdir 'sm': Directory not empty", "unable to unlink 'p': Is a directory"). `checkout -m`/`--conflict` and `restore -m` merge with the `merge` attribute's driver (union, binary, `-merge`, registered drivers) and `conflict-marker-size`, through the same `ll_merge` as the merges. A blob missing partway through a path checkout is reported for its path ("unable to read sha1 file of ..."), the other paths are still checked out and the index written, and the result carries git's exit status 255 (`CheckoutResult.exitCode`).
- bd2140b: **Performance pass: object headers without resolving deltas, one-pass inflate in index-pack, one merge-base walk per merge.**
  
  - A packed object's type and size come from its entry headers, as git's `oid_object_info` reads them: a delta's size from the start of its instructions, its type from the whole entry at the bottom of its chain, nothing applied or cached (`PackFile.header`/`objectType`, `PackSet.header`/`objectType`, the optional `GitObjects.header`/`objectType`). `objectHeader`, `hasObjectOfType` (every typed store's `has`), `Blobs.size` and pack writing's planning read them before falling back to `get`, so checks such as a checkout's `has` before each blob, blame's size checks and the planning of a fetch's pack no longer resolve whole delta chains. A header that cannot answer (a REF_DELTA base outside the pack, an unreadable entry) leaves the object to `get`, which reports errors as before.
  - index-pack inflates an entry whose zlib stream lies whole in the chunk at hand in one pass (`inflateWhole`) instead of through the streaming inflater, reads entry header bytes without an await per byte, and feeds the pack hash and the entry CRC in runs; phase 2 inflates re-read entries the same way. Streams that cross a chunk, fail or inflate to another size take the streaming path, which reports the same errors as before. A clone's or fetch's pack import is about 20% faster.
  - `merge` decides "already up to date" and fast-forward from the merge bases it computes anyway (git's `cmd_merge`), instead of two ancestry walks that, without a commit-graph, read the whole history when the merged branch is not an ancestor.
- 7dc8308: **Faster `git diff` output, status untracked scans and `commit -a` of small files.**
  
  ```typescript
  // one worktree operation for the whole output: attributes and config read once
  const patch = await git.diff().setOldTree("main~999").setNewTree("main").callText();
  ```
  
  `DiffCommand.callBytes`/`callText`, `callDiffstat` and `callFormats` run as one worktree operation
  (`Worktree.batch`), as `git log` already ran its diffs: an attributes lookup outside an
  operation opened one of its own, re-reading the configuration, `info/attributes` and the
  `.gitattributes` files once per file printed. The untracked scan passes over the tracked files
  of a directory listing without a promise or a result per file. A small object stored from a
  stream (a worktree file of at most 64 KiB, as `add` and `commit -a` store them) is gathered and
  compressed in one synchronous call instead of through a compression stream; `GitObjects.put`
  copies each streamed chunk it keeps, so a source may refill one buffer for every chunk, and
  closes the source when the store fails before reading it to its end.
- 9234799: **receive-pack advertises as git, commands surface alternates errors, local clones copy objects, alternates' commit-graphs are read.**
  
  - receive-pack (over a duplex and smart HTTP's `info/refs?service=git-receive-pack`) advertises only the refs under `refs/` (git's `write_head_info`): no `HEAD`, which upload-pack still advertises; an empty repository, or one whose refs are all hidden, sends `capabilities^{}`. The connectivity check still counts HEAD's history as present (`--all`). New `receivePackRefs`.
  - `GitCommand.alternatesErrors()`: the `error: ...` lines git prints on stderr when it reads the repository's alternates (a missing directory, a path that cannot be normalized, nesting too deep), once per command; fsck folds them into `errorOutput`/`findings` and gc into `GcResult.errors`, first.
  - `git clone <path>` follows `clone_local`: the source's objects directory is copied through the FilesApi (loose objects, packs, `info/packs`, the commit-graph byte for byte; no hard links: the FilesApi has no link primitive), its `objects/info/alternates` entries carried over as absolute paths, the refs listed through the transport and written without fetching. `setLocal(false)` (`--no-local`) fetches; `setLocal(true)` warns for a URL ("--local is ignored") or a shallow source (clone.c's check of the source's own git directory: a linked worktree of a shallow repository is still copied, as git copies it); `setNoHardlinks` is accepted; `--depth`, `--shallow-since` and `--shallow-exclude` are ignored for local clones with git's warnings. Clones keep the received pack whatever its size (git's `TRANS_OPT_KEEP`; new `keepPack` fetch and `importPack` option). `beforeFetch` may return `completeTips`.
  - The commit-graph is the first one found among the repository's objects directory and its alternates' (git's `prepare_commit_graph`), and fsck verifies the graph of every object directory (`commit-graph verify --object-dir` per source). Each layer of a split chain is looked up in every object directory, the repository's own first (git's `load_commit_graph_chain_fd_st`): a chain written over an alternate's layers loads (`readCommitGraph(files, dir, settings, objectDirs)`, `verifyCommitGraph`'s `objectDirs`). `ObjectAlternates.onChange` reports every later `add()`/`refresh()`, and the files backend invalidates its commit-graph on each.
- 65e2515: **Server-side parity: a smart-HTTP server over served repositories, the quarantine path for hooks, and local remotes from bare repositories.**
  
  - **vcs-transport:** `createServedHttpHandler` / `createServedFetchHandler` serve smart HTTP from the same `ServedRepository` resolver `serveGitConnection` uses: the repository's hooks run for a push (through `servedRepositoryOf`: pre-receive, update, post-receive, post-update, push-to-checkout, and reference-transaction through `around`, which now lasts until the push has ended), settings are layered as `serveGitService` layers them (`resolveServeSettings`), and services are enabled as git's `http-backend` enables them (`http.uploadpack`, `http.receivepack` for authenticated users only by default via `remoteUser`, `http.getanyfile`; refusals are an empty 403, unknown paths 404, the wrong method 405). `createFetchHandler` is unchanged. `ReceivePackHookContext.quarantinePath` names the push's quarantine directory while its objects are quarantined; `RepositoryQuarantine.path` carries it. `ReceivePackServerOptions` takes `relayPostReceive`, `batchNonAtomic`, `interleaveUpdateHook`, `publishBeforeUpdate` and `preReceiveSeesRefused` (pre-receive given every command, refused ones included, as git's `run_receive_hook` with `skip_broken` 0), as `serveOverDuplex` does. `configBoolOf` reads a boolean of a served repository's config. The handlers' context takes `done`, the promise of the whole run.
  - **vcs-core:** `ObjectQuarantine.path`: the quarantine's directory, where the backend keeps one in files.
  - **vcs-store-files:** the files quarantine reports its `objects/tmp_objdir-incoming-<id>` directory; `openAdmin()` of a bare repository gives `bareRepository` (open other repositories by path from the git directory, `FETCH_HEAD`).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `WorktreeAdmin.bareRepository`.
  - **vcs-commands:** receive hooks get `GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` and `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` (tmp-objdir.c's `tmp_objdir_env`) during pre-receive, in local, duplex-served and HTTP-served pushes, so a hook running native git reads the pushed objects; pre-receive sees commands refused before it (a hidden ref) as git's does. A bare repository's working copy (`Git.fromWorktreeAdmin`) fetches, pushes, lists and clones through local remotes (relative paths read from its git directory), writing `FETCH_HEAD` in its git directory. `remoteHookSession` (a served repository's hooks, for `servedRepositoryOf`) and `quarantineEnv` are exported.
  - **Served HTTP hardening (vcs-transport):** the dumb protocol (`getAnyFile`) is off by default (http-backend's is on; no object files are served here); turned on, `HEAD` and `info/refs` are refused where upload-pack is not served and `info/refs` leaves out the refs upload-pack hides. An operator's `enableUploadPack` / `enableReceivePack` / `getAnyFile` set to `false` wins over a repository's `http.*=true` (left unset, the config still decides, as in git). The repository path is percent-decoded segment by segment (an encoded `/`, `\` or NUL is a 404) before `isUnsafeServicePath`, so repositories named with spaces or non-ASCII letters are reachable. `remoteAddr` joins `remoteUser`; both reach receive-pack's hooks (`ReceivePackHookContext.remoteUser` / `remoteAddr`, `HttpHandlerContext`) and `ServedRepository.around` (a third argument, `ServedRequestContext`); `ReceivePackConfig.updateWorktree` is given the hook context. An `around` that returns without serving is a 500; a 500 the served handler answers for a thrown error, and `createHttpHandler`'s when a handler throws (a behaviour change: it carried the exception's message), has the fixed body `Internal server error`, the detail going to the logger; a throwing `remoteUser` is a 500. A delete of a ref that does not exist joins the push's ref transaction (git's `did_not_exist`), so the reference-transaction hook hears `<zero> <zero> <ref>`, still kept from post-receive and post-update (local, duplex-served and HTTP pushes).
  - **vcs-commands:** receive hooks of an HTTP push get `REMOTE_USER`, `REMOTE_ADDR` and, where the host sets no committer, `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=<user>` / `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=<user>@http.<addr>` (http-backend's `run_service`), the reference-transaction and push-to-checkout hooks included; `httpBackendEnv` exported, `HookSession.hostCommitter`.
- 68cedb7: **Faster one-shot inflate of small objects; fsck's grafts hint with a commit-graph and its links limit.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** `inflateWhole` builds a dynamic block's Huffman tables about a third faster (the header's reader buckets the symbols by code length as it decodes the lengths, so the builder does not sort them, and the root table is filled by doubling as libdeflate does instead of one write per entry), refills its bit buffer with four bytes at once without a branch and decodes two literals per refill, copies overlapping matches by doubling, and sums the Adler-32 word by word from 32 bytes on. Small objects inflate about 10–30% faster (commits about 17%, trees about 13% in an independent measurement), large ones up to 20%; the result is byte for byte the same and fails where zlib fails (differential tests against node zlib on git objects of every type, levels 0 to 9, every strategy, and damaged ones).
  - **vcs-core:** a `VerifyCommitSource` gets a second argument, `note`, which adds a line to the verification's messages where the commit is read.
  - **vcs-commands:** `fsck` with a commit-graph, `core.commitGraph` on and `info/grafts` prints the grafts deprecation hint twice, as git does (once more from `git commit-graph verify`, when it parses its first commit). `setMemoryLimits({ links })` is a limit on the links kept in all (the pack-order scan's and the walk's tree records together), the arrays never grow past it, and 0 keeps none.
- 479fa64: **A cheaper start for status and `commit -a`; checkout folds case in `verify_clean_subdirectory`.**
  
  - **vcs-working-tree:** an index read no longer hashes the file to check its trailer: git checks it only in `fsck` (read-cache.c `verify_hdr` with `verify_index_checksum`, which fsck alone sets), and never for a null trailer (`index.skipHash`). `parseIndexFile` takes `verifyChecksum` (default true, a null trailer accepted), `IndexFile` and `readIndexFileEntries` take `verifyChecksum` (default false), and `readSharedIndex` hashes a shared index only when asked. An index with a wrong trailer is read as git reads it.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `createGitFilesBackend` loads the config sequence once for the pack and ref store settings (it loaded it twice), `readPackSetSettings` takes a loaded `config`, and the loose object store draws its random temporary-name part at its first write, so a store opened to read never loads the runtime's crypto.
  - **vcs-commands:** `fsck` checks each index's trailing hash (and a split index's shared index). The index host works out `splitIndex.sharedIndexExpire` only when a write expires shared indexes. On a 15k-file repository the open before status and `commit -a` takes about 10 ms less.
  - **vcs-commands:** under `core.ignorecase`, checkout's `verify_clean_subdirectory` (`findInTheWay` with `caseVariantsExpendable`) takes a file the index has in another case as tracked, as git's `read_directory` asks the index through its folded name hashes: a directory a file replaces no longer refuses the checkout because of such a variant, which goes with the directory (git's `checkout_entry` removes the subtree). New files there still refuse it, and a nested repository is still asked exactly (kept, refusing the checkout). `untrackedUnder` takes the folded `IndexPathLookup` and collects the variants; `InTheWay.caseVariants` lists them.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** corrupt index files are refused as git dies on them: `parseIndexFile` throws `IndexCorruptError` (git's stderr `lines`, `exitCode` 128) for a bad signature or version (`verify_hdr`), a wrong trailer under `verifyChecksum` (`error: bad index file sha1 signature`, `fatal: index file corrupt`), unknown extended flags and malformed version 4 names (`create_from_disk`), and for entry counts, entries, names and varints that run past the entries (git reads past its mapping there). A version 2/3 entry is sized as `ondisk_ce_size` whatever the byte after its name holds; a broken or missing shared index throws `IndexCorruptError` with git's `fatal:` line.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `index.skipHash` (`IndexSettings.skipHash`, default `feature.manyFiles`, read by the commands' index host) writes the index with a null trailer, as git's `do_write_index` does, in whole, sparse and split index files alike; a new shared index is still hashed (git names it `sharedindex.0000…` and loses its entries). `encodeIndexFileBytes` and `encodeIndexInOrder` take `skipHash`. A staging tells null-trailer index files apart by their content's hash (`indexFileIdentity`), so a change native git makes is still seen.
  - **vcs-commands:** `fsck` on a corrupt index (the current one, a linked worktree's, or a split index's shared index) ends with git's lines and exit 128 instead of rejecting, and hashes each index once.
- 8a603c0: **Clean never removes a repository it cannot check; review fixes for status, clean and renames.**
  
  - **vcs-store-files (data loss fix):** `isRepository` takes a gitfile whose target's HEAD cannot be checked (reached through a symbolic link, unreadable) as a repository, as git keeps it: `clean -d`, `-dx` and `-Xd` no longer remove such a nested worktree. A `.git` link is kept too. A submodule whose gitdir cannot be read is shown clean (not a fatal error); only a confirmed missing HEAD is git's fatal error.
  - **vcs-commands:** `clean -X -d` removes ignored directories that are empty or hold only empty directories, and removes a directory holding a `.git` that is no repository whole (dry run and real run agree, as git). `clean -d` without `-x` no longer removes empty directories inside an ignored directory.
  - **vcs-commands:** stash apply never pairs empty files as renames (git's merge-ort); status still does, as git status. `detectRenames` takes `renameEmpty`, `size` (contents are read only when compared and not kept) and `onRenameLimit`; a rename limit of 0 or less is git's 32767. New `Status.warnings`: git's "exhaustive rename detection was skipped" text when the rename limit stopped inexact detection.
  - **vcs-commands, vcs-working-tree:** status does not list files inside a deinitialised submodule's directory (`findUntrackedFiles` takes `isGitlink`); a submodule's own `status.showUntrackedFiles=no` (any false value) hides its untracked files, as git.
- eb5ad83: **`git status` is faster and keeps git's untracked cache (`UNTR`).**
  
  - The stat pass compares each index entry with its file synchronously where the stat settles it (`isCleanByStat`, `compareWithWorktreeStat`), so only racy, changed or submodule entries cost a promise; stat entries are plain objects with their name and ignore flag as prototype getters, and the Node FilesApi converts an lstat with fewer BigInt operations.
  - Untracked files are found by `scanUntracked` (git's `read_directory` for `-uall`): index lookups by binary search (`IndexPathLookup`), no nested-repository check for directories the index has, no sort per directory.
  - The untracked cache is read, used, updated and written as git does (dir.c: ident, dir flags, `info/exclude` and `core.excludesFile` ids and stats, per-directory stat data and `.gitignore` ids, valid/check-only bitmaps), byte for byte, for `status.showUntrackedFiles=all`; `core.untrackedCache` (true / false / keep, `feature.manyFiles`), `GIT_DISABLE_UNTRACKED_CACHE` and `GIT_FORCE_UNTRACKED_CACHE` are honoured. Index writes keep the extension and invalidate the directories of the paths they add or remove, as `untracked_cache_invalidate_path` does (it used to be dropped).
  - New: `Worktree.untrackedScanHost`, `Staging.getUntrackedCache` / `setUntrackedCache`, `refreshStats(updates, { untrackedCache })`, `LinkStats.statSize`, `listKinds(path, { readdirOrder })`, and `GitHostEnvironment.systemName` / `statFile` (set by `createNodeGitHostEnvironment`).
- eb5ad83: **`git status` pairs worktree renames with unmerged paths as git does.**
  
  An unmerged path takes part in the worktree side's rename detection as `diff-files` queues it: its file, when there, is a rename destination (a worktree deletion paired with it is no longer shown), and its stage #2, when the file is gone, is a source (an intent-to-add file paired with it shows ` R`). Porcelain v2 prints the unmerged (`u`) lines after the changed ones, as git does.
- c80355f: **Tree entries are sorted exactly as git sorts them, so some tree ids change to git's.** A directory `a` next to a file `a-` (or any file whose name continues `a` with a byte below `/`, such as `a!` or `a.`) used to compare equal to it: the order of the two in the encoded tree then followed the order the entries came in, and a tree built from a different order got an id native git never writes (and `git fsck` reports as not properly sorted). Now a tree compares as its name plus `/` in every case (git's `base_name_compare`), so `a-`, `a.b`, then the tree `a`, then `a0`, whatever the input order. Trees that were written in git's order before keep their ids; a tree written out of order before is written with a new id (git's) when it is built again.
  
  - **vcs-core:** `compareTreeEntries` and `compareTreeEntryNames` (exported) are git's tree order over UTF-8 bytes; `encodeTreeEntries`, `serializeTree` and the structural tree deltas use them (the deltas sorted with `localeCompare` before).
  - **vcs-store-sql:** the native tree store sorts and hashes trees with the same comparator (it compared UTF-16 strings).
  - **vcs-commands:** patch ids walk trees in that order.
- 7dc8308: **`status` folds case under `core.ignorecase`; a diff of two trees reads no index attributes.**
  
  ```typescript
  // core.ignorecase=true, a/b/two.txt tracked, a/b/TWO.txt in the worktree
  (await git.status().call()).untracked.has("a/b/TWO.txt"); // false, as git status
  ```
  
  The untracked scan asks the index as dir.c does through its name hashes: under
  `core.ignorecase` a file whose name differs from an index path only by case (a skip-worktree
  entry's included) is tracked, and a directory differing from a tracked one only by case is
  entered rather than shown whole (`index_file_exists` with icase,
  `directory_exists_in_index_icase`); the untracked cache records the same names as native git.
  `IndexPathLookup` takes `{ ignoreCase }`, and `UntrackedScanOptions.isTrackedExactly` keeps
  `index_name_is_other`'s exact check for ignored paths.
  
  A diff of two trees (`git diff A B`, `log -p`, `show`, `format-patch`) takes the diff drivers'
  in-tree `.gitattributes` from the worktree alone, as builtin/diff.c, which reads no index
  there; the diffs against the index or the worktree still fall back to the index's files.
- 190f0e1: **`add`, `clean` and `stash -u`/`-a` fold case under `core.ignorecase`; `apply`, `am` and `reset -- <paths>` run as one worktree operation.**
  
  ```typescript
  // core.ignorecase=true: a/b/two.txt tracked, a/b/TWO.txt and A/y.txt in the worktree
  (await git.clean().setDryRun(true).call()).cleaned; // Set { "A/y.txt" }, as git clean -n
  await git.add().addFilepattern(".").call(); // stages A/y.txt as a/y.txt, as git add
  ```
  
  The untracked walks of `git add`, `git clean`, `git stash -u`/`-a` (its untracked tree and
  the clean after it) ask the index as dir.c does through its name hashes, as `status` already
  did: a file whose name differs from an index path only by case (a skip-worktree entry's
  included) is tracked, so it is neither listed, added, stashed nor removed, and a directory
  differing from a tracked one only by case is entered rather than taken whole
  (`index_file_exists` with icase, `directory_exists_in_index_icase`, `dir_add_name`);
  `index_name_is_other` stays exact. `git add` also spells a new file's leading directories as
  the index does (`adjust_dirname_case`), forgetting a directory whose entries the same
  `add -A` removed first, as name-hash.c's `remove_dir_entry` (a dry run removes nothing):
  `IndexDirnameCase.forget`. `stash push -u -- A` with `A/` a case variant of a tracked `a/`
  saves and removes `A/y.txt` and succeeds, where native git saves the stash and then fails
  its reverse apply, leaving the file (a deliberate divergence). `IndexPathLookup` takes `gitlinks` and answers
  `indexDirectory` (git's `directory_exists_in_index`), and `untrackedIndexPredicates` gives
  `findUntrackedFiles` the same answers.
  
  `git apply`, `git am` and `git reset [<tree>] -- <paths>` run inside `Worktree.batch`, so the
  configuration and the attributes files are read once per command instead of once per file
  converted or refreshed (2000 changed files: `apply --index` 4.3 s → 1.6 s, `reset -- .`
  1.0 s → 0.19 s).
  
  The files `git apply` writes are converted by the attributes as read before its writes: a
  `.gitattributes` the patch adds or changes no longer converts the files written with it
  (apply.c converts through convert.c's attribute stack, built on its first check and kept).
  `Worktree.batch` and `WorktreeContentFilter.batch` take `OperationOptions`
  (`keepAttributes`), which nested batches pass on.
