## 0.5.0

### Minor Changes

- 7d77bf8: **`git.clean()` as `git clean`; the index refresh is compare-and-set; submodule gitfiles.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fix):** `clean().setCleanDirectories(true)` no longer removes tracked directories, staged new files, nested repositories or ignored files. As `git clean -fd`: untracked files are removed one at a time; an untracked directory (no tracked or staged path in it) is entered only with `-d` or a path filter and removed once empty, deepest first, never recursively; nested repositories are skipped unless the new `setForceNestedRepositories(true)` (`-ff`); ignored files only with `setIgnore(false)` (`-x`); the index decides what is tracked (not HEAD). `cleaned` lists what `git clean -n` prints ("Would remove", a wholly removed directory as `dir/`), the new `skipped` the "Would skip repository" lines.
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-store-files (index refresh):** status and stash refresh entries compare-and-set (`applyStatUpdate`): only a stage-0 entry still holding the compared id, mode, size and mtime changes, the rest of it (flags such as assume-unchanged) kept; an add made meanwhile is never undone. An index file is refreshed by the new optional `Staging.refreshStats` (`GitStaging`, `FileStagingStore`): read afresh, written under `index.lock` only while it is the index this instance read or wrote, without writing other in-memory edits, and memory changed only once the write succeeded. `isOutdated()` compares the index's checksum, not its mtime. Index entries keep nanoseconds, uid and gid (`StagingEntry.mtimeNs`/`ctimeNs`/`uid`/`gid`), and refreshed entries record the file's exact stat (`LinkStats`/`WorktreeEntry` gain `mtimeNs`, `ctimeNs`, `dev`, `ino`, `uid`, `gid`; the Node FilesApi reads them with a bigint `lstat`), so native git trusts them.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `readGitlinkHead` resolves `gitdir: ../.git/modules/sub` (as `git submodule add` writes it) against the submodule's directory, refusing a path above the FilesApi's root, and reads only regular files (never a FIFO).
  - **vcs-utils / vcs-utils-node:** new optional `PosixFilesApi.rmdir` (an empty directory only, never recursive), implemented by the Node, simulated-links and mounted FilesApis; `FileWorktree.removeEmptyDirectory` uses it, and no directory pruning falls back to a recursive removal.
- 7d77bf8: **Clean never enters tracked paths; git's racy smudging on every index write; exact nanoseconds.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fix):** `clean()` never enters a directory standing where the index has a file, link or submodule (a file replaced by a directory, a deinitialised submodule's directory), as git; a path filter removes the untracked directory it names as `git clean -f -- u` does (`u/`), globs (`*.txt`) match as git's pathspecs, a nested repository named by a path filter is passed over silently, and a directory whose `.git` is no repository (an empty `.git`) is removed with `-d`, as git (new optional `Worktree.isRepository`).
  - **vcs-working-tree / vcs-store-files (racy git):** every index write of `GitStaging` / `FileStagingStore` applies git's `ce_smudge_racily_clean_entry`: an entry as read whose mtime second is not older than the index file's, and whose content changed behind a matching stat, is written with size 0 (new `Staging.setRacyCheck`, installed by every command from the worktree; `smudgeRacilyClean`). The index time is the index file's own mtime after every read and write (0, every entry racy, when unknown; never "now"). Entries a refresh proved clean are recorded as they are, not smudged.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `lstat` builds millisecond times from the nanosecond parts exactly (a ~1.8e18 nanosecond time no longer drifts below its millisecond), so the index records the file's nanoseconds exactly.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a submodule `gitdir:` file pointing where there is no repository, or beyond the FilesApi's root, makes status fail with git's message ("fatal: gitfile does not point to a valid repository: sub/.git") instead of reporting the submodule clean.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** stash push keeps the index's commit for a submodule (as git stash), even when the submodule checked out another one.
  
  **Exact index times.** The index writer records the exact nanoseconds a file's stat carried, and takes the seconds from them: a time in milliseconds (a double) cannot hold nanoseconds at today's dates and can round across a millisecond or a second. Status takes whole seconds the same way.
- 875db86: **Clone and fetch leave what git leaves: remote and upstream configuration, `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD`, `FETCH_HEAD`; shallow clones on the KV store. Breaking: `CloneCommand.setBranch` no longer implies `--single-branch`.**
  
  - **vcs-commands, `CloneCommand`:** writes the configuration `git clone` writes: `remote.<name>.url`, `remote.<name>.tagOpt = --no-tags` for `setNoTags()` and for a mirror (as git 2.49+, whose `--mirror` implies `--no-tags`), the `remote.<name>.fetch` refspec of the clone (`+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/<name>/*`; one branch or tag for a single-branch clone; none for a bare clone; `+refs/*:refs/*` with `remote.<name>.mirror = true` for a mirror), and `branch.<b>.remote` / `branch.<b>.merge` for the checked-out branch (an empty remote's unborn branch included). `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD` becomes a symbolic ref to the tracking ref of the remote's default branch when the clone took it. `setRemote(name)` (`--origin`) names the remote in all of it. The configuration is saved through the working copy's config (`.git/config` for the files backend), and native git reads it: `git status` shows the upstream and `git pull` works in our clone.
  - **vcs-commands, breaking:** `CloneCommand.setBranch(b)` is git's `--branch`: every branch is still cloned and `b` (or a tag `b`, checked out detached) is set up. New `setSingleBranch(true)` (`--single-branch`) restricts the clone to it, as before; a shallow clone and `setBranchesToClone` (with `setCloneAllBranches(false)`) stay single-branch unless `setSingleBranch(false)`.
  - **vcs-commands, `FetchCommand`:** writes `FETCH_HEAD` in git's format through the checkout (`<oid>\t[not-for-merge]\t<description>`, `branch 'main' of <url>`, `tag 'v1' of <url>`, the URL without credentials or `.git`), the refs to merge first: every ref of explicit refspecs, or with configured refspecs the current branch's `branch.<b>.merge` refs (fetched for this alone when no refspec takes them), or, when the current branch has no upstream at all, the first ref of an exact first refspec; a ref named twice without destination is listed twice; tags (auto-followed ones by name) and the rest are `not-for-merge`. Byte-identical to native `git fetch` for the same fetch. New `setAppend(true)` (`--append`) and `setWriteFetchHead(false)` (`--no-write-fetch-head`); FETCH_HEAD is truncated when the fetch starts (a failed fetch leaves it empty); a dry run writes nothing. Without `setTagOpt`, a configured remote's `remote.<name>.tagOpt` decides; a configured remote without `fetch` lines fetches the current branch's merge refs when it is that branch's upstream, else its HEAD only, as git (the default refspec is no longer filled in).
  - **vcs-commands:** new `git.remoteSetHead()` (`RemoteSetHeadCommand`, `git remote set-head <name> <branch>` / `-d`). `fetch --prune` keeps `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD` (a symbolic ref), as git.
  - **vcs-transport:** `FetchClientResult.fetched` lists every remote ref a fetch took, with or without a local ref, in git's FETCH_HEAD order (refspec matches, then auto-followed tags: those of fetched or present objects first, then those found in the fetched history), each with the index of the refspec that selected it (`PlannedRef.refspec`). New `formatFetchHead`, `parseFetchHead`, `fetchHeadUrl` and `anonymizeUrl` (git's transport_anonymize_url).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Checkout` has optional `readFetchHead()` / `writeFetchHead(content, { append })`; `MemoryCheckout` keeps it in memory.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitCheckout` reads and writes `.git/FETCH_HEAD`.
  - **vcs-store-kv:** `KVRefStore.getShallow()` / `setShallow(commits, expected)`, with the `expected` check of the other stores (one compare-and-swap of a `shallow:root` key), so shallow fetches and clones work on the KV store.
- d45dfb0: **A non-bare clone checks out its files, as `git clone` does.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `CloneCommand` writes the files of the checked-out commit into the worktree, not only the index: contents, the executable bit and symbolic links where the worktree can represent them, an empty directory for each submodule (gitlink), and the index with the size and mtime of every file written, so native `git status` reports a clean tree right after the clone. The tree is planned (every blob checked) before the first file is written. A symlink becomes a plain file holding its target where the worktree cannot make links or `core.symlinks` is false; the index keeps mode 120000. When the worktree cannot represent executables or symlinks, the clone sets `core.filemode` / `core.symlinks` to false, as `git init` does after probing the filesystem. `setNoCheckout(true)` writes no files and leaves the index empty (git writes no `.git/index`); a bare clone writes neither. `planTreeRestore` (internal, shared with `reset --hard` and `stash apply`) takes `{ symlinks: true }` and reports the tree's gitlinks.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Worktree` gains the optional `capabilities` (`WorktreeCapabilities`: `executable`, `symlinks`); undefined means both. `WorktreeWriteOptions.mode` documents that mode `0o120000` makes the content the link target.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree` writes mode `0o120000` as a link where its FilesApi can make one (else as a plain file holding the target), chmods regular and executable files with their permission bits (`0o644`/`0o755`, was the whole git mode), and reports `capabilities` (see the changeset on symbolic links for what it requires of the FilesApi).
- d45dfb0: **Links only through the FilesApi contract; checkout keeps local changes as git; workspace sync records links as 120000 entries.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** the `PosixFilesApi` contract is spelled out (links, beneath-link refusal, modes, `pathSeparators`, case folding) and gains `refusesBeyondLinks` and `openLinkTarget`. New portable `createSymlinkFilesApi(base, { pathSeparators, ignoreCase, precomposeUnicode, defaultFileMode })`: simulated links and permission bits over any FilesApi, kept in a side table in the reserved hidden file `/.webrun-symlinks.json` (`SYMLINK_TABLE_PATH`), with simulated case and Unicode folding for tests. New `createMountedFilesApi(base, mounts)` and `createSubtreeFilesApi(base, root)`, passing the link operations through. All re-exported by vcs-core.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `PosixNodeFilesApi.write` replaces files with webrun-files' `replaceFile` semantics (Windows retry on EPERM/EACCES/EBUSY; in-place copy over a mount point or across devices, never through a link) and `writeBatched` (short writes repeated, small chunks batched); `createExclusive` too. `chmod` masks with `0o777` (no setuid/setgid/sticky) and works on a descriptor opened without following links. `read` opens with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and checks the descriptor is a regular file (a FIFO is never read). New `openLinkTarget` (a FilesApi at a link's target, for a `.git` linked elsewhere). Directories verified as not-links are cached per instance (cleared on its own removes and moves), and `list` streams in path order (`listInPathOrder`) instead of buffering the tree. New dependency on `@statewalker/webrun-files`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a `.git` or `.git/objects` that is a symbolic link is followed as git follows it: `resolveLinkedGitDir(files, gitDir)` resolves it through the FilesApi (`openLinkTarget`, or `readlink` within it) and mounts the target; `createGitFilesBackend` uses it. Ref, packed-refs and reflog readers rethrow `BeyondSymlinkError` instead of reading "no ref" (an empty repository). `FileWorktree` skips its own leading-component checks over a FilesApi with `refusesBeyondLinks`, and a forced write (`replaceInTheWay`) also removes an untracked directory where a file must go.
  - **vcs-commands:**
    - **Breaking (behaviour):** a branch checkout without `setForced` is git's two-way merge: a path HEAD and the target agree on keeps its index entry and worktree file (unstaged edits survive); a path they differ on is switched only when index and worktree hold HEAD's version; otherwise the checkout is refused (`status` CONFLICTS, new `CheckoutResult.message` with git's text: "Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by checkout", "The following untracked working tree files would be overwritten by checkout", "you need to resolve your current index first"). A plain checkout between identical trees no longer rewrites the worktree; `setForced(true)` does, as `git checkout -f`.
    - With `core.ignorecase`, files differing only in case are both checked out (the last wins) and reported in the new `CloneResult.collidedPaths`, as git's "the following paths have collided" warning; only a path beneath a file, link or submodule of another case is refused.
    - `stash apply` writes with `replaceInTheWay`, as `reset --hard` does.
    - `InitCommand` resolves a linked `.git` as clone does.
  - **vcs-workspace (behaviour change):** a symbolic link in a synced folder is recorded as a mode 120000 entry whose content is its target (never followed), as git records it, instead of being skipped (which made peers delete it); written back as a link where the folder's FilesApi can make one, else as a file holding the target (git's `core.symlinks=false`). An `lstat` error fails the walk instead of dropping the file. Special files stay skipped (`special-file`). `FolderFile.link`, `WalkedFile.link`, `folderHasSymlinks`; the `symbolic-link` skip reason is gone.
- d45dfb0: **Breaking: no operation follows a symbolic link; git's Windows path rules on Windows worktrees; the core CheckoutCommand is gone.**
  
  - **vcs-core:** `isValidWorktreePath(path, mode?, { windows? })` / `assertValidWorktreePath`: with `windows: true` (a worktree on Windows) git's Windows rules apply too, as its `verify_path` built for Windows: `\` refused (CVE-2019-1354, `d\post-checkout` would land inside `d`), and `is_valid_win32_path` (drive prefix, control characters and `<>:"|?*`, components ending in a space or a dot, reserved device names `CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`, `LPT1`, `CONIN$`...). Without it `a\b.txt` is an ordinary file name, as git on Linux and macOS; `.git` behind a backslash (`.git\x`) is still refused everywhere (git's `is_ntfs_dotgit`). New `WorktreePathOptions`. New `isGitWorktreeFile(component, "gitmodules" | "gitattributes" | "gitignore" | "mailmap")` (git's `is_hfs_dot*` / `is_ntfs_dot*`, with the NTFS fallback short names `gi7eba~N`, `gi7d29~N`, `gi250a~N`, `maba30~N`); a symlinked `.gitmodules` is refused under all of them.
  - **vcs-utils:** new `BeyondSymlinkError` / `isBeyondSymlinkError`; `PosixFilesApi.lstat` throws it for a path beneath a link (never "absent"). New optional `PosixFilesApi.pathSeparators` (`"/\\"` on Windows).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `PosixNodeFilesApi` (from `createNodeFilesApi`) never follows a link in any operation: read, write, createExclusive, mkdir, list (recursive too), stats, exists, remove, move, copy, chmod, symlink, lstat, readlink all refuse a path beneath a link (`BeyondSymlinkError`; `exists` answers false). A link at the path is a link: listed and stat'ed with its own size, read as nothing, never listed into, replaced by a write (new file written beside it and renamed over the link itself; a regular file it replaces passes on its permission bits, so a new file gets a new inode), unlinked by remove, copied and moved as a link. Directories are created without following a linked component. On Windows (`platform` option, default `process.platform`) a `\` in a path is refused and `pathSeparators` is `"/\\"` (`"/"` elsewhere).
  - **vcs-store-files:**
    - `FileWorktree` no longer chmods to fixed bits: an executable gets x where r is set (`mode | ((mode & 0o444) >> 2)`) and a regular file loses x, from the file's own mode, so the umask holds.
    - `readContent` / `computeHash` refuse a path beneath a link; `getEntry` / `exists` report it absent; `remove` of it returns false (git's `has_symlink_or_noent_leading_path`).
    - New `WorktreeWriteOptions.replaceInTheWay` (forced checkout: a file or link where a parent directory must go is unlinked, as git's `create_directories` with force).
    - FIFOs, sockets and devices are skipped by `walk` and are no `getEntry`.
    - New `probeCaseFolding(files, gitDir)` / `FileWorktree.probeCaseFolding()` (git init's `CoNfIg` and NFC/NFD probes; only over a FilesApi with `lstat`).
    - `GitResolutionStore` reads and writes worktree files through a `FileWorktree` (new optional `worktree` constructor argument): a link is staged as a link by `markAllResolved` and replaced, not written through, by `acceptOurs` / `acceptTheirs` / `applyRecordedResolution`.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Worktree.probeCaseFolding?()`; `WorktreeWriteOptions.replaceInTheWay`; `WorktreeCapabilities.windowsPaths?` (set by `FileWorktree` from its FilesApi's `pathSeparators`: checkout and the restore plans, via `planTreeRestore`'s new `windows` option, then apply git's Windows path rules).
  - **vcs-commands:**
    - Removed the unexported core `CheckoutCommand` (`core-commands/checkout.command*.ts`), which wrote and deleted through raw FilesApi calls; nothing used it.
    - The porcelain `CheckoutCommand` writes a branch checkout through the restore plan, worktree first: paths verified and collisions refused before writing, tracked files the target drops removed, links written as links (or files holding the target), executables, an empty directory per submodule; then HEAD and the index.
    - `reset --hard` and `stash apply` restore symbolic links (as links, or as files holding the target where the worktree or `core.symlinks` cannot have them) instead of refusing them; deletions go first; `reset --hard` unlinks a file or link where a directory must go.
    - `writeTreeRestorePlan` takes `{ symlinks, replaceInTheWay, gitlinks }`.
    - `CloneCommand` and `InitCommand` write `core.ignorecase = true` / `core.precomposeunicode = true` when the probe finds the filesystem folds case or Unicode composition (git writes each only then on Linux; on macOS it also writes `precomposeunicode = false`, which we do not).
  - **vcs-workspace:** `walkFolder` / `snapshotFolder` never read or descend a symbolic link or a special file (over a FilesApi with `lstat`, e.g. `createNodeFilesApi`): they are listed in the new `FolderWalk.special` and reported as skipped with the new reasons `symbolic-link` / `special-file`.
- 01d2219: **Every ref and reflog write goes through git's locks; the unlocked writers are gone (breaking).** `vcs-store-files` no longer exports `writeRef`, `writeObjectRef`, `writeSymbolicRef`, `deleteRef`, `updateRef` (`refs/ref-writer.ts`) or `appendReflog`, `deleteReflog` (`refs/reflog-writer.ts`, removed; `hasReflog` moved to `reflog-reader.ts`). They wrote loose refs and reflogs with plain writes. Use `FileRefStore` instead: `set`, `setSymbolic`, `remove`, `batch`, `compareAndSwap` and the new `deleteReflogEntry` all take `<ref>.lock` (and `packed-refs.lock` where git does), and a reflog line is appended while the ref's lock is held, as git does. A batch now also locks every symbolic ref that logs the update (not only HEAD), the updated ref first. As in git, a reflog line that cannot be written now fails the update before its commit (the ref keeps its value) instead of being skipped.
  
  **Stash as git keeps it.** `GitStashStore` (the files `WorkingCopy.stash`) writes `refs/stash` through the history's ref store: a push is a one-ref batch under `refs/stash.lock` that creates the reflog (git's REF_FORCE_CREATE_REFLOG) with the stash commit's message, a drop is `git reflog delete --rewrite --updateref refs/stash@{n}` under `refs/stash.lock` and `logs/refs/stash.lock`, and the last drop deletes the ref and its log. Messages follow git ("WIP on main: abc1234 subject", "On main: message", "index on main: …"), commit timestamps are seconds (they were milliseconds), and `list()` returns the reflog message. Native `git stash list` / `show` / `drop` work on our stashes and we read and drop git's. `GitStashStoreOptions` loses `files` and `gitDir` (and `StashFilesApi` is gone): the store needs only `history`. In `vcs-commands`, `stashCreate` logs every stash (a custom message becomes "On <branch>: <message>", a detached HEAD "(no branch)", as in git), `stashList` lists every `stash@{n}` from the reflog, `stashApply` resolves `stash@{n}`, and `stashDrop` drops any `stash@{n}` (stores without reflogs keep the old `stash@{0}`-only behaviour).
  
  **Pseudo refs and HEAD.** ORIG_HEAD, MERGE_HEAD, CHERRY_PICK_HEAD and REVERT_HEAD are written and removed through the ref store (under `<name>.lock` on git files) by the merge, cherry-pick and revert state stores and by `GitCheckout`, which also reads them from its `refs`. `createGitFilesBackend({ create: true })` writes HEAD with `setSymbolic` (under `HEAD.lock`).
  
  **New core API.** `BatchOptions.createReflog` creates the updated refs' reflogs whatever their names. `Refs.deleteReflogEntry(name, index, { rewrite, updateRef })` (optional) deletes `name@{index}`; it is implemented by the memory, KV, SQL and git-files stores. `deleteReflogLine` and `ReflogEntryNotFoundError` are exported from `vcs-core`.
- 3818fee: **`git fetch` and `git remote` as git does them: the opportunistic remote-tracking update, `followRemoteHEAD`, prune configuration and `--prune-tags`, `remote set-head -a`, `remote remove` and the new `remote rename`.**
  
  - **vcs-commands, `FetchCommand`:** a fetch with explicit refspecs (`git fetch origin main`) also updates the remote-tracking refs the remote's `remote.<name>.fetch` maps the fetched refs to (git ≥ 1.8.4); FETCH_HEAD is unchanged. New `setRefmap(...refspecs)` (`--refmap`) maps them instead; an empty refmap turns the update off, and a refmap without explicit refspecs is refused with git's message. A configured fetch now creates `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD` from the remote HEAD as git 2.48+ does, steered by `remote.<name>.followRemoteHEAD` / `fetch.followRemoteHEAD` (`create`, the default; `always`; `warn` and `warn-if-not-<branch>`, whose warning goes into `FetchResult.messages` in git's words; `never`); in a bare mirror the fetch moves HEAD itself. Pruning now follows `remote.<name>.prune` and `fetch.prune` when `setRemoveDeletedRefs` is not called, prunes before fetching (a remote branch `foo` that became `foo/bar` no longer conflicts), counts overlapping refspecs together and deletes in one ref batch (`fetch: prune`); a dry run reports what it would delete. New `setPruneTags(bool)` (`--prune-tags`, `remote.<name>.pruneTags`, `fetch.pruneTags`).
  - **vcs-commands, `RemoteRemoveCommand`:** as git: removes the refs the remote's fetch refspecs map under `refs/remotes/` (not those another remote maps too, `HEAD` included) with their reflogs in one batch, unsets `branch.<b>.remote` / `branch.<b>.merge` of its branches and `branch.<b>.pushRemote` naming it, and `remote.pushDefault` naming it.
  - **vcs-commands, new `git.remoteRename()` (`RemoteRenameCommand`, `git remote rename <old> <new>`):** renames the config section in place, rewrites the fetch refspecs writing under `refs/remotes/<old>/` (keeping the others, with git's warning in the result's `warnings`), updates `branch.<b>.remote` / `pushRemote` and `remote.pushDefault`, and moves `refs/remotes/<old>/*` in one batch with their reflogs and git's `remote: renamed …` line, re-pointing `refs/remotes/<new>/HEAD`. Errors in git's words.
  - **vcs-commands, `RemoteSetHeadCommand`:** new `setAuto(true)` (`git remote set-head <name> -a`) asks the remote for its HEAD branch (errors "Cannot determine remote HEAD", "Multiple remote HEAD branches. …", "Not a valid ref: …" as git); setting HEAD on a remote with `followRemoteHEAD = always` turns it into `warn`, as git 2.48+. It now extends `TransportCommand` (credentials, headers, timeout).
  - **vcs-transport:** `FetchClientOptions.opportunisticRefspecs` (refs the fetch took are also written through these, outside FETCH_HEAD) and `beforeFetch(remoteRefs)` (called after the listing, before any object or ref is written).
  - **vcs-core:** `RefCommand.reflog` (`OwnReflogLine[]`): the reflog lines a command writes in place of its default line (git's `ref_transaction_update_reflog`); implemented by every batch-capable store.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitWorkingCopyConfig.renameSection(old, new)` renames a section in place, as git's `rename_section`.
  - **Follow-ups.** `RemoteRemoveCommand` refuses a remote without a config section with git's "No such remote: '<name>'" and changes nothing (it no longer returns undefined; breaking for callers that relied on the lenient JGit-era behaviour), keeps a ref any of whose sources a negative refspec excludes, and reports git's note on kept local branches in `getMessages()`. `RemoteSetHeadCommand.getMessages()` gives `set-head -a`'s report ("'origin/HEAD' is now created and points to 'main'", …). The fetch's `followRemoteHEAD=warn` messages carry git's hint lines (off with `advice.fetchRemoteHEADWarn=false`), a prune names the symbolic refs it leaves dangling, a refused prune goes into the new `FetchResult.errors` instead of failing the fetch, and a failing `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD` update is ignored, as in git. The one-by-one move for stores without multi-ref batches never overwrites or loses a ref; the rename fallback for configs without `renameSection` copies every `remote.<old>.*` variable. `RefCommand.reflog` lines are checked (object ids, identity shape) and their control characters collapsed.
- 3292f46: **upload-pack honours `include-tag` and negotiates over v0 as git does, both servers hide refs as git's `hideRefs`, upload-pack gets hooks, and the fetch client gzips large requests.**
  
  - **vcs-transport, v0 negotiation (fix):** upload-pack answers haves exactly as git's `get_common_commits`: `ACK <oid> common` / `continue` / `ready` while a batch is read (`ready` also without no-done, once every want reaches a common commit), NAK at the end of every batch in the multi_ack modes, the final `ACK <oid>` on `done`, and with no-done after `ready` NAK and the final ACK before the pack. Before, a batch that found a common commit was answered without NAK, and a later batch with nothing new got no answer: our fetch client failed with "Expected ACK/NAK, got a flush" and native git with "expected ACK/NAK, got '?PACK'" (a stale remote-tracking ref plus many local commits over v0). In single-ack mode the server now waits for `done` after its ACK, as git. `multi_ack` is advertised again (git advertises both `multi_ack` and `multi_ack_detailed`).
  
  - **vcs-core:** `listObjects` takes `includeTags` (candidate tag ids) and `ExportOptions.includeTags` passes it from `SerializationApi.exportPack`: as `git pack-objects --include-tag`, each annotated tag peeling to a packed object is added after the other objects, with every tag of its chain. Only tag objects are read: a listed target, a lightweight tag of a listed object and an object the walk met cost no read.
  - **vcs-transport, include-tag:** upload-pack (v0/v1 and v2) adds the annotated tags of packed objects when the client asks for `include-tag`, so a client no longer needs a second fetch to backfill them. The candidates are the visible `refs/tags/*` (a hidden tag is never sent; git's pack-objects ignores hideRefs). `ExportPackOptions.includeTags` carries them to the facade; `includeTag: true` without it uses the facade's own `refs/tags/*` (both `createRepositoryFacade` and vcs-transport-adapters' `VcsRepositoryFacade`).
  - **vcs-transport, hidden refs:** new `hideRefs` (git's `transfer.hideRefs`) on `serveOverDuplex`, `createHttpHandler`, `createGitHttpServer`, `createFetchHandler` and `messagePortServe`, plus upload-pack's (`uploadPack.hideRefs` / `uploadPackHideRefs`, git's `uploadpack.hideRefs`) and receive-pack's (`receivePack.hideRefs` / `receivePackHideRefs`, git's `receive.hideRefs`), with git's pattern rules (`isRefHidden`: component prefixes, `!` negation, `^` full names, last match wins). Hidden refs are left out of the v0 advertisements and v2 `ls-refs`; a want of a hidden tip is `not our ref` under the "ADVERTISED" policy ("TIP" and the reachable policies count hidden tips, as git's `allow*SHA1InWant`); a v2 `want-ref` of one is `unknown ref <name>`; receive-pack refuses changing one (`deny updating a hidden ref`, `deny deleting a hidden ref`) after the connectivity check, as git. `handleGitSocketConnection` passes the hidden refs and both services' hooks through. **Hiding refs is not access control**, as in git: hidden history stays fetchable by id under the "ANY" (v2's default), "TIP" and reachable policies, and HEAD naming a hidden branch is still advertised.
  - **vcs-transport, upload-pack hooks:** new `UploadPackHooks` (`uploadPack.hooks` on the HTTP handlers, `uploadPackHooks` on `serveOverDuplex`, `hooks` on `messagePortServe`): `authorize(request, ctx)` sees the wants, every ref whose tip they name (hidden ones included), the `want-ref`s, the haves of a v2 request, a copy of the capabilities and the shallow/deepen arguments (`depth`, `deepenSince`, `deepenNot`, `filter`) before any negotiation or pack byte, and may refuse with `ERR <message>`; it runs on every request, so once per round over stateless HTTP. A want need not be a tip (a parent under "ANY" or the reachable policies), so a hook guarding refs must check reachability or keep "ADVERTISED". `postUpload(result, ctx)` runs detached once a pack was sent.
  - **vcs-transport, v2:** `ls-refs peel` fully peels a chain of tags (was one level); an unknown `want-ref` reads git's `unknown ref <name>` (was `Unknown ref: <name>`).
  - **vcs-transport, fetch client:** upload-pack request bodies over 1024 bytes (large have-batches) are sent with `Content-Encoding: gzip`, as git's remote-curl, where `CompressionStream` exists. `gzipRequests: false` (`fetch`, `openHttpConnection`) turns it off, for browsers behind a CORS proxy that does not allow `Content-Encoding` (not a CORS-safelisted header).
  - **vcs-commands:** `TransportCommand.setGzipRequests(false)` passes that to the fetch and clone commands.
  - **vcs-workspace:** `createSyncServer` takes `hideRefs` and `uploadPackHooks`.
- d45dfb0: **Stash apply as git (index, content merge, conflicts); ignore rules read fresh as git; no leftover walk cache.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (breaking defaults):** `stash apply` no longer restores the index by default (git's default; `setRestoreIndex(true)` is `--index`). The stash's change is merged into the tree written from the current index, so staged content and newly staged files survive; both-sides changes to a file are merged line by line (`mergeLines`), a real conflict is written with git's markers (`Updated upstream` / `Stashed changes`) and staged at stages 1-3 (status CONFLICTS). Without `--index` the index keeps its entries plus the files the stash adds (git stages them); with it, the stash's index change is merged into the index, refused on a conflict with git's "error: conflicts in index. Try without --index.". An index with unmerged entries is refused. Stashed untracked files are restored first and refused, nothing written, when a file stands on a leading component (git's "fatal: cannot create directory at '…': File exists" / "error: could not restore untracked files from stash"). Outcomes compared with native `git stash apply`.
  - **vcs-commands:** restore plans refuse a reserved name only where the worktree's storage reserves it (new `capabilities.reservedNames`, at the storage's root), not on plain worktrees.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `IgnoreManager.setIgnoreFile(dir, content | undefined)` and `setExcludeFile(source, content | undefined)` replace a source's rules (`addIgnoreFile` now replaces too); a path inside an ignored directory is ignored and cannot be re-included (git: `build/` then `!build/keep.txt`). `WorktreeCapabilities.reservedNames`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree` rereads `.gitignore`, `info/exclude` and `core.excludesFile` for every walk and ignore check (a changed or removed file is always seen); a walk keeps its lstat-caching view to itself (an abandoned iterator leaves nothing behind).
  - **vcs-utils:** `createSymlinkFilesApi` creates no table on a base without links and rewrites it only when it changed; a stale entry is dropped before `createExclusive`/`write` so a new empty file never reads as a link; `PosixFilesApi.reservedNames` (the table's name at the wrapper's root; passed on by mounts, not by subtrees).
  - **vcs-utils-node:** new `openRepositoryFiles(rootDir, { gitDir })`: the FilesApi to open a repository with, following a `.git` / `.git/objects` linked elsewhere (as git) while every other link stays unfollowed; a FilesApi opened by `openLinkTarget` follows no links itself; `PosixNodeFilesApi.rootDirectory`.
- d45dfb0: **Stash apply: move-aside names made unique, file/directory collisions in conflicts, rename conflicts; `stashCreate` needs a working tree.**
  
  - **Unique move-aside names.** A file moved aside by a file/directory conflict now gets a unique name, as git's `unique_path` makes it: `<path>~<side>`, then `_0`, `_1`, … appended while the name is a path or directory in the base, the index or the stash, or is already taken. A tracked file, directory or link of that name is never overwritten. An untracked file there still refuses the apply, and an ignored one is overwritten, as in git.
  - **File/directory collisions inside conflicts.** A modify/delete conflict whose surviving file collides with a directory now moves aside the same way, and the directory keeps its name. That covers ours modifying `p` while the stash deletes it and adds `p/x`, the stash modifying `p` while the index or HEAD has `p/x`, and a rename onto a directory the stash adds.
  - **Safety net.** `findInTheWay` refuses any write onto a directory that holds tracked files the operation does not remove.
  - **Rename conflicts.** Renames are no longer reported as clean merges when git reports a conflict:
    - rename/delete is staged at 1 and 2 or 3 (DU/UD), with the renaming side's file in the worktree
    - rename/rename to two paths stages the original at 1 (DD) and each side's file at its own path (AU/UA)
  - **`stashCreate` (breaking).** Without a working tree provider it now throws `StoreNotAvailableError` instead of reporting a stash of HEAD's tree while the local changes stay unsaved. Pass one with `setWorkingTreeProvider`.
- d45dfb0: **Stash apply and checkout protect untracked files and local changes as git; no instance-lifetime lstat cache; confined composed FilesApis.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:**
    - `stash apply` touches only the paths the stash changes and refuses before writing anything, with git's merge messages: local changes on such a path ("Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by merge"), untracked files where it writes ("The following untracked working tree files would be overwritten by merge"), untracked files in a directory a stashed file replaces ("Updating the following directories would lose untracked files in them"). It no longer deletes untracked directories nor overwrites unrelated unstaged edits.
    - Branch checkout: untracked files on a leading component of a written path are refused up front (git's `check_leading_path`); a clean directory switches to a file (git's `verify_clean_subdirectory`; untracked files inside are refused with the directories message); directories a switch empties are removed; a local executable-bit change counts as a local change where `core.filemode` holds; a staged deletion of a file the target deletes too is refused ("would be removed by checkout"); ignored files in the way are overwritten (the worktree's `.gitignore` files are read); a case-only rename switches on a case-folding worktree; the stat shortcut compares ctime too.
    - The restore plan refuses a tree entry named `.webrun-symlinks.json` (any case), the simulated-links table.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `PosixNodeFilesApi` caches nothing across calls (an outside change is always seen); `withLstatCache()` returns a view for one batch whose directory cache is dropped with it, and forgotten on ENOENT/ENOTDIR/ELOOP or when an entry is seen not to be a directory (a write retries once after a cached directory vanished). `openLinkTarget` is opt-in (`openLinkTargets`, also on `createNodeFilesApi`). `chmod` works on a file it cannot read (lstat, then chmod by path).
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree.batch(fn)` runs operations in one lstat-cache batch (a walk is one batch too); `isIgnored` reads the `.gitignore` files of the path's directories (never a symlinked one).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `Worktree.batch?()`.
  - **vcs-utils:** `PosixFilesApi.withLstatCache?()`. `createSymlinkFilesApi`: one table per base shared by all its wrappers, reloaded before and serialized across mutations; a link counts only while its entry and an empty placeholder agree (mutations ordered so an interruption never makes a file read as a link); move and copy forget what the target held; `..` refused; the reserved table name compared case- and NFC-insensitively; new `isReservedFilesName`. `createSubtreeFilesApi` / `createMountedFilesApi`: `..` refused, `openLinkTarget` forwarded only with `forwardOpenLinkTarget`, a mount point (or a directory holding one) cannot be removed, and listings include what is mounted below.
- 7d77bf8: **Nested repositories are never deleted; stash push and status closer to git; index refresh.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (data loss fix):** emptied directories are pruned only when their listing is truly empty, one level at a time (git's `rmdir`), never recursively: a directory holding an untracked nested repository (its `.git`), a FIFO or an ignored file stays. This affected `stash push` (`-u`, `-a`, a staged new file or removed tracked files next to a nested repository), `checkout` and `stash apply`. New optional `Worktree.removeEmptyDirectory` (FileWorktree, MemoryWorktree).
  - **vcs-commands (stash push as git):** `setRef(null)` (`git stash create`) leaves the index and worktree alone; a tracked path beyond a symbolic link refuses the stash with git's message ("error: 'd/e.txt' is beyond a symbolic link"), changing nothing; an intent-to-add entry refuses it ("error: Entry 'n.txt' not uptodate. Cannot merge."); a tracked file replaced by a directory and a staged new file deleted from disk keep their index blob in the stash; `-u` always writes the untracked commit (the empty tree when there is nothing), and removes empty untracked directories as `git clean -d`; a submodule keeps the index's commit in the stash, as git.
  - **vcs-commands (status):** a submodule whose checked-out commit differs from the index is ` M sub` (porcelain v2 `SC..`; read through the new optional `Worktree.readGitlinkHead`); its own dirty content is not inspected. Porcelain v1 quotes paths with spaces as git does; v2 lines of intent-to-add entries as git.
  - **vcs-commands / vcs-working-tree (performance):** status and stash write back the stats of entries they proved clean by hashing (git's index refresh, only when the index is unchanged and under `index.lock`), smudging racily clean entries that changed (size 0); stash push compares the index with the worktree once (`scanIndex`) for the check, the worktree tree and the reset, and checks only the blobs it writes (`TreeRestoreOptions.writing`). 3000 files: status after a reset re-hashes once instead of every time; stash push 5.8 s (6005 files hashed) to 1.3 s (none hashed with a fresh index).
  - **vcs-working-tree:** new `scanIndex`, `IndexScan`, `statUpdateOf`, `refreshableEntries`, `refreshIndexStats`, `indexTimeOf`; `createWorktreeTree`/`hasTrackedChanges` take an optional scan; `createUntrackedTree` always returns a tree; `WorktreeComparison.smudge`; a directory in place of a file is reported with its entry.
  - **demos/examples:** the webrtc-p2p-sync commit check looks at staged changes only; the examples' `addFileToStaging` writes the worktree too, as `git add` leaves it.
- 7d77bf8: **Status sees the worktree; `stashCreate()` works without a provider; stash apply follow-ups.**
  
  - **vcs-commands (breaking behaviour):** `git.status()` compares the worktree with the index as `git status` does: modified, deleted and type-changed files (`modified`, `missing`), untracked files (`untracked`, `-uall`), ignored ones with `setIgnored(true)` (`ignored`), unmerged entries with `conflictingStageState`. Files are compared through the index stat; a racily clean entry (modified at or after the index was written) is re-hashed, as git does; links are 120000, the executable bit counts only where `core.filemode` trusts it, `core.symlinks=false` keeps a link checked out as a file. `isClean()` now also requires no unstaged change and no untracked file; `hasUncommittedChanges()` counts unstaged changes. New `status.files` (one entry per porcelain line) and `formatStatusPorcelain(status, 1 | 2)` print `git status --porcelain[=v2] --untracked-files=all` (renames are not detected). `setUntracked(false)` is `-uno`.
  - **vcs-commands:** `git.stashCreate()` on a working copy with an index and a worktree uses a built-in provider (`WorkingCopyStashProvider`): the index tree, the worktree tree and (`setIncludeUntracked(true)`, new `setIncludeIgnored(true)` for `--all`) the untracked tree as `git stash push` builds them, then `git clean -fd` (with `-u`), `git reset --hard` (only differing paths are written; untracked files stay) and, with new `setKeepIndex(true)`, the index tree checked out again. "No local changes to save" (untracked files only count with `-u`) returns undefined; an index with unmerged entries is refused (`<path>: needs merge`). Without a worktree it still throws `StoreNotAvailableError`. `StashWorkingTreeProvider.hasLocalChanges`/`getUntrackedTree`/`resetHard` take optional options (`StashResetOptions`).
  - **vcs-commands (stash apply):** move-aside names (`<path>~<side>`) are compared case-insensitively with `core.ignorecase`; a gitlink moved aside keeps its stage-2 entry, gets an empty directory at the new path and its old directory is removed only if empty (as git); a conflict that neither writes nor removes the worktree file (rename/rename, rename/delete on our side, binary) no longer refuses an unstaged edit there (git keeps it); a file standing where a stashed untracked file goes is refused with git's message (`<path> already exists, no checkout` / `error: could not restore untracked files from stash`), whether it is untracked or staged.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** new `compareWithWorktree`, `diffIndexWithWorktree`, `findUntrackedFiles`, `trackedUnder`, `directoriesOf`, `defaultModeTrust` (the worktree side of status) and `createIndexTree`, `createWorktreeTree`, `createUntrackedTree`, `hasTrackedChanges`, `storeFlatTree`, `assertNoUnmergedEntries` (the stash trees, shared by `GitStashStore` and `stashCreate()`).
  - **vcs-store-files:** `GitStashStore.push` builds its trees with the shared helpers: nested paths, deleted files and untracked files in subdirectories are stored as git stores them (they were flattened or kept); new `trust` option.
- d45dfb0: **The worktree never follows a symbolic link; Node clones keep executables and links; add stages modes as git.**
  
  - **vcs-utils:** new `PosixFilesApi` (a `FilesApi` with optional `chmod`, `symlink(target: Uint8Array, path)`, `lstat` and `readlink`) and `LinkStats` (`kind` `file`/`directory`/`symlink`/`other`, `size`, `lastModified`, `ctime?`, `mode?`); re-exported by vcs-core.
  - **vcs-utils-node:** `createNodeFilesApi` returns a `PosixNodeFilesApi` (new, exported): `NodeFilesApi` with `chmod`, `symlink`, `lstat` and `readlink`, contained to the root and never following a link (at the path or in a leading component; `chmod` refuses a link). Its `remove` unlinks a link (a dangling one too) instead of following it, `exists` sees a dangling link, and a non-recursive `list` lists links, dangling ones included, with their own size. Link targets round-trip as bytes.
  - **vcs-core:** `isValidWorktreePath(path, mode?)` / `assertValidWorktreePath(path, mode?)`: with mode 120000, a symlinked `.gitmodules` (any case, NTFS `gitmod~1`, trailing dots/spaces, HFS-ignorable code points) is refused, as git's `verify_path`.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `WorktreeFilesApi` is now `PosixFilesApi` (its `symlink` takes the target as bytes). `FileWorktree` reports `capabilities.executable` only with `chmod` and `lstat`, and `capabilities.symlinks` only with `symlink`, `lstat` and `readlink`. With `lstat` it never follows a symbolic link: `writeContent`, `mkdir`, `remove` and `rename` refuse a path beneath a symlink or a file (git's `has_symlink_leading_path`); `writeContent` unlinks a link at the path instead of writing through it and refuses to write over a directory; `remove` unlinks a link (a dangling one too) and leaves a directory in place unless `recursive`; `walk`, `getEntry`, `readContent` and `computeHash` report a link as mode 120000 with its target as content (never descending into a linked directory), an executable as 100755, and `ctime`; a symlinked `.gitignore` is not followed. `checkoutTree` / `checkoutPaths` now write symlinks as links (or files holding the target), chmod files, create an empty directory for a gitlink instead of failing it, and refuse paths beneath a symlink.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `WorktreeEntry` gains `ctime?`. New `modeFromWorktree(worktreeMode, indexMode, trust)` and `WorktreeModeTrust`, after git's `ce_mode_from_stat`. The status calculator ignores the executable bit where the worktree cannot show it (`capabilities.executable === false`).
  - **vcs-commands:**
    - `AddCommand` stages the mode git would: a link as 120000 with its target as content (never the file it names), and, where the worktree or `core.filemode` / `core.symlinks` cannot be trusted, the index's 100755 / 120000 kept for a regular file.
    - `CloneCommand` checks out last, after HEAD, `refs/remotes/<name>/HEAD` and the configuration, as git; a failed checkout throws the new `CloneCheckoutError` ("Clone succeeded, but checkout failed: …", with the clone's `result`), the repository usable. Every path of the tree is checked before the first write. The index records the worktree's own stat of each entry (a link's from `lstat`) and its ctime.
    - `planTreeRestore` (clone, `reset --hard`, `stash apply`) refuses paths `verify_path` refuses and colliding entries: the same path twice, or a path beneath a planned file, symlink or submodule, case-folded with `core.ignorecase`.
    - `reset --hard` deletes the tracked paths the target drops before writing, as git's unpack-trees: a tracked file or link `d` makes way for `d/x` (this also fixes a file replaced by a directory failing with EEXIST).
    - `core.*` settings are read from the repository's own configuration only (no global or system configuration).

### Patch Changes

- 7d77bf8: **Stat comparison as git's checkStat; smudged entries; unreadable gitdirs; clean pathspecs.**
  
  - **vcs-working-tree:** status, stash and the racy check compare a file with its index entry as git's `ce_match_stat_basic` (new `basicStatMatches`): besides size and the whole-second mtime and ctime, the inode, owner and group where the worktree reports them (an entry recorded without an inode, as 0, then does not match and is re-read, as in git); `core.checkStat=minimal` compares neither (new `WorktreeModeTrust.fullStat`). An entry git smudged (size 0 while its blob is not the empty blob) never matches. The racy smudging on index writes judges seconds as status does, and returns early (no hashing) when the basic stat already differs.
  - **vcs-store-files:** a submodule `gitdir:` that is absolute or climbs beyond the FilesApi's root no longer fails status: the submodule's commit is unknown and no change is reported; only a gitdir inside the repository with no `HEAD` is git's fatal error. `isRepository` checks a gitfile's target: one pointing to no repository does not make its directory a nested repository (git clean -d removes it); an absolute target is taken as a repository (never removed on a guess).
  - **vcs-commands:** `clean()` with a path filter removes only the directories the filter names (`-- '*.txt'` leaves an emptied `d/sub`), and a glob naming a directory's contents (`u/*`) removes and reports `u/`, as git.
  
  **Repositories with a linked HEAD.** A nested repository or gitfile target whose HEAD is a symbolic link (git writes one under `core.preferSymlinkRefs`) counts as a repository, as git's `validate_headref` accepts it; `objects` and `refs` may be links to directories. `clean -d` keeps such repositories instead of removing them. Owner and group are compared only where the index entry recorded them, so stores that do not keep them never re-hash every file.
- d45dfb0: **Stash apply leaves conflicts as git does; ignore rules read once per operation, for every parent of a walk's prefix.**
  
  - **vcs-commands:** `stash apply` conflicts now match native git. A conflicted path is never deleted. In a modify/delete conflict the surviving side stays in the worktree, staged at 1 and 2 or 3 (UD/DU); when the stash's side survives it is written, behind the untracked-file check. In a file/directory collision the directory keeps the name and the file moves aside to `<path>~Updated upstream` (ours) or `<path>~Stashed changes` (the stash's), as git names it. Binary files (a NUL in the first 8000 bytes of any side) are not merged: ours stays without markers, all three versions staged. Renames on either side are detected, exact matches first and then by similarity of at least 50%, so a change made to the old path merges into the new one. Conflicts that only the stash side touches are staged too. With `--index`, an entry records the file's size and mtime only when the worktree holds the staged blob; otherwise it records 0, so the unstaged edit is seen. Stash apply and checkout each run as one worktree batch.
  - **vcs-store-files:** `FileWorktree` reads the ignore rules once per operation (per `batch`) and for each walk and ignore check outside one. That includes the `.gitignore` of every parent directory of a walk's `pathPrefix`, so a rule changed or removed since an earlier walk is seen.
  - **vcs-working-tree:** `info/exclude` takes precedence over `core.excludesFile` whichever was read first, as in git.
- 7d77bf8: **Index stat data as native git keeps and compares it.**
  
  - **Exact stat on every index write.** `add`, checkout, `stash apply` and clone record a file's full stat (ctime, exact nanoseconds, device, inode, owner) through the new `entryStatOf`; `UpdateStagingEntry` accepts it. Before, `add` kept only the size and a millisecond time.
  - **`core.trustctime` and `core.checkStat=minimal`.** Status and the racy check no longer compare ctime when either says so (`WorktreeModeTrust.ctime`).
  - **Native git tests** pin git's behaviour (checked on 2.43 and 2.56): exact nanoseconds recorded, a nanosecond-only change ignored, a whole-second change re-checked, a same-size edit caught when the index was written in the file's second and missed when written a second later, and ctime compared only under `core.trustctime`.
